# Does deacon behave like the DevContainers CLI?

Compared against **`@devcontainers/cli` 0.87.0** and the [containers.dev spec]
(https://github.com/devcontainers/spec) at commit `113500f4`.

This is the durable record of what we know about deacon's behavior relative to the
reference. It is **hand-maintained**: when a scenario in `parity/cases/` teaches us
something new, the row changes here in the same commit.

## How to read a row

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Conformant and matching** | The spec defines it, deacon follows it, and the reference agrees. Nothing owed. |
| **deacon follows the spec where the CLI does not** | An observable difference in which deacon is the conformant side. The reference's deviation, not work we owe. |
| **Documented choice** | The spec is silent and the two tools differ deliberately. Each is allowlisted with its reasoning in `parity/ALLOWLIST.json`. |
| **deacon extension** | Capability the reference has no equivalent for. Nothing to compare against. |
| **Open nonconformance** | deacon is the wrong side and we know it. Always carries an issue link. |

A status is a claim backed by a scenario or an allowlist entry — never by argument
alone. Where a row has no scenario yet, it says so.

## Summary

Of **232 recorded behaviors**:

- **0** — open nonconformance
- **10** — deacon follows the spec where the CLI does not
- **15** — documented choice
- **25** — deacon extension
- **182** — conformant and matching

**Batch 10 mined `src/test/cli.build.test.ts` — twenty-nine `it`s, the densest remaining e2e file — and found one defect that no result document could ever have shown.** [#641](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/641): the image `deacon build` produces did not carry the configuration's own `containerEnv`, and where a Feature declared the same variable the FEATURE's value survived into the image. Both CLIs exited 0, both printed `{"outcome":"success"}`, and both wrote an identical `devcontainer.metadata` label — the configuration was resolved and merged correctly the whole time; it simply never reached the artifact. `up` was never affected, because it applies `containerEnv` as `--env` at create time and the two CLIs already agreed inside a running container. What was wrong was the thing `build` exists to hand over: an image that goes to CI, to a registry, or to any tool that has never heard of deacon, carrying only what is baked into it.

**The batch's own lesson is that the sharp end of a defect is rarely the half you notice first.** The obvious symptom was a missing variable. The one worth failing a build over was the variable that was PRESENT with the wrong value — an image contradicting the document that built it — and it only became visible because the fixture was written with a name declared on both sides before anything was measured. A case asserting the absence alone would have gone green on a fix that emitted the configuration's `ENV` lines in the wrong place, which is precisely where the reference's own shape turns out to be load-bearing: its two escaping rules and its ordering are the merge rule, not an implementation detail of it. Reverting the fix leaves exactly one diverging case out of eighty-four, and its first mismatch is the overridden entry rather than the missing one.

**The batch's second finding runs the other way, and needed a rule stated rather than a fix.** `build --label` reaches the produced image on every configuration shape from deacon and only on image-reference ones from the reference — measured across all four shapes, and narrowed to the flag itself by the fact that the same Dockerfile shape still carries `devcontainer.metadata` and still forwards `build.options`-supplied labels. The standing rule on a spec-silent output surface is that the reference is the authority, and this is the case where saying so out loud matters: there is no answer to match, only an inconsistency inside the reference to pick a side of. deacon keeps its behavior, the difference is characterized with a self-invalidating tolerance, and the ruling is referred up. It also exposed a smaller gap on the way — nothing in the suite had ever passed `--label` twice, which is precisely what upstream's own test asserts.

The other two cases are claims deacon already met and nothing pinned: `--config` naming a document in a subfolder, which is really an assertion about resolving `build.dockerfile` against the config's directory rather than the workspace's, and `build` in a directory with no configuration and no `--workspace-folder`. The second closes a family batch 6 opened for `exec` and batch 9 continued for `run-user-commands`: every consumer subcommand with a cwd default now has a case on BOTH arms.

**Batch 9 mined the densest unmined file in the reference's e2e suite — `src/test/cli.test.ts`, eleven `it`s — and found three defects in it, every one of them a case of deacon being the LAST subcommand out of step in a family it had already closed twice.** [#635](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/635): `run-user-commands` wrote nothing at all to stdout, on success and on failure alike, where `up` has printed a result document since 001, `set-up` prints one, and `build` was brought into line by [#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594). [#637](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/637), found by taking that document seriously rather than as boilerplate: its `result` field carries one of four words and the fourth, `stopForPersonalization`, would have been a LIE — `--stop-for-personalization` was parsed and dropped, so `postStart` and `postAttach` ran anyway, at exit 0, with real side effects in the container. It was fixed in the same commit that started reporting it, and both now come from one decision so they cannot drift apart. [#636](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/636): `read-configuration` left `${containerEnv:*}` literal even with a container up, the third instance of a gap [#608](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/608) closed for `up` and [#616](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/616) for `set-up` — and the shared helper's own module doc had already predicted it in as many words: *every consumer subcommand that prints a configuration alongside a live container owes this pass, and each one of them got it late.*

**The rule this batch earns: when a fix closes a family, check the family for stragglers instead of waiting for one to be reported.** All three defects were sitting in subcommands whose siblings had been corrected, and each was findable by asking "who else does this?" rather than by reading upstream at all. The batch also completed the coverage the file implies — `run-user-commands` had no cwd-default case while its four sibling subcommands all did, and `fx-upstream-subfolder-config` turned out to be a second fixture vendored from an execution test and only ever parsed, which is batch 7's lesson recurring. Eleven cases in total, and one normalizer change: the first case to run `read-configuration` after an `up` exposed that `workspace_basename_token` was scoped to one channel when the artifact it corrects reaches two.

**Batch 8 ran the fixtures nobody had ever run, and that is the whole batch.** Batch 7
ended by measuring that **twelve** of the vendored `fx-upstream-*` fixtures were referenced
by `read-configuration` operations and by nothing else, and proposed one question as the
next batch: *which upstream test was this fixture taken from, and does that test RUN it or
merely PARSE it?* Six of the twelve turned out to come from execution tests, and five of
those six could be run here. They produced **five cases and four defects** —
[#617](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/617) (an anonymous volume mount —
`type: volume` with no `source`, which is how Docker's own `--mount` asks for one — was
REJECTED at `up`, where the reference creates the volume; **now fixed**, and its row below
reads `matches the reference`),
[#618](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/618) (`updateRemoteUserUID` remaps
`remoteUser` onto a UID another user already owns, so the container silently runs as a
DIFFERENT user than the configuration named, and even `exec --user` cannot get back),
[#619](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/619) (`build` on a Compose config with
no Features reports `outcome: success` and an `imageName`, creates none of the tags, and
drops every `--image-name` but the last) and
[#620](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/620) (a `build` cache hit ignores a
changed `--image-name` — the old name is reported and the new tag is never created).

Three of the four are in code paths every user reaches; #618 was the sharpest, because it
was wrong in a way the result document actively denied — deacon reported
`"remoteUser": "bar"` for a container whose processes ran as `foo`. (Closed 2026-08-22; see
its row in the `up` section, which also records the fixture hardening the batch's own
measurement turned out to need.) The reference's own
`scripts/updateUID.Dockerfile` has carried the guard against exactly this since before the
pin (`elif [ "$OLD_UID" != "$NEW_UID" -a -n "$EXISTING_USER" ]`), and the fixture that
exposes it — a Dockerfile creating `foo` at uid 1000 and then `bar` at 1001, with
`"remoteUser": "bar"` — has been sitting in this repository since batch 1, parsed and never
built.

**Two of the four defects carry a red-on-purpose case and two deliberately do not.** #617
and #618 are `live-differential` cases in the nightly lane, which gates nothing, and each
asserts the FIXED behaviour so it turns green on the fix rather than on an assertion edit —
which is exactly what #617's did, with no assertion touched.
#619 and #620 have no scenario, each for a reason written into its own row: #619 is
inexpressible on BOTH oracle types — the reference's `build` has no `--output-format` flag,
so `chan-structured-output` cannot be compared on that subcommand at all, and reaching
`chan-image` instead converts the defect into a fail-loud `HarnessError` that aborts its
whole resource group (measured, both times); #620 needs a vocabulary for running one
subcommand twice with different arguments, which the operation model does not have. Both are
the [#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594) precedent, and both assertions
land with their fixes. **Both are now fixed and their assertions landed exactly that
way** — #619's as the Docker-gated `build_compose_without_features_tags_every_image_name`
and #620's as `test_build_cache_hit_applies_a_changed_image_name`, each verified to fail
on the pre-fix behaviour, with the harness vocabulary for running one subcommand twice
left as batch-9 material rather than built to serve one case.

**#619's aftermath closed one gap and opened another, which is the arc working as intended.**
That row recorded a companion observation it explicitly did not claim — deacon's Compose
image carried no `devcontainer.metadata` when the config declared no Features, where the
reference's does — and it is now fixed
([#628](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/628)), by mirroring the reference's
actual mechanism rather than the one the issue proposed: Compose keeps driving the build and
deacon layers an inline override naming only what it adds. Measuring the proposed shape
first is what changed the plan, and it found the batch's fifth defect on the way —
[#629](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/629), a Feature-extended Compose build
silently discarding the service's own `build.args`, which reuse of that path would have
extended to the shape it does not yet reach. It was filed rather than folded in, and then
fixed on its own terms — by putting that path on the SAME override mechanism, which also
turned out to restore a `build.target` nobody had noticed was being ignored. Two fixes, one
shape, and the second was cheap because the first had already built it.

**The hit rate is the finding.** Batch 7's twelve-fixture list was a guess at where to look
next; measured, it returned a defect for every one and a quarter fixtures executed, against
a suite-wide rate closer to one defect per file mined. A fixture vendored from an execution
test and covered by a parse case is not weak coverage — it is coverage that reads as
present in every ledger this project keeps while asserting nothing about the behaviour the
fixture was built to demonstrate. Six of the twelve remain, and the six that remain are the
ones whose upstream tests genuinely only parse.

**The sixth execution fixture was measured and is NOT expressible, which is a cap on the
harness rather than a gap in deacon.** `fx-upstream-feature-digest-and-v1-ids` (upstream
`src/test/container-features/e2e.test.ts`, `dockerfile-with-v2-oci-features`) layers
`docker-in-docker` pinned by MANIFEST DIGEST alongside two bare v1 ids. Both CLIs bring it
up successfully — measured, `outcome: success` on each side — but the Feature layer alone
takes about six minutes on deacon and roughly twenty on the reference with every image
already cached, against the harness's `CASE_TIMEOUT` of five minutes per case. A case would
fail on the bound rather than on behaviour. The claim it would carry — that a digest-pinned
Feature reference installs — is worth having and needs either a per-case budget or a
lighter digest-pinned Feature; recorded on [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480)
as batch-9 material rather than capped silently.

**Batch 8's parse-only six, named so nobody re-opens the question.** Five of them —
`fx-upstream-dependson-installsafter-local`, `fx-upstream-dependson-local-with-options`,
`fx-upstream-installsafter-circular`, `fx-upstream-override-order-intermediate`,
`fx-upstream-override-order-round-priority` — were vendored from
`src/test/container-features/containerFeaturesOrder.test.ts`, which never invokes the CLI
at all: it imports `computeDependsOnInstallationOrder` and asserts on the array it returns.
There is no `up` or `build` behind them to reproduce, and deacon's equivalent claims are
already carried by `case-up-feature-multi-dependency-order{,-differential}` and the
`read-configuration` cases over these same fixtures. The sixth,
`fx-upstream-local-feature-missing-folder`, is referenced by NO upstream test — it sits in
`src/test/container-features/configs/invalid-configs/` unused — and its rejection is
already asserted, at the layer where it happens, by
`case-readconfig-upstream-local-feature-outside-devcontainer` (#488).

**Batch 7 took `src/test/cli.up.test.ts` and found three defects, all of them behind
fixtures the suite already owned.** SIX of its seven cases run over FOUR configs batch 1
vendored from `up` tests that nothing had ever RUN — `fx-upstream-runargs-env-subst`,
`fx-upstream-containerenv-escaping`, `fx-upstream-containerenv-dollar-escape` and
`fx-upstream-subfolder-config`, each read by a `read-configuration` case and never brought
up. That is the batch-6 lesson recurring, and MEASURED this time rather than noticed: at the
parent commit **16 of the vendored `fx-upstream-*` fixtures were referenced by
`read-configuration` operations and by nothing else.** This batch moves four of them; twelve
remain. Some of those twelve are legitimately parse-only — vendored from the reference's own
`read-configuration` tests, where parsing IS the claim — but at least one is not
(`fx-upstream-feature-no-version-tag`, from this very file). The rule this earns:
**vendoring a fixture from an execution test and covering it with a parse case records the
fixture, not the claim.**

The three defects, each with measured evidence in its issue:
[#608](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/608) — `up --include-configuration`
serializes the configuration as substituted BEFORE the container existed, so every
`${containerEnv:*}` reaches the caller as a template while the reference resolves it (the
environment deacon actually builds is correct, so the defect is output-only);
[#609](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/609) — a backslash in a Compose
`containerEnv` value arrives as a BACKSPACE, because `escape_yaml_value` emits a
double-quoted YAML scalar down BOTH branches and escapes on only one of them (**CLOSED**:
the predicate is gone, every value is escaped because every value is quoted, and its
red-on-purpose case went green without one byte of the case changing);
[#610](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/610) — `up` demands
`--workspace-folder` where the reference defaults to the current directory, which is also an
internal inconsistency, since deacon's own `exec` and `build` already default to it.

**Two of the three carry a red-on-purpose case and one deliberately did not.** #609 and
#610 are recorded by `live-differential` cases in the nightly lane, which gates nothing, and
each asserts the FIXED behaviour so it turns green on the fix rather than on an assertion
edit. **#609's has now done precisely that** — the defect is closed and
`case-up-upstream-containerenv-compose-backslash` went from red to green with its assertions
untouched, which is the whole argument for writing these cases forwards. #608 was filed with
no scenario, on the [#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594) precedent: a
`spec-expectation` case would have been red in `parity_docker`, a blocking PR lane, and a
`live-differential` cannot express it — the two CLIs' `up` result documents disagree
structurally by construction, so the comparison would report that disagreement forever and
say nothing about substitution. **#608 is now fixed and its assertion has landed**, in the
green case that already exercised the flag (`case-up-upstream-include-configuration`), which
is what that deferral was for.

**One observation was referred up rather than filed, tolerated or asserted — and has since
been ruled.** The same Compose fixture shows `containerEnv` values whose authored REAL
newlines deacon delivers as real newlines and the reference converts to the two-character
sequence `\n`. The maintainer ruled on 2026-08-22 that deacon KEEPS its behavior, and the
follow-up measurement the ruling asked for turned the argument from "arguably more faithful"
into a demonstrated inconsistency inside the reference itself: it preserves the same authored
newlines on the IMAGE path and flattens them only on the Compose one. It is now a documented
choice with a row of its own under `up`, backed by a one-path tolerance on a differential
case that re-measures the divergence every nightly run — so the record fails as stale, rather
than silently outliving the difference, if upstream ever fixes it.

**Batch 6 of the mining pass added five rows and found no defect — which is the result, not
the absence of one.** The batch ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480))
returned to `src/test/cli.exec.base.ts`, the file batch 4 opened and left 31-of-35 accounted
for, and took what its own inventory had set aside: the one negative `exec` claim it named as
the cheapest remaining item, and the lifecycle-marker block it had ruled "not `exec` claims"
and filed against the `up` row without mining. Six cases landed, all green, all measured at
oracle 0.87.0 before being written — `up --skip-post-attach`, `run-user-commands
--skip-post-attach`, `up --skip-non-blocking-commands` under two different `waitFor` values,
the object-form `initializeCommand` actually EXECUTING, and bare `exec` in a directory with no
config. Every one of them exercised a flag or a path that no scenario in the suite had ever
passed: `--skip-post-attach` existed only in the prose of three `notes` fields, and
`initializeCommand` had been parsed by a case since batch 1 and never once run.

**The one thing batch 6 could not express, it now can.** The batch reported that upstream's
restart-timing arms — a completed `postCreate` must not re-run when a stopped container comes
back up — needed a container stop/start primitive the operation vocabulary did not have, and
referred the question up rather than growing machinery on its own authority. The maintainer
ruled for the primitive. `Operation::stopContainerBefore` is that ruling: one flag, one
`docker stop` scoped to the side's own workspace label, no new operation kind and no change to
`CONSUMER_SUBCOMMANDS`. It carries three further cases and the suite's first assertion about
what a restart does and does not re-run. The rest of that report stands unchanged — the three
PTY arms remain unexpressible.

**The batch's own lesson is about a near-miss.** `--skip-non-blocking-commands` was almost
filed as a defect from a code read: the two CLIs' help texts describe DIFFERENT semantics —
the reference stops after the phase `waitFor` names, deacon's `--help` says it "skips
postStart & postAttach phases" — and those coincide only when `waitFor` is
`postCreateCommand`. Measured, deacon reads `waitFor` correctly on both the default and an
explicit value, and only its help string is wrong. That is a documentation gap, deliberately
not filed as a divergence, and it is why the behavior got TWO scenarios rather than one: the
default-`waitFor` case cannot distinguish "reads the field" from "hardcodes the default", so
a second fixture moves `waitFor` one phase earlier and makes the two readings disagree. It is
also the configuration in which a later "fix" aligning behaviour to deacon's own help text
would be a silent regression.

**All four of batch 5's open nonconformances are CLOSED.**
[#592](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/592) and
[#593](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/593) were fixed together because they turned
out to share a diagnosis as well as a surface: both are cases of a check or a build that ran
without carrying something the caller had already said. #592's BuildKit gate existed and asked
whether the host HAS BuildKit rather than whether this build will USE it, so `--buildkit never`
sailed past it; #593's second build — the Feature-layering pass, or the `devcontainer.metadata`
stamp — inherited neither the platform its base was built for nor the reason it had to. Both
red-on-purpose cases turned green on the fix rather than on an assertion edit, which is what the
campaign asks of them, and each gained a `spec-expectation` sibling pinning the half a
differential cannot express. Fixing #593 also CORRECTED ITS OWN SCOPE: the discriminator is not
the configuration shape the issue named but whether a second build runs at all, which a
Dockerfile configuration carrying `containerEnv` reaches just as readily.
[#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594) is CLOSED too — the smallest of the
four, and the one whose fix was mostly deleting a duplication: `build` already had the error
document and used it for the failures it caught before doing any work.
[#595](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/595) is CLOSED too, and it is the one that cost a design rather than a patch. Its fix removes the chained build entirely: base and Features are now one BuildKit invocation over one Dockerfile, and the `devcontainer.metadata` label is computed from the base image BEFORE that build instead of stamped by a third one afterwards. With nothing handed between passes through a daemon-local tag, the `--builder default` pin that made the chain work — and that silently overrode whatever builder the user had selected — is gone from every site. **All four of batch 5's open nonconformances are closed.**

The fix for #595 also RETIRED the fix for #593 rather than building on it. #593 was closed by threading `--platform` into the second and third builds so their `FROM` could resolve the base it named; there is no second or third build any more, so nothing needs threading and the class of failure is unreachable rather than handled. Both of #593's scenarios stay green, and the one that pins `devcontainer.metadata` on an image-reference configuration with a foreign `--platform` is now proving a stronger claim than it was written for: the label it reads is written by the same single build that produces the image.

**Batch 5 of the mining pass reopened the open-nonconformance column with four rows, and
corrected three existing ones that were built on a false premise.** The batch
([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480)) took `src/test/cli.build.test.ts`
at v0.87.0 — the densest file in the vein batch 4 opened, where upstream asserts CLI
behaviour through flags rather than through a fixture config. Six new rows match; the four
that do not are [#592](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/592) (`--buildkit never`
with `--platform` silently builds for the HOST platform and exits 0),
[#593](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/593) (`--platform` fails on an
image-reference configuration, because the intermediate `FROM` tag exists only for the host
platform), [#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594) (`--output-format json`
prints no result document on failure, though `up` and the reference both do) and
[#595](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/595) (`--builder default` is pinned, so a
selected buildx builder is ignored and OCI export is unreachable). #593 and #595 share a
root — deacon builds the base as a local intermediate tag and layers Features with a second
build that `FROM`s it, which is why a foreign platform cannot be resolved and why the docker
driver has to be forced — so they are worth deciding together rather than patching apart.

**The three corrected rows are the batch's own lesson.** `--cache-to`, `--platform` and
`--output` were each recorded as `deacon extension` on the stated ground that the reference
CLI has no such `build` flag. It has all three, and `devcontainer build --help` says so in
one line — the rows were written from a reading of the spec rather than from the reference's
own help output, and each one closed off a comparison that was never run. The comparison, once
run, is what found #595. A row that says "nothing to compare against" is the one kind of row
that can never go red, so it deserves the same scepticism as an assertion that cannot fail.

**One case is red on purpose and the next nightly is expected red with it.**
`case-build-upstream-buildkit-never-platform-refused` asserts the FIXED behaviour and is
`live-differential`, so it runs in the nightly lane, which gates nothing. #593's case is red
in the same way, with a caveat recorded in its own row: it is arch-relative, and it agrees on
an aarch64 machine because the platform it names is native there. #594 has NO scenario yet,
also deliberately — a `spec-expectation` case would be red in a gating lane and a
`live-differential` one cannot express it, since the two CLIs word every one of those
messages differently.

**Batch 3 of the mining pass reopened the open-nonconformance column with two rows, both
found by measuring something the previous batch had only reasoned about.** The batch
([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480)) took three items:
`lockfile-oci-integrity`, which batch 2 had judged expressible and left unmeasured, and the
three compose-project-naming configs, which batch 2 had classified as blocked on a
maintainer ruling. Both classifications turned out to be wrong in the same direction — the
work was ordinary and the answers were not what the paperwork said. **Both have since
closed.**

[#571](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/571), CLOSED: **deacon never read the
lockfile's `integrity`.** A lockfile whose checksum did not match the Feature it named
installed anyway, the build succeeded, and deacon then rewrote the field to the digest it
had just fetched — a tampered lockfile was not ignored, it was silently repaired. The fix
is the reference's own shape rather than the obvious one: `integrity` is the manifest
LOOKUP KEY, so a Feature whose content no longer matches cannot be resolved at all, which
is strictly stronger than comparing a checksum after downloading. Closing it added two
rows the fix made observable — that `--no-lockfile` suppresses the READ and not only the
write, and that an unparseable lockfile now fails the build rather than being quietly
replaced.

[#572](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/572), CLOSED: **a compose file's
top-level `name:` was not interpolated.** `name: ${CUSTOM_NAME}` was passed to Compose
verbatim, which rejects it as an illegal project name, so `up` failed outright where the
reference succeeds. This one was predicted from a code read and could easily have been
filed on that basis; it was measured first, which is what turned "the parser looks
line-wise" into a reproduction with an exit code. Its two siblings in the same group
MATCHED, and the pair of green rows is what said the defect was interpolation alone and
not deacon's project-name policy. The fix is the reference's own shape — an authored name
is resolved by reading `name` off `docker compose config`, where the reference's `Rp`
resolver has always taken it from — and it settled the design question the issue left open
by MEASURING it: `docker compose config` is a client-side call that succeeds with an
unreachable daemon, and the reference's compose `up` already fails outright when that call
fails, so "daemon-dependent" was the wrong frame and "no fallback" is the reference's own
answer.

Both were recorded as red-on-purpose cases asserting the FIXED behaviour, so each turns
green on its fix rather than on a case edit — which is how #571's turned.

[#580](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/580), CLOSED: **`COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME`
was read from one of the three places Compose reads it from.** This one is #572's aftermath
rather than a new area, and it is here because of how it was found: closing #572 produced
three side-observations, and re-measuring them against the MERGED result refuted two — they
described the code the work had branched from, not the code it shipped. The one that
survived was real and was filed with a measured matrix. Fixing it then found a fourth thing
the issue did not have, again by measuring rather than reading: the two missing sources sit
on either side of the one deacon had, so this was a PRECEDENCE defect as well as a
missing-source one, and the obvious fix — hand the question to `docker compose config`, as
the authored-`name:` branch does — answers correctly in the scenario the issue described and
incorrectly the moment both `.env` files exist.

**Batch 2 of the mining pass reopened the same column with three rows, all
about lockfiles, all measured against the pinned oracle. All three have since closed.** The batch
([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), the lockfile family) triaged
twelve upstream configs, landed five scenarios and deferred seven; the two defects it found
are [#556](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/556) — `build` parsed
`--no-lockfile` and `--frozen-lockfile` and consulted neither, so both flags were inert
while `up` implemented them, **CLOSED 2026-08-11** — and
[#557](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/557) —
`up --frozen-lockfile` compared the lockfile byte-for-byte, so it rejected every lockfile the
reference CLI writes even though the two documents said exactly the same thing, **CLOSED
2026-08-11** and its case green. Each red case asserted the FIXED behaviour, so all three
turned green on their fix rather than on a case edit, and no assertion moved. #556's fix is
the one the two green control rows predicted: routing `build` through the SAME decision `up`
makes (`commands/shared/lockfile`) rather than giving it a second implementation, which is
what "a missing wire, not a missing capability" is worth in code.

**#557's second half — which key ORDER deacon EMITS — was ruled and landed with it.** The
spec prescribes the three properties and says nothing normative about their order, so this
was a spec-silent output surface of exactly the class
[#486](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/486) and
[#490](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/490) were: filed for a maintainer ruling
rather than asserted. Ruled MATCH on 2026-08-11 — deacon writes `version, resolved,
integrity`, the reference's order and the order every example in `devcontainer-lockfile.md`
uses. It shipped in the SAME change as the tolerant comparison on purpose: the order change
rewrites every existing deacon-written lockfile once, and a byte comparison still in place
would have failed `--frozen-lockfile` across that churn. No tolerance was authored, because
after the change there is nothing to tolerate — the files deacon writes are byte-identical
to the reference's.

**The batch-1 mining pass is fully dispositioned.** That pass ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480)) ran 22 scenarios harvested from the reference CLI's own e2e
fixtures at `v0.87.0`. Fourteen agreed on arrival and are rows below. Eight cases were red on
seven distinct deacon-side differences, every one measured against the pinned oracle and
filed. ALL FIVE clear-cut defects closed on 2026-08-05 —
[#487](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/487) and [#488](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/488) (Feature-level legacy VS Code properties; local Features
outside `.devcontainer/`), [#489](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/489) (local-Feature identity per option set), then
[#491](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/491) (bare v1 Feature ids) and [#492](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/492) (`build.options` never reaching the
builder).

The remaining two sat on SPEC-SILENT output surfaces where matching the reference might or
might not be worth it, so they were filed for a ruling rather than as defects and carried no
row until one existed — asserting a status before the ruling is the failure this document
exists to avoid. **The maintainer ruled FIX on both on 2026-08-05**, and each is now an
ordinary "matches the reference" row in the `read-configuration` section:

- [#486](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/486) — the pre-`customizations` VS Code properties (`settings`, `extensions`,
  `devPort`) on the CONFIGURATION were echoed where authored; the reference migrates them
  into `customizations.vscode`. The ruling turned on the standing rule that a spec-silent
  output surface makes the reference the authority, PLUS internal consistency: the same
  properties on a FEATURE were already migrated (#487).
- [#490](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/490) — `featuresConfiguration…sourceInformation.userFeatureId` was canonicalized
  (`…/git` reported as `…/git:latest`); the reference preserves the authored form. The ruling
  followed deacon's own precedents #407 and #491 — the reference reports the id the
  configuration wrote — rather than #490's own (absent) collision harm.

Both fixes reuse the mechanism their precedent introduced rather than adding a second: one
legacy-property migration shared by the configuration and Feature readers, one
canonical → authored carry shared with #491 and the lockfile. Every case mined by the batch
is now green with no tolerance, each verified live against the pinned oracle on its own
fixture, and none of the seven left an open-nonconformance row behind.

The open-nonconformance column emptied on 2026-08-03 when duplicate Features
([#430](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/430)) left it, refilled the same day
with the two compose-path gaps that closing #448 surfaced
([#462](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/462) uid remapping,
[#460](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/460) lifecycle phase coverage), and both
left it on 2026-08-04. Fixing #460 then surfaced
[#467](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/467), which held the column at one until
it too was measured and closed on 2026-08-04. Each was found and filed by the suite's own
cases before any user hit it, and each fix was verified the way it was found — live against
the pinned oracle, on the issue's own repro.

That chain is the system working rather than a treadmill: each measurement was
finer-grained than the one before it. #467 is a defect neither of its predecessors could
see, because a path that ran no lifecycle phase at all could not reveal how two SOURCES of
the same phase combine — and it was invisible to every image-metadata scenario that
existed, all of which declared the image's hook in a phase the configuration left alone.
An empty column is a statement about what has been MEASURED, not a claim of correctness,
and it has a short shelf life: the honest claim is that every difference we have measured is
either fixed, deacon-conformant, allowlisted with a ruling, or in this column with an issue
link.

It had a shelf life of hours. Closing #467 prompted an audit for other callers of the merge
it fixed, and [#477](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/477) is what that audit
measured: the same collapse on the CONTAINER's stamped label, which `set-up` reads and #467's
fix did not reach. It refilled the column the day #467 emptied it, and left again the same
day when the fix was measured against the oracle on all three of its shapes. That is the
fourth link in the same chain and it was found the same way as the other three — by
measuring a surface against the pinned oracle rather than by reasoning about the code.

So the column reads zero again, with the same caveat it carried the last time it did: an
empty column is a statement about what has been MEASURED, and its shelf life is however
long it takes someone to look somewhere new. #477 is the standing evidence for that — it
existed the whole time #467 was open and became visible only when someone audited the other
callers of the merge #467 fixed. The audit that closed it went one step further and found
`exec`, the only other caller, clean for a reason rather than by luck: it reads the
last-wins scalars off that helper and runs no lifecycle phase at all. The next link, if
there is one, will come from measuring a surface nobody has measured yet.

The fifth link came from the same measurement that closed the fourth. Closing #477 read
`set-up --include-merged-configuration` on its way past and found the BLOCK wrong even
though the hooks it reported were now right:
[#483](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/483) refilled the column on 2026-08-04
and left it on 2026-08-05. It is the clearest case yet for measuring a surface rather than
reasoning about it — the collection logic was correct and shared, and only the reporting
path around it was not, which no test of what set-up RAN could see. The fix routes that
block through the pipeline `read-configuration` already used, and the row below is now
backed by the first parity case ever to invoke `set-up`.

Two rows moved from "deacon follows the spec where the CLI does not" to "conformant and
matching" at [#439](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/439) — not because deacon
changed status by argument, but because the classification was re-adjudicated against the
pinned spec and then the gap it was hiding was closed. See the
`--include-features-configuration` row.

One row moved out of "open nonconformance" at
[#440](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/440): `build --push` pushing deacon's
internal tag ([#438](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/438)). It was not fixed
for its own sake — stamping `devcontainer.metadata` on a pushed image requires deacon to
own the push, and owning it means choosing what to push. See the `build --push pushes the
image names the user supplied` row.

## Coverage this document does *not* claim

Combinatorial coverage of the scenario space is **no longer tracked**. The previous
model counted obligations across a six-dimension scenario grid and dispositioned each
one; it measured a hole rather than closing it, and cost more to maintain than the
measurement was worth. What remains is the scenario suite itself: if a behavior matters,
it gets a scenario.

Four areas of coverage were **consciously dropped** when the parity corpora were
retired, rather than ported:

- **Cross-CLI container handoff** — running the reference's `up` then deacon's `up` over
  one workspace and asserting the second provisions its own container.
- **Rendered compose-project state** — *partially recovered.* The primary service's
  resolved volumes, environment, mount graph and project name are now compared by
  `case-state-compose-feature-mounts` and `case-state-compose-sidecar-volume`. What stays
  dropped is the rendered project *document* — the merged Compose file each CLI hands to
  `docker compose` — which the two deliver differently (deacon on stdin, the reference via
  a temp file) and which no channel observes.
- **Runtime-versus-merged cross-check** — relating one CLI's
  `read-configuration --include-merged-configuration` to the container it actually made.
- **Intra-deacon comparison** — diffing two deacon runs (single-container versus compose)
  against each other, with no reference side.

Each needed machinery no declarative channel provides, and none had produced a finding.
They are recorded here so their absence is a decision on the record rather than an
oversight nobody noticed.

## Behaviors by area

### `build`

| Behavior | Status | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `build` uses the container runtime it was told to use — flavor and binary — instead of hardcoding docker. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test + the Podman CI lane | CLOSED by [#694](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/694). `build` constructed `CliDocker::new()` at four sites, so it ignored `--runtime` entirely and `--docker-path` with it: MEASURED, `deacon build --runtime podman` exited **0** having built with docker on a machine where podman was not installed at all. It now resolves once through the shared helper and threads that instance, which carries the FLAVOR as well as the path — podman needs the flavor, because the `localhost/` image-ref qualification and the SELinux/userns handling hang off `CliRuntime::is_podman()`. **The refusal this issue originally proposed was NOT taken, and the reason is the finding.** Refusing would have entrenched a limitation that the evidence says may not exist: the reference supports podman (`src/test/cli.podman.test.ts` runs `up --docker-path podman` over image-with-features AND dockerfile-with-features, asserting `outcome: success`) and does **not** branch its build command on it — `containerFeatures.ts:67` and `singleContainer.ts:184` push `buildx build` unconditionally, and `podman buildx` is an alias for `podman build`. Podman supports `--build-context`, which is what Feature installation needs; deacon likewise pushes `buildx build` as ARGUMENTS with the binary taken from the runtime path, and already implements both podman-specific behaviors. So the `// deacon build is docker-only today` comment reads like the stale `dockerfile_utils` "small subset" doc [#686](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/686) retired: a claim that outlived its truth and then excused a gap. It was stale, and the Podman CI lane said so directly. On [run 32990502325](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/actions/runs/32990502325), under `DEACON_CONTAINER_RUNTIME=podman`, `smoke_basic::smoke_build_json_then_text` — which builds a real Dockerfile configuration and asserts `{"outcome":"success"}` plus `imageName` — **PASSED**, alongside `test_build_arg_handling`, 433/433. That job had run those same tests before and they told us nothing, because `build` used docker no matter what the job selected. **Scope of the claim, because it is narrower than "build works on podman":** `integration_build` is NOT in the `mvp-integration` profile the Podman job runs, so this covers the `smoke_basic` build tests and `parity_hermetic`'s build cases, not deacon's full `build` surface, and not Feature-installing builds. Widening that selection is the next step, recorded on [#30](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/30). Not verifiable in this dev container: podman is installed and answers `-v`, but cannot create user namespaces (`newuidmap`) or mount fuse, so it runs no containers here — `build --runtime podman` was confirmed to REACH podman (it fails with podman's own rootless error), which is as far as local measurement goes. |
| `_REMOTE_USER_HOME` / `_CONTAINER_USER_HOME` are the user's REAL home, read from the image's passwd database, not derived from the user's name. | matches the reference | 3 unit tests + 1 Docker-gated test | CLOSED by [#695](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/695), found mining `getHomeFolder` / `getEntPasswd` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 23). Two of the four variables the Features spec guarantees every `install.sh` were computed on the HOST by string formatting — `if name == "root" { "/root" } else { format!("/home/{name}") }` — so any user whose home lives elsewhere was handed a path that does not exist. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on a fixture whose Dockerfile is `RUN useradd -m -d /opt/custom-home devuser`: the reference reports `_REMOTE_USER_HOME=/opt/custom-home`, deacon reported `/home/devuser` — and **both exited 0 with `outcome: success`**, so nothing in either result document distinguished them. deacon now emits the reference's own construction, which resolves the home INSIDE the build from the real passwd DB rather than computing it anywhere: `$( (command -v getent >/dev/null 2>&1 \|\| getent passwd '<user>' \|\| grep -E '^<user>\|^[^:]*:[^:]*:<user>:' /etc/passwd \|\| true) \| cut -d: -f6)`. Every part of that shape earns its place and each was verified against a real image rather than reasoned about: `getent` is tried first but **busybox has none at all** (measured — `command -v getent` is empty there, which is the first case upstream's `getEntPasswd` tests pin), and the `/etc/passwd` fallback still resolves `/home/foo` for a user created with `adduser -D -h /home/foo`; the pattern matches by NAME **or by UID**, so a numeric user resolves too (measured in busybox: uid 1000 → `/home/foo`); and `\|\| true` keeps a missing user an empty value rather than a failed build. The user name is single-quoted with embedded quotes escaped in BOTH halves, since it comes from configuration and reaches a shell. The Docker-gated test asserts on IMAGE CONTENTS for a deliberately non-conventional home — with a conventional one the guess and the lookup agree, and the test would pass against the defect. Two sibling guesses remain and are named in the issue rather than silently left: `user_mapping::default_home_dir` and the dotfiles `~` expansion. deacon's RUNTIME path was already correct (`container_env_probe::resolve_home_folder` probes `HOME` then falls back to `getent`); only the build-time path guessed. |
| The Feature-install stage runs as `root` and hands the image back to the user it was running as, so a base Dockerfile ending in a non-root `USER` still installs Features. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test | CLOSED by [#685](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/685). deacon's generated install stage went straight from its `FROM` to the install `RUN`, so `install.sh` executed as whatever user the base Dockerfile left in effect — and `grep -rn "USER root" crates` returned ZERO hits in product code, the only occurrence being a comment in the parity normalizer asserting the opposite. Any Dockerfile ending in `USER vscode` — what the `mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/*` images and most hand-written devcontainer Dockerfiles do — therefore FAILED to build with Features. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on a fixture whose Dockerfile ends `USER user2` and which declares one local Feature: the reference exits 0 and the Feature reports `_REMOTE_USER=user2`; deacon exited **1**, its install `RUN` dying on `cannot create /marker.txt: Permission denied`. The reference's shape, confirmed from both its source (`getContainerFeaturesBaseDockerFile`, `src/spec-configuration/containerFeaturesConfiguration.ts:206-224` at v0.87.0) and the produced image's own history (`USER root` … `ARG _DEV_CONTAINERS_IMAGE_USER=user2` … `USER user2`): become root after the `FROM`, install, then restore. deacon now emits the same, on BOTH generator entry points — the ARG-driven `FROM` and the stage spliced into a user-authored Dockerfile. One deliberate difference in mechanism, not in result: the reference declares `ARG _DEV_CONTAINERS_IMAGE_USER=root` and overrides it with a build arg, while deacon bakes the resolved value in as the ARG's default, because the spliced form is assembled by a different build path that would otherwise have to thread the same build arg twice. The restored user follows the IMAGE, not the configuration's `containerUser`, matching the reference's `imageBuildInfo.user`. Backed by a Docker-gated test that asserts on IMAGE CONTENTS (`docker run <tag> cat /marker.txt`, `whoami`) rather than the result document — with only the *user resolution* wrong the build still reports `outcome: success` while handing every Feature the wrong identity, which no outcome assertion can see. Not a parity case: the harness cannot assert on the contents of a produced image. |
| A Dockerfile's `FROM` and `USER` are resolved with real variable substitution — in-stage `ARG`/`ENV` with the reference's precedence, the base image's own environment, `${var:+word}` / `${var:-word}` expressions, and stage references in either direction. | matches the reference | 89-case differential table + 1 Docker-gated test | CLOSED by [#686](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/686). `dockerfile_utils` re-implemented `findBaseImage` / `findUserStatement` with a line-oriented approximation that consulted only the `ARG`s declared before the first `FROM`. MEASURED differentially against the reference's OWN `src/spec-node/dockerfileUtils.ts` at tag v0.87.0 — compiled and invoked directly, and self-checked first against every expectation in upstream's own `src/test/dockerfileUtils.test.ts` before being trusted to judge deacon — **27 of 89 cases diverged, 17 of them cases the reference's own suite asserts.** Five root causes: in-stage `ARG`/`ENV` never consulted (12 rows); no base-image `ENV` fallback (the parameter did not exist); stage references resolved only BACKWARDS, so a `FROM stage3` declared before `stage3` returned the string `"stage3"` as if it were an image reference; variable expressions unimplemented, so `${cloud:+mcr.microsoft.com/}azure-cli:latest` with `cloud=true` became **`trueazure-cli:latest`**; and no cycle detection. `ensureDockerfileHasFinalStageName` was byte-exact clean on all 7 of its cases throughout — that half had genuinely been ported, which is why the gap survived. The engine is now a statement-for-statement port of `findValue` / `replaceVariables` / `getExpressionValue`, including the `beforeInstructionIndex` scoping that makes an inherited stage contribute its `ENV`s but not its `ARG`s. Observable end to end, not merely at the unit boundary: this value is exported to every Feature's `install.sh` as `_REMOTE_USER`/`_CONTAINER_USER` and supplies the user restored by the row above. On a Dockerfile whose `USER` comes from an `ARG`, the reference reported `user2` and deacon reported `root`. Backed by `crates/core/tests/dockerfile_utils_parity.rs`, whose every expectation was measured rather than written — regenerating it means re-measuring, never hand-editing an expectation to make a test pass. Hermetic; needs no oracle at run time. Not a parity case: these are library-level answers the CLI surfaces only indirectly. |
| `resolve_base_image` reports nothing for `FROM scratch` and for a `FROM` whose `ARG` resolved to nothing, where the reference reports `"scratch"` and the empty string. | documented choice | 89-case differential table | The reference's `findBaseImage` returns these verbatim and its callers cope. Every deacon caller uses the result to PULL and INSPECT a real image — for its `devcontainer.metadata` and its baked-in `USER` — and neither value names one, so passing them on would turn "nothing to inherit" into a failed pull reported as a warning on every build of a `FROM scratch` Dockerfile. The faithful answer is still available and still measured: `Dockerfile::base_image` returns exactly what the reference returns and is asserted against it across all 89 cases, while `resolve_base_image` is that plus this guard, asserted separately so the guard cannot be mistaken for reference behavior ([#686](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/686)). Related and deliberately NOT guarded: stage-label lookup is case-SENSITIVE, matching the reference's plain-object `stagesByLabel`, even though BuildKit itself is case-insensitive there — following the reference costs nothing because the value only feeds metadata inspection and the real build is resolved by BuildKit, so a `FROM base` naming a stage aliased `Base` is reported as an external image by both CLIs. |
| A BuildKit build flag (`--cache-to`) is accepted and passed through, and the build still produces the configured image — including a LOCAL cache export on a container-driver builder, which actually writes the cache. | matches the reference | 1 scenario + 1 Docker-gated test | **Status corrected in [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 5: this row read `deacon extension` on the ground that `--cache-to` was a deacon-only surface, and that was simply false.** `devcontainer build --help` at 0.87.0 lists `--cache-to`, along with `--platform`, `--output`, `--cache-from`, `--label`, `--push` and `--buildkit` — three rows in this section claimed a deacon-only surface for a flag the reference has, and each was written from a reading of the spec rather than from the reference's own help output. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-flags` with `--cache-to type=inline`: both CLIs exit 0 and both produce the image. The scope of the claim is unchanged and deliberate — acceptance and pass-through, exercised with `type=inline`, the one export type buildx's default `docker` driver supports across Docker versions, so the case measures deacon rather than the runner. It used `type=local`, which needs the `docker-container` driver and has no Podman equivalent; there the build produced no image and the case failed for reasons unrelated to deacon. Which driver deacon reaches at all was its own defect, closed by [#595](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/595) — see the `--output` row below. The narrowing to `type=inline` is kept anyway: it is the export type every driver supports, so the case measures deacon rather than the runner's builder configuration. **The claim it named as out of reach is now reached, by a Docker-gated test rather than a case ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 11).** The `type=inline` narrowing was right and stays; what it could not assert was that a LOCAL export writes anything, because that needs the `docker-container` driver and the case vocabulary has no way to create a buildx builder — an operation takes argv and fixtures, not host setup, and inventing that primitive for one claim is a harness design decision rather than a data edit (the Docker-gated-test fallback is the one [#619](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/619) took, for a different reason). `integration_build_output::build_honors_a_container_driver_builder_and_can_export_a_local_cache` creates the builder, selects it with `BUILDX_BUILDER` (not `docker buildx use`, which mutates host-global state concurrent tests share), builds a configuration that declares a Feature — so the build is on the merged base + install-stage path, the one the retired `--builder default` pin existed to prop up — and asserts `index.json` on disk, which an `outcome: success` cannot give. Upstream asserts this same pair over a Dockerfile-with-Features and an image-with-Features configuration ('should execute successfully and export buildx cache with container builder', `src/test/cli.build.test.ts` at v0.87.0); BOTH were MEASURED to agree at oracle 0.87.0 before it landed, each CLI exiting 0 and writing `index.json`, as did the two Compose arms alongside them — the `--cache-to` refusal (already pinned by `case-build-upstream-compose-cache-to-refused`) and a featureless Compose build on the same builder, both agreeing on both sides. The `index.json` assertion is NOT uniformly strong and the test says so rather than implying otherwise: on a daemon without the containerd image store the docker driver cannot serve this exporter and a redirected build fails it outright, but on one WITH it — this repo's dev container — a deliberate `BUILDX_BUILDER=default` run of the same configuration exits 0 and writes the cache too (measured). There the discriminating assertion is the builder identity, which holds on every substrate, so both are kept and neither is redundant. |
| A Dockerfile instruction that exits non-zero fails the build, and the failure is reported at the build stage rather than as a later container-creation failure. | matches the reference | 3 scenarios | The exit code alone cannot distinguish a build failure from a container-creation failure; the declared failure phase is what makes the two different facts rather than… |
| `build` reports and tags the FEATURE-EXTENDED image, so a user-supplied `--image-name` resolves to the image with the Features installed rather than to the pre-Feature… | matches the reference | 4 scenarios | This project shipped the inverse once: the post-build Feature pass layered correctly and the user's tag still pointed at the base, which every outcome-only assertion… The count read `2` while three cases already carried the behavior and is corrected here alongside the fourth, `case-build-dockerfile-feature-multi-order-label` (#536), which reads the `devcontainer.metadata` label off the image the user's `--image-name` names — a tag left pointing at the pre-Feature base carries no Feature entries at all, so the ordering case pins this claim as a by-product of pinning its own. |
| `build` stamps `org.deacon.configHash` on the image it produces; the reference CLI sets no such label. | deacon extension | 2 scenarios | Measured against oracle 0.87.0 on fx-build-image-labels and fx-build-image-args, not inferred. A deacon-only bookkeeping label in deacon's own `org.deacon.*` namespace —… |
| The `devcontainer.metadata` label on a BUILT image records one entry per installed Feature plus the configuration pick, so a later consumer of the image can read what it… | matches the reference | 5 scenarios | CLOSED by #436 for the Dockerfile and image-reference shapes, and by #440 for the three that #436 left unstamped — `--push`, `--output` and Compose. `build` used to stamp a hardcoded `[{"configHash":…}]`, dropping every Feature entry. It now writes the value `up` writes, from the one shared construction (`up::merged_config::container_metadata_entries`), stamped on the final image once it exists. Re-measured against oracle 0.87.0: on fx-build-dockerfile-feature both sides record `{"id":"./features/marker"}`; on a config with `remoteUser`/`remoteEnv` and no Features both record the config pick; on a base image carrying its own label both prepend that entry. On fx-build-image-labels and fx-build-image-args — no Features, nothing in the config pick — neither side writes the label at all, which is what the two differential cases observe. The byte form still differs in JSON whitespace (`[{…}]` vs `[ {…} ]`); that is the separate documented choice recorded by `bhv-container-metadata-label-serialization` (its key-order half was a deacon defect and was closed by #394). #440 then closed the three shapes that were skipped rather than fixed: after #436 a `--push`, `--output` or Compose build carried NO label where it had previously carried a wrong one. deacon now builds into the local daemon, stamps, and publishes from there, so all four shapes go through the one construction. Measured on this branch: a Dockerfile config with `containerEnv`/`remoteUser` pushed to a throwaway `registry:2` and pulled back records `[{"containerEnv":{…},"remoteUser":"root"}]`, byte-identical to what the local shape stamps; the same config exported with `--output type=docker,dest=…` carries it in the exported tar's image config; and a Compose config with a local Feature records `[{"id":"./features/marker"},{"containerEnv":{…},"remoteUser":"root"}]`. The one shape still unstamped is a MULTI-platform `--push`/`--output`, which BuildKit will not load into the daemon — deacon warns rather than pretending. Fifth scenario added at [#536](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/536), which closed a COVERAGE gap rather than a defect: the label's entries are ordered — one per installed Feature, in install order, then the configuration pick — and every `build` fixture declaring a Feature declared exactly ONE, so nothing would have reported the day `build`'s order stopped matching `up`'s (they share `resolve_features_ordered` and `apply_features_and_lockfile`). `case-build-dockerfile-feature-multi-order-label` builds `fx-build-feature-multi-order`, whose declared order (`late`, `early`) is inverted by `installsAfter` and which pulls in a third Feature (`pulled`) through `dependsOn` that the configuration never names. Measured at oracle 0.87.0, both CLIs, no divergence: `[{"id":"./features/early"},{"id":"./features/pulled"},{"id":"./features/late"},{"containerEnv":{…},"remoteUser":"root"}]`, differing only in the JSON whitespace this row already records, and both produced images report `early/pulled/late` from the marker file the three `install.sh` scripts append to — so the label records the order the installs actually ran in. The assertion pins the label's whole STRING rather than a subset of parsed entries because `jsonSubset` over an array is order-insensitive; and it is a spec-expectation rather than a differential because tolerating the whitespace on `chan-image.labels.devcontainer.metadata` would excuse an order difference on the same path. The cross-CLI half is carried by `case-up-feature-multi-dependency-order-differential`, which compares the marker sequence through the same resolver. |
| build produces a container image and reports the build outcome, matching the reference. | matches the reference | 3 scenarios | Backed by case-build-image-labels-differential and case-build-image-args-differential, which compare the produced IMAGE's whole configuration between the two CLIs. Since #436 closed the `devcontainer.metadata` difference, the only key on which the two sides differ is deacon's `org.deacon.configHash`, tolerated by one named allowlist entry. |
| The image a `build` produces carries what the configuration and Dockerfile authored into it — labels, environment, command and working directory — and `build.args` reach… | matches the reference | 3 scenarios | Measured against oracle 0.87.0 on fx-build-image-labels and fx-build-image-args: `Env`, `Cmd`, `Entrypoint`, `WorkingDir` and every authored label agree exactly on both… |
| `build --output <spec>` exports the built image through the exporter the user named, on the buildx builder the user selected. | matches the reference | 3 scenarios | Fixes [#595](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/595). **Two corrections and then a fix, all from [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 5.** The row read `deacon extension`, justified as "the reference CLI has no `--output` flag on `build`, so there is nothing to compare against" — it has one, and the comparison that was never run is the one that found the defect. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-flags` with a container-driver builder selected exactly as upstream's own e2e test does it (`docker buildx create --driver docker-container --use`), `--output type=oci,dest=…`: the reference exits 0 and writes a 3,367,424-byte tar; deacon exits 1 with `OCI exporter is not supported for the docker driver`. The cause is that `commands/build/mod.rs` pins `--builder default` on every buildx invocation, so the builder the user selected is silently overridden and every capability needing a non-docker driver — OCI export, local cache export, multi-platform output — goes with it. That pin is the fix for [#391](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/391) (a container-driver builder cannot see the local daemon store that deacon's chained `FROM` names), so this is the unwritten cost of a deliberate choice rather than an oversight, and it shares a root with [#593](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/593). What the two existing scenarios pin is unchanged and still correct — `type=docker,dest=<path>` writes the tar and the EXPORTED image carries `devcontainer.metadata` (added at #440; before it, the export was handed to the primary BuildKit invocation and left no local image to stamp). They are `spec-expectation`, so the corrected classification costs them nothing. **THE FIX IS A DESIGN CHANGE, not a flag edit.** Removing the pin alone would have reintroduced [#391](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/391), so the chain it protected had to go: `deacon build` now splices the Feature-install stage onto the end of the base Dockerfile (a synthesized `FROM <image>` for an image-reference configuration) and builds both in ONE invocation, with `devcontainer.metadata` computed from the base image beforehand and written by that same build. `up` does the same for a Dockerfile-plus-Features configuration. This is the reference's own shape — it concatenates `dockerfilePrefixContent` + the user's Dockerfile + the Feature stages into `Dockerfile-with-features` and builds once — and the primary build now runs through `docker buildx build` rather than `docker build`, which always uses the daemon's own "default" instance and ignores the selected builder regardless of any pin. MEASURED after the fix, same fixture, container-driver builder selected: `#0 building with "p595test" instance using docker-container driver`, exit 0, and the OCI tar written; with the default builder selected, still exit 0 and still written. A third scenario now guards it — `build_honors_a_container_driver_builder_and_can_oci_export` in `integration_build_output`, which provisions its own `docker-container` builder and selects it with `BUILDX_BUILDER` (per-invocation, so it does not mutate the daemon-global selection concurrent tests share). It lives there rather than in `parity/cases/` because the declarative runner models no builder provisioning. |
| `build --platform <os/arch>` on a DOCKERFILE configuration builds for the named platform and produces a real image carrying the configuration's authored labels. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | **Status corrected in [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 5**, which read `deacon extension` because "`--platform` is not a reference `build` flag". It is: `devcontainer build --help` at 0.87.0 lists it. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-flags` with `--platform linux/amd64` from an aarch64 host: both CLIs exit 0 and both produce an image whose `.Architecture` is `amd64`. The row is also narrowed to the Dockerfile configuration, which is all its scenario ever covered — the image-reference path is a separate row below and is broken ([#593](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/593)). Its assertion is unchanged and still earns its keep: it replaced one that tolerated either outcome and therefore asserted nothing on any machine with BuildKit. |
| `build --push` to a registry that cannot be reached fails with a non-zero exit and a diagnostic naming the push as the failing step, rather than reporting success. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Both CLIs fail non-zero and name the push. deacon's message used to name the WRONG registry — it pushed its internal `deacon-build:<hash>` tag, which resolves to Docker Hub, and that push failed first (#438). Closed by #440's restructure: the push is now deacon's own step over the image names the user supplied, so the diagnostic reads `failed to push 'registry.invalid/conformance-push:latest'`, and the case asserts that string rather than the bare phrase. |
| `build --push` pushes the image names the user supplied and nothing else. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was an open nonconformance (#438): deacon handed the push to BuildKit, which pushes every `-t` tag, so the internal `deacon-build:<hash>` bookkeeping tag went along — no registry component, therefore Docker Hub, therefore an authorization failure that made `build --push` fail against EVERY registry, reachable or not. Closed as a necessary consequence of #440 rather than as separate work: stamping `devcontainer.metadata` on a pushed image requires building locally and pushing afterwards, and a push deacon performs itself has to choose its targets. It pushes the `--image-name`s (and, only when none were given, the deterministic tag — the same thing BuildKit would have pushed). Measured on this branch: `--push --image-name localhost:<port>/…` against a throwaway `registry:2` succeeds and the pulled-back image carries the metadata label; `--push --image-name registry.invalid/conformance-push:latest` fails naming that target and not Docker Hub. |
| A Dockerfile whose first line is a `# syntax=` frontend directive reaches the builder with that directive still first, so the produced image is the one BuildKit's frontend built. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e test fixtures at tag `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 1) — coverage chosen by the reference's authors rather than by ours. The directive is meaningful only when it is line 1, so a CLI that rewrites, prepends to, or re-emits the Dockerfile — to inject Feature layers, or to resolve an `ARG` consumed by `FROM` — can displace it, after which Docker reads it as an ordinary comment and silently falls back to the classic frontend. Measured at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-dockerfile-syntax-directive`: the two images agree on everything except deacon's `org.deacon.configHash`, the extension every `chan-image` case already tolerates. The fixture carries one authored `LABEL` for a reason worth keeping: without it the reference's image has NO labels at all and the two sides differ at the `labels` NODE, which a tolerance scoped to one key inside that map cannot reach — it reported as a STALE tolerance instead of a tolerated difference. |
| `build.options` reaches the builder, so a `--label` the configuration asks to be passed through appears on the produced image. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was an open nonconformance ([#492](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/492)): deacon accepted `build.options`, echoed it from `read-configuration`, and never passed it to the build — the silent-fallback shape constitution IV rules out, with no signal to the user because nothing failed. The cause was a SHAPE error, not a missing wire: `build.options` was read with `as_object()` and folded into the same map as `build.args`, so the spec's array (`devcontainerjson-reference.md`: "an array of Docker image build options … passed to the build command when building a Dockerfile") matched nothing and was dropped. `build.args` and `build.options` are now distinct fields, and a non-array `build.options` is a validation error rather than a silent drop. Pass-through semantics were measured off the reference rather than guessed — at 0.87.0 it appends the array VERBATIM as discrete argv elements, immediately after the `--build-arg` pairs and before the context, with no filtering — and deacon now does the same on BOTH build paths: `build` (`commands/build`) and `up`'s Dockerfile build (`commands/up/image_build.rs`), which the issue asked be checked. Verified on `fx-upstream-build-target-options`: `docker image inspect` reports `test_build_options=success` on the image from each path, `/var/test-marker` still reads `||test-content||` (so `build.target` is unchanged), and `case-build-upstream-target-and-options` is green against the pinned oracle with no tolerance beyond the already-adjudicated `org.deacon.configHash`. |
| `build --frozen-lockfile` refuses a workspace with no lockfile, before it builds anything. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#556](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/556). The flag's contract is "resolution may not change the lockfile", and with no lockfile there is nothing for resolution to agree with. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-lockfile-no-lockfile`, BEFORE the fix: the reference exited 1 with `{"outcome":"error","message":"Lockfile does not exist."}` and left the workspace clean; deacon exited 0, built the Feature-extended image, and WROTE `.devcontainer-lock.json` (327 bytes) — the inverse of what the flag asks for. The cause was admitted in the source: `commands/build/mod.rs`'s `BuildArgs` documented `no_lockfile`/`frozen_lockfile` as "not yet consumed by `build`'s lockfile-writing path", so both were parsed, threaded and dropped, and `apply_features_and_lockfile` wrote unconditionally. AFTER: deacon exits 1 with `Lockfile does not exist.` and writes nothing. The fix is a WIRE, not a second implementation — the skip / semantic-compare / write decision `up` already made now lives in `commands/shared/lockfile` and BOTH subcommands reach it, so a lockfile's bytes and its fate never depend on which subcommand resolved the Features (the seam #563 opened when it consolidated every writer onto `canonical_lockfile_json`). The refusal happens BEFORE the build, matching where the reference throws: its `writeLockfile` is reached from `generateFeaturesConfig`, which runs before the image build. That same call site is why the refusal is gated on the configuration DECLARING Features — `generateFeaturesConfig` returns early when it does not, so `build --frozen-lockfile` on `{"image": …}` with no lockfile exits 0 (measured, both CLIs); the gate also corrected `up`, which refused there and now agrees. `case-build-upstream-lockfile-frozen-missing` asserted the FIXED behavior (exit 1, lockfile absent) on both channels from the day it landed, so it turned green on the fix and NOT on a case edit. Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e fixtures at `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 2). |
| `build --no-lockfile` suppresses lockfile generation: the build succeeds and no `devcontainer-lock.json` is written. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#556](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/556), the same missing wire as the row above seen through the other flag, and the half NO exit code can catch. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-lockfile-no-lockfile`, BEFORE the fix: both CLIs exited 0 and both produced an image; the reference left `.devcontainer-lock.json` ABSENT, deacon wrote it (327 bytes, the resolved `git:1.3.2` entry). AFTER: deacon leaves it absent. The two flags are kept as separate rows and separate cases deliberately — their correct outcomes are opposite (refuse vs succeed-and-write-nothing), so a fix that wired only one would leave the other silently broken and a single row would go green while half the defect remained; the shared `LockfilePolicy` makes that impossible to get half-right, because one enum decides both. `--no-lockfile` also means "do not READ": measured at 0.87.0, a deliberately stale lockfile came back byte-identical from a `--no-lockfile` build, and deacon now leaves it alone too (deacon never pinned resolution from the lockfile, so the read half was already vacuous — the write half was the observable). `case-build-upstream-lockfile-no-lockfile-flag` asserted the fixed behavior from the day it landed and turned green without an assertion edit. |
| Passing `--no-lockfile` and `--frozen-lockfile` together is rejected as mutually exclusive, before any Docker, registry or filesystem work. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The two flags ask for contradictory things — ignore the lockfile entirely, and require it present and unchanged — so naming both is a user error rather than a precedence question, and neither CLI picks a winner. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-lockfile-no-lockfile`: both exit 1 with the same sentence (`--no-lockfile and --frozen-lockfile are mutually exclusive.`) and both leave the workspace byte-identical. `case-build-upstream-lockfile-flags-mutually-exclusive` needs no daemon and is `resourceGroup: fs-heavy` — the group that isolates a workspace WITHOUT touching Docker. `none` was the obvious choice and is the wrong one, and perturbing the case is what showed why: a `none` case runs against the COMMITTED fixture directory, so dropping `--no-lockfile` from the argv during the watched-to-fail run left a real build to run and a stray `.devcontainer-lock.json` in `parity/fixtures/fx-upstream-lockfile-no-lockfile/` — the untracked-copy failure #423 documented for `fx-upgrade-overlay-lockfile`, which would have silently changed what the other cases on that fixture measure. A `build`/`up` operation must never run un-isolated however certain today's argv looks; the `absent` filesystem claim is kept as the second line of defence. `chan-stderr` is deliberately not declared — the reference prepends its whole `yargs` usage block, so comparing the stream would report a difference about help-text formatting. Upstream drives this from three parameterised tests; the graduated pair is the one both CLIs share. Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e fixtures at `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 2). |
| A `devcontainer-lock.json`'s `integrity` PINS the Feature content: a Feature whose content does not match the recorded checksum is refused rather than installed, and the lockfile is not rewritten. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | Fixes [#571](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/571). deacon never read the field. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-lockfile-oci-integrity`, whose committed lockfile names the CORRECT `resolved` digest for `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/git:1.3.2` and an `integrity` differing from it in ONE hex character. BEFORE: the reference exited 1 (`Could not resolve Feature manifest for …` → `ERR: Feature … could not be processed`) leaving the file byte-unchanged; deacon exited 0, built the Feature-extended image, and **overwrote the corrupted `integrity` with the digest it just fetched** — so a tampered lockfile was not merely ignored, it was silently repaired, and a `git diff` was the only thing that would ever have said so. AFTER: deacon exits 1 and the file is byte-unchanged, on `build` and on `up` alike (both measured; `up` also creates no container). The control that made this a measurement rather than an inference: the same fixture with the CORRECT `integrity` builds successfully on both CLIs, so one hex character is the whole difference between the two runs. **Both authorities agreed deacon was the wrong side**, which is unusual and worth stating: `parity/spec/113500f4/devcontainer-lockfile.md` gives as a goal of the file "Improved security by detecting when a feature's release artifact changes after its checksum was first recorded in the lockfile ('trust on first use')", so this was not a spec-silent surface where the reference is merely the authority.
