The FIX is the reference's shape, transcribed from the bundle rather than guessed — because "compare the checksum after downloading" would have been wrong, and weaker. `integrity` is the LOOKUP KEY: `kb(ctx, ref, integrity)` → `ji`, which does `let r = e.version; t && (r = t)` and requests `…/v2/<path>/manifests/<integrity>` INSTEAD of the tag, then re-checks the returned `docker-content-digest` against it (`if (r && C !== r) throw`). deacon's `FeatureFetcher::fetch_feature_pinned` now does exactly that, so a Feature whose content no longer matches cannot be RESOLVED at all — there is no window in which it is downloaded, installed, or written back, which a post-hoc comparison would have left open. The consequence beyond the tampering case, measured on both CLIs: a lockfile pins a FLOATING tag too. With `git:1` recorded at 1.3.2's digest, both CLIs install 1.3.2 and leave the lockfile untouched, though an unpinned resolution of `:1` today yields 1.3.8 — that IS "trust on first use", and `upgrade` is how the pin moves. Before the fix deacon silently followed the tag and rewrote the file. The pin applies to auto-installed `dependsOn` targets as well as declared Features, keyed by the id AS WRITTEN, because that is the key `generateLockfile` writes and the key the reference looks up. The lockfile is read for pinning under `--frozen-lockfile` exactly as by default; `--no-lockfile` is the only carve-out (next row). The near miss worth keeping on the record: `build --frozen-lockfile` DID exit 1 here even before the fix, because the freshly-resolved entry differs from the corrupted one — but that is a comparison of two documents, not a verification of content, and it is opt-in; the default path, which is what upstream's own test exercises and what a user gets, checked nothing. Two scenarios, deliberately: `case-build-upstream-lockfile-oci-integrity` (`live-differential`, nightly) asserted the FIXED behaviour on both channels from the day it landed and turned green on the code rather than on a case edit, and `case-build-lockfile-integrity-pin-enforced` is its `spec-expectation` twin with identical assertions, so the behaviour GATES — a regression here would silently reinstate a security defect, and the nightly gates nothing. Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e fixtures at `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 3 — the item batch 2 named "the most landable of the deferrals" and could not land because it had not been measured). |
| `--no-lockfile` means "do not READ" as well as "do not write": resolution is not pinned by the lockfile's `integrity`, and a corrupted lockfile neither fails the build nor is repaired by it. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The boundary of the pin above, and the half a fix is most likely to get wrong by over-applying — an over-applied pin is invisible, because it looks like security. TRANSCRIBED: the reference computes `A.noLockfile ? {lockfile: undefined} : await fI(t)` in `generateFeaturesConfig` and threads that `undefined` into every Feature lookup, so the flag suppresses the read, not merely the write. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-lockfile-oci-integrity`, the same tampered workspace that exits 1 on a plain `build`: under `--no-lockfile` both CLIs exit 0, both produce a Feature-extended image, and both leave the corrupted lockfile byte-identical. One flag is the whole difference between the two runs. `case-build-lockfile-integrity-no-lockfile-bypasses-pin` asserts all three — the exit code is the carve-out, `outcome: success` is what makes that 0 mean "the image was built" rather than "something quietly declined to happen", and the exact corrupted bytes are the write half of the same flag. Without this case the cheapest wrong implementation — read the lockfile unconditionally — passes every other lockfile case in the suite. |
| A `devcontainer-lock.json` that is not valid JSON fails the build, before anything is built, and is left on disk unchanged. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The other consequence of reading the lockfile at all ([#571](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/571)): once `integrity` pins resolution, a lockfile that cannot be parsed is an input the run depends on and cannot have, not a cosmetic problem to be papered over. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-lockfile-unreadable`: the reference exits 1 with `{"outcome":"error","message":"Expected property name or '}' in JSON at position 2 …"}` and leaves the file byte-unchanged — its `readLockfile` (`fI`) swallows only `ENOENT`, and a `SyntaxError` carries no `.code`, so the `throw e` rethrows. BEFORE the fix deacon exited 0, built the image, and REPLACED the unparseable file with a freshly-resolved lockfile: the same silent repair as the tampered-`integrity` row, reached by a different corruption. deacon refuses in the shared pre-build gate (`commands/shared/lockfile::ensure_lockfile_usable`), which is where `--frozen-lockfile`'s "Lockfile does not exist." already lived, so both refusals happen before any image is built. The read is deliberately NOT the strict, fully-modeled `read_lockfile` deacon uses for `--frozen-lockfile`: pinning parses strictly and validates only the one field it is about to use, because a lockfile the REFERENCE writes for a `direct-tarball` Feature has a `resolved` that is a URL, which deacon's structural validation rejects — failing that build would be a deacon-only surprise for a migrating user on a path where deacon has no reason to be the stricter of the two. `case-build-lockfile-unreadable-rejected` is `spec-expectation` + `fs-heavy`, which puts it in the HERMETIC lane, and that is a claim rather than a convenience: the refusal happens while reading a local file, so a regression that moved it after the Feature fetch or the base build would fail to reach the assertion at all inside `parity_hermetic`'s network namespace. |
| `--buildkit never` together with `--platform` is refused rather than silently satisfied by building for the HOST platform. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | Fixes [#592](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/592). `--platform` is a BuildKit capability and the legacy builder cannot target a platform at all, so the combination has exactly two honest answers and "build the wrong thing and exit 0" is neither. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-flags` from an aarch64 host, BEFORE the fix: the reference exits 1 with `{"outcome":"error","message":"--platform or --push require BuildKit enabled."}` and builds nothing; deacon exited 0, reported success, and produced an arm64 image after being asked for `linux/amd64`. The silent-fallback shape constitution IV rules out, and the worst variety of it — the artifact is plausible, tagged, and runs on the machine that built it, so nothing downstream can tell. **The gate already existed and asked the wrong question**, which is why this was a one-condition fix rather than new code: it consulted `is_buildkit_available`, i.e. whether the host HAS BuildKit, and `--buildkit never` does not make BuildKit unavailable — it tells deacon not to use it. `should_use_buildkit` could not be reused as the missing half either: it returns false both when the user disabled BuildKit and when they simply did not ask for it, and the second case still runs BuildKit, because the primary build sets no `DOCKER_BUILDKIT` at all and the daemon's own default applies — gating on it would have refused the ordinary `deacon build --platform linux/amd64`, which works. The new `buildkit_disabled` answers only the decidable question (did something EXPLICITLY switch it off: `--buildkit never`, or a falsey `DOCKER_BUILDKIT`) and is pure logic over the flag and one environment variable, so it short-circuits before any probe. **Scope is deliberate and narrower than the reference's rule**: only the flags the PRIMARY build carries are gated — `--platform`, and `--cache-to`, which the legacy builder rejected with a leaked `unknown flag: --cache-to`. `--push` and `--output` are NOT gated, measured rather than assumed: a single-platform build defers both to their own pass (`docker push` / a `buildx` export), which runs regardless of how the primary build ran, so both work under `--buildkit never` today and refusing them would break a working invocation for nothing. The reference refuses `--push` there and — measured — exits 0 while exporting NOTHING for `--output`; deacon honouring both is a superset in the direction that gives the caller what they asked for. Two scenarios: `case-build-upstream-buildkit-never-platform-refused` (`live-differential`) pins that the two CLIs agree on refusing, and turned green on the fix without an assertion edit; `case-build-buildkit-never-platform-refused-spec` (hermetic) pins what deacon SAYS, including the `description` that names the switch — the difference between an actionable message and `Enable BuildKit`, which is advice the user has already followed. |
| `build --platform <os/arch>` builds for the named platform even when producing the image takes a SECOND build — the Feature-layering pass or the `devcontainer.metadata` stamp. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | Fixes [#593](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/593). deacon builds the base as a local intermediate tag and then issues a second build whose `FROM` names it. That second build carried no `--platform`, so BuildKit resolved the base for the HOST platform, missed in the local store, and fell through to a registry that had never heard of `docker.io/library/deacon-build-run:<n>-0` — the user saw a registry authorization failure for an image deacon invented. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 from an aarch64 host with `--platform linux/amd64`, BEFORE the fix: the reference exits 0 and produces a genuine `amd64` image; deacon exited 1. AFTER: exit 0, `.Architecture` == `amd64`, and the `devcontainer.metadata` label byte-identical to the one a build with no `--platform` writes. **The issue's own title understated the scope and the fix corrects it**: the discriminator is not the configuration SHAPE but whether a second build runs at all. Measured on all three, before and after — an image-reference configuration with `containerEnv`, a DOCKERFILE configuration with `containerEnv`, and a configuration with Features — every one failed and every one now succeeds. `fx-build-flags` escaped only because it declares nothing to record, so the stamp pass returns early and no second build happens, which is why `case-build-platform-flag` stayed green throughout and why the gap was invisible. The fix threaded the platform through both secondary builds by putting it on `BuildOptions`, next to the `--builder default` pin those builds carried for the neighbouring reason: a build whose `FROM` names an image deacon just built locally must ask for the same platform that image was built for, exactly as it must use a builder that can see the local store. **That threading is now gone, and so is the row's original failure mode**: closing [#595](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/595) removed the secondary builds altogether, so there is no longer a second `FROM` that could resolve the wrong platform. The row keeps its scenarios and its claim — a `--platform` build still has to produce a correctly-platformed image carrying its metadata — but what backs it is now the absence of the chain rather than care taken with it. Shares a root with [#595](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/595), which is the same chained `FROM` seen from the other side and remains open. Two scenarios: `case-build-upstream-platform-image-config` (`live-differential`) and `case-build-platform-image-config-metadata` (`spec-expectation`, `docker-shared`), the latter asserting the `devcontainer.metadata` label rather than the architecture — the label is the OUTPUT of the pass that was broken, so it disappears if the pass regresses, whereas `chan-image` does not carry the architecture and growing the channel to reach a fixture is what the suite's own rule warns against. **Both are ARCH-RELATIVE and vacuous wherever the requested platform is native**, written `linux/arm64` because they gate on x86_64. |
| `build --output-format json` prints a result document on stdout for a FAILURE as well as a success. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594). MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 across four failure classes — a Dockerfile `RUN` that exits non-zero, an image reference no registry serves, an unparseable `devcontainer-lock.json`, and a workspace with no `devcontainer.json`. Both CLIs exit 1 in all four; BEFORE the fix the reference printed `{"outcome":"error","message":…}` on stdout every time and deacon printed NOTHING, having written a perfectly good diagnostic to stderr. The gap was the document, not the information. **It was an inconsistency inside deacon rather than a design choice**, which is what made the fix small: `deacon up` already printed the document on a missing config, and `build`'s own PRE-FLIGHT refusals printed it too — so `build` had the document, used it for the failures it detected before doing any work, and lost it for every failure that happened afterwards, which is the half a caller cannot route around. `execute_build` is now a wrapper that renders one document for any failure that has not already written its own; the four refusals keep composing their richer one (each names what to do about it in `description`) and mark it, because two documents on stdout would break the output contract as surely as zero did. The marker carries its own message rather than wrapping one in an `anyhow` context, so TEXT mode is byte-identical to before — a context would have printed `Caused by: result document already written` under an otherwise clean diagnostic. JSON mode only: in text mode the diagnostic is already on stderr and the binary's top-level handler renders the chain. `case-build-json-error-document-on-failure` deliberately uses a failure detected while doing WORK (an unreadable lockfile) rather than a pre-flight refusal, since a case built on a refusal would have passed against the defect; it was WATCHED TO FAIL by suppressing the document, which turns `chan-structured-output` and `chan-stdout` red together. |
| A Compose configuration REFUSES the image-only build flags (`--platform`, `--cache-to`) rather than accepting and ignoring them, and writes nothing on the way out. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | The platform belongs to a single image build and a Compose project builds whatever its services declare, so there is nothing for either flag to mean. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-compose`: both CLIs exit 1 and both print an error document; the WORDING differs (`--platform or --push not supported.` against deacon's `Cannot use --platform with Docker Compose configurations`), which is why the two cases are `spec-expectation` and pin deacon's own document — a differential would report a message-text disagreement forever and need a tolerance to say nothing more than that they phrase it differently. deacon's wording names the flag AND the configuration shape that cannot take it; the reference's names two flags, only one of which was passed. Kept as two cases because the flags are refused by different code and a fix wiring only one would leave the other silently accepted — upstream splits them for the same reason. Both are `docker-shared`, and the lane was CORRECTED before merge in a way worth keeping: they were authored HERMETIC on the reasoning that a refusal about the configuration's SHAPE cannot need a daemon, and verified here by running each with `DOCKER_HOST` pointed at a nonexistent socket and again with `docker` off `PATH`. Both probes were worthless, because this machine has a working daemon and neither one stopped deacon's BuildKit-availability probe from answering. The macOS and Windows `dev-fast` runners, which have no daemon at all, said so: there deacon answers `BuildKit is required for --platform` and never reaches the Compose refusal, because it probes BuildKit BEFORE it looks at the configuration shape. **A check that runs before another check is a prerequisite, whatever it is about** — and a hermetic verification performed on a machine that has the resource proves nothing. That ordering is also why #592 exists: the probe asks whether BuildKit is AVAILABLE, not whether the user disabled it, so `--buildkit never` sails past it. `--cache-to` additionally asserts the destination directory is ABSENT afterwards — refusing and writing nothing are two claims and only one of them is an exit code. Mined from the reference's e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 5). |
| `--push` and `--output` are refused together as mutually exclusive, before anything is built and without writing the export. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Both flags are destinations for the built image — one hands it to a registry, the other writes it to a path — so taking both would mean silently picking one. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-flags`: both CLIs exit 1 before building and neither writes the export; deacon says `Cannot use both --push and --output`, the reference `--push true cannot be used with --output.` The `chan-filesystem` assertion carries the half the exit code cannot — a CLI that validated the combination only after starting the export would satisfy the refusal and fail the cleanliness. HERMETIC: pure argument validation, verified with `DOCKER_HOST` pointed at a nonexistent socket. Mined from the reference's e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 5). |
| `build` on a workspace with no `devcontainer.json` exits 1 and names the file it looked for. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Nothing covered this for `build`: the existing no-config cases belong to `read-configuration` (two), `doctor` and `down`, and `build` reaches config resolution by its own path. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-errors-missing-config`: both CLIs exit 1 and both name the absolute path of the document they looked for. WHERE they say it differs — the reference on stdout as a result document, deacon on stderr — and that difference is [#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594), carried by its own row; this case asserts only the half that is correct today, so it is green from the day it lands and stays green after the JSON gap closes. HERMETIC, and the lane is a claim rather than a convenience: config resolution fails before any daemon contact, so a regression that contacted the daemon first would fail here rather than pass. Mined from the reference's e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 5). |
| `--image-name` is repeatable: every name becomes a tag on the one built image, and the result document lists them in the order they were passed. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Every other `build` case in the suite passes the flag exactly once, so the repeatable half was carried by the argument parser alone. The tag ORDER is the claim and it needs `jsonEquals` to express — `jsonSubset` treats an array as an order-insensitive contains, so a subset assertion would pass on a CLI that reversed the list; the document has exactly two keys, which is what makes equality on the whole of it possible. The tokenized `${IMAGE_TAG}` is deliberately the SECOND name: `chan-image` inspects only the tag the runner issued, so putting it last is what proves the later names are real tags on a real image rather than echoes in the result document — the exact failure this project has shipped once before, where `--image-name` was reported correctly and pointed at the pre-Feature base. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-flags`: both CLIs exit 0, both report the two names in order, and `docker images` shows one tag per name. Mined from the reference's e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 5). |
| A `--cache-from` reference that cannot be resolved is a cache MISS, not an error: the build proceeds and produces the image. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The shape CI actually runs — a pipeline names the cache image for the target branch, and on a first build for a new branch that image does not exist yet. Upstream's own comment on the test says exactly that ("example scenario: CI builds for PRs"), which is what makes this worth a row rather than filing under "flag accepted". The reference `ghcr.io/devcontainers/notFound` is kept VERBATIM from upstream and deliberately not repinned: the claim is about a name that cannot resolve, so a name that resolves would destroy it, and unlike a Feature reference there is no third-party CONTENT here to go stale — the only dependency is the repository continuing not to exist. `chan-image` is what stops the case passing vacuously: exit 0 and `outcome: success` are also what a CLI that silently skipped the build would print, so the assertion reads the Dockerfile's own label off the produced image. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-flags`: both CLIs exit 0 and both produce the image. Mined from the reference's e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 5). |
| With no `--workspace-folder`, `build` resolves the configuration from the current directory. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The shape a developer types — `cd` into the project, run `deacon build` — and every other `build` case passes the flag explicitly, so nothing covered it. It costs nothing to express: the runner already invokes each operation with its cwd set to the case's isolated workspace, so OMITTING the flag is the whole test. That is also the case's one fragility and it is invisible in the argv — if the runner ever stops anchoring cwd to the workspace, the case starts failing for a reason that has nothing to do with `build`; `case-exec-default-workspace-folder` (batch 4) carries the same note for the same reason. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-flags`: both CLIs exit 0 and build from the cwd's configuration. The IMAGE NAMES differ by construction and are not compared — deacon derives `deacon-build:<hash>`, the reference `vsc-<folder>-<hash>`. Mined from the reference's e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 5). |
| `build` on a Compose configuration with NO Features honours every `--image-name`: each becomes a tag on the built service image, and the result document lists them in the order they were passed. | matches the reference | no scenario — neither oracle type can express it; 1 Docker-gated test | Fixes [#619](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/619). The THIRD arm of an upstream trio and the one that was left. Batch 5 mined 'should succeed with multiple --image-name parameters …' over the Dockerfile and image-reference shapes (`bhv-build-multiple-image-names`); the `dockerComposeFile is present` arm went unmined, and it is the arm that fails. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-devport-userenvprobe`, brand-new workspace, both tags removed first: the reference reports `["first","second"]` and `docker image inspect` finds both; deacon reports `["second"]` — only the LAST name — and creates NEITHER tag, leaving only its own deterministic `deacon_<project>-test:latest`. Exit 0 and `outcome: success` on both sides, so nothing reading the outcome sees it. SCOPED by measurement rather than by reading: Compose WITH Features reports both names and creates both tags on the same daemon in the same session, because `resolve_compose_feature_image` retags the image it produces — nothing retags when there are no Features to force that rebuild. **NO SCENARIO, and the reason is worth recording because it is a property of the harness rather than a shortage of effort — the [#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594) and [#608](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/608) precedent, reached here for two independent reasons.** A `spec-expectation` case would be red in `parity_docker`, a blocking PR lane. A `live-differential` cannot express it either: (1) the claim lives in the result document, and the REFERENCE CLI's `build` has no `--output-format` flag at all — it exits 1 with `Unknown arguments: output-format, outputFormat` — while deacon prints human text without it, so `chan-structured-output` cannot be compared on this subcommand by any argv (no existing differential `build` case declares that channel, for exactly this reason); and (2) naming `${IMAGE_TAG}` to reach `chan-image` instead turns the defect into a fail-loud `HarnessError` — the runner treats a missing image after a SUCCESSFUL build as a broken observation, which is the right judgement and is precisely this defect, but it ABORTS the whole `docker-shared` group and took forty sibling cases down with it when tried. Measured, both times. Another fixture batch 1 vendored from an execution test and only ever parsed. Mined from the reference's e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 8). **THE FIX, and it is one call the path never made.** `execute_compose_build` ran `docker compose build` and then reported `args.image_names` verbatim as `tags` without tagging anything, so the names were an ECHO of the request rather than a record of the daemon's state — and because the first entry of `tags` is the deterministic tag on every other build path, `output_result`'s unconditional `tags[1..]` then removed one of the user's own names, which is the whole of the second symptom. It now resolves the reference Compose tagged the result with — the service's own `image:` when authored, else Compose v2's `<project>-<service>` default, read back through the new `ComposeManager::resolve_service_image_name` rather than assumed — puts THAT at `tags[0]`, and `retag_image`s every `--image-name` off it, which is the same helper and the same shape the Compose-WITH-Features sibling already used. This is also the reference's own shape: its compose branch resolves `overrideImageName || service.image || <project>-<service>` and issues one `docker tag` per name. MEASURED after the fix at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-devport-userenvprobe`, brand-new workspaces on both sides: deacon `["first","second"]` / both tags present / both resolving to ONE image id, reference `["first","second"]` / both present — and the `image:`-authored variant agrees on both sides too, tagging off the authored name. **NO differential scenario, and the row's own reasoning above is why** — nothing about the harness changed, so the assertion lands as `integration_compose_features_build::build_compose_without_features_tags_every_image_name`, which asserts at the ARTIFACT level (both names inspectable, same image id, and the base layer's marker readable out of the tagged image) because a JSON `outcome` is exactly what stayed green while no tag existed. Watched to fail: reverted, it reports `["…second:v1"]` and neither tag. **Its sibling was found VACUOUS in the same commit** and is repaired here: `build_compose_with_features_tags_final_image` — the guard CLAUDE.md cites for the with-Features retag — spelled `dockerComposeFile` workspace-relative where it resolves against the config dir, so `deacon build` failed on a missing compose file and the test's tolerant `stderr.contains("Docker")` early-return read that as "no daemon" and passed in 0.3s. Both tests now assert `status.success()` outright, Docker availability already being gated at the top. **One pinned case moved with the fix, and it was a fiction being repinned rather than a regression being papered over.** `case-build-compose-service-shape` runs `fx-build-compose`, whose service is `image:`-shaped (`image: alpine:3.19`, no `build:`), and pinned the reported name as Compose's `<project>-app` default — but Compose builds NOTHING for an `image:`-only service, so no such image was ever on the daemon (measured). It now reports `alpine:3.19`, which exists and which `--image-name` can actually be tagged from — measured on that fixture, two names, both created, both at alpine's id, where the old source would have failed `docker tag` with 'No such image'. The replacement pin is the stronger one: nothing but a read of that service's entry in the resolved `docker compose config` yields `alpine:3.19`, the devcontainer.json declaring no `image` of its own. Nothing else moved — the same `parity_docker` run reports 79 of 80 `docker-shared` cases agreeing. A companion observation on the same path is NOT fixed here and is not claimed by this row: deacon's Compose image carries no `devcontainer.metadata` label when the config declares no Features, where the reference's does. Measuring the repin sharpened WHY: the reference ALWAYS runs an extended Compose build, even with no Features and even on an `image:`-only service — at 0.87.0 on `fx-build-compose` it reports and creates `vsc-<workspace>-<hash>`, a distinct image from `alpine:3.19` — and that build is both where its label comes from and why its `overrideImageName || service.image || <project>-<service>` chain never reaches its second term. deacon implements the second and third terms and not the first. A label cannot be added by retagging, and adding one needs that build-time override on the Compose invocation. |
| A `build` over a Compose configuration that declares NO Features still produces an image carrying `devcontainer.metadata`, as one that declares Features does. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios + 1 Docker-gated test | Fixes [#628](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/628), the companion observation the #619 row above recorded and deliberately did not claim. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-compose`: the reference reports and creates `vsc-<workspace>-<hash>` carrying `[ {\"remoteUser\":\"root\",\"shutdownAction\":\"stopCompose\"} ]`; deacon reported `alpine:3.19` — the PULLED BASE — with no such label anywhere. Exit 0 and `outcome: success` on both sides, so nothing reading the result document saw it, and an image a Compose `build` produced told a later `up` (or VS Code / Zed / envbuilder) nothing about the configuration it was built from. **The fix could not be a stamping pass, and that constraint chose its shape.** A label cannot be added by retagging, and adding one afterwards would mean a second build `FROM` a daemon-local tag — the chain [#595](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/595) forbids, because a `docker-container` driver builder cannot read the daemon's image store. The label therefore has to be an INPUT to the build, which is what the reference's own mechanism does and what deacon now mirrors: **Compose still drives the build, and deacon layers ONE inline override over the project's own files** (`ComposeBuildOverlay`, piped through the existing `-f -` path that `up`'s injection override already used). Reading the reference's generated override is what settled the shape, and it does the SAME thing in two apparently different ways: on a `build:` service it overrides only `build.dockerfile`, leaving `context` and `args` to be merged from the author's file; on an `image:`-only service — which Compose builds NOTHING for — it supplies both a synthesized build and an `image:` naming the result, which is its `overrideImageName` and the reason that chain's second term is never reached. deacon's override carries `build.labels` rather than a spliced Dockerfile on the `build:` shape (measured equivalent: Compose applies `build.labels` to the produced image), and the synthesized one-`FROM` build plus `image:` on the `image:`-only shape. **Overriding rather than replacing is the whole of it, and the alternative was measured before it was rejected.** The issue's own suggested shape — reuse `resolve_compose_feature_image`, the with-Features path — would have built OUTSIDE Compose, and that path silently drops the service's `build.args`: on a service declaring `args: {MARKER: from-compose-args}`, deacon's Feature-extended image reads `unset` where the reference's reads `from-compose-args`. Adopting it would have satisfied this row while extending that defect to the shape it does not yet affect, so it was not adopted; the defect it exposed on the with-Features path is filed separately as [#629](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/629) rather than folded in here. AFTER the fix, both shapes were re-measured against the reference on fresh workspaces: on `fx-build-compose` deacon's produced image carries `[{\"remoteUser\":\"root\",\"shutdownAction\":\"stopCompose\"}]` where the reference's carries the same document (it pads arrays with cosmetic spaces); on a `build:` service the two agree on `[{\"remoteUser\":\"root\"}]` AND on `from-compose-args`; and with a base image itself carrying a label, both inherit it in the same order — deacon `[{\"remoteEnv\":{\"FROM_BASE\":\"yes\"}},{\"remoteUser\":\"root\"}]`, reference the same. That inheritance is not incidental: `resolve_compose_base_image` resolves a `build:` service's base through its Dockerfile the same way the single-container path does (`resolve_base_image`, the reference's `findBaseImage`), and returns `None` rather than failing a build when the Dockerfile cannot be read. **Two scenarios, and the reported name moved with the fix.** `case-build-compose-metadata-label-no-features` pins the label WHOLE and by value (a presence check would pass on a label recording the wrong configuration); `case-build-compose-service-shape` — same fixture — is RE-PINNED from `alpine:3.19` to `deacon-build:<12 hex>`, because deacon now reaches the first term of the reference's chain and reports what its build produced instead of what it pulled. That is the second re-pin of that case in as many fixes, and it moved for the opposite reason each time: #619 replaced a name that never existed with the pulled base, #628 replaces the pulled base with a produced image. Both were watched to fail — with the assertions broken, the `docker-shared` lane reports exactly these two cases diverging and no others. The third assertion is the Docker-gated `integration_compose_features_build::build_compose_without_features_labels_the_image_and_keeps_build_args`, which asserts BOTH halves in one test because they are one decision — reverted, it reports an empty label. It is artifact-level for the reason the whole row is: `outcome: success` is exactly what stayed green while no label existed. |
| `build --output-format json` reports `imageName` as an ARRAY on every configuration shape and arity but one: a Compose configuration built with no `--image-name` reports the produced image as a BARE STRING. | matches the reference | 1 scenario + 3 hermetic tests | Fixes [#632](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/632), which is [#310](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/310) re-measured rather than reversed. #310 found deacon collapsing a single tag to a bare string, concluded "the reference CLI always emits an array", and made deacon's unconditional. That conclusion was drawn from the image-reference shape, and it is right for EIGHT of the nine cells. **The full matrix, measured at oracle 0.87.0** — three config shapes (image reference, Dockerfile, Compose) × three `--image-name` arities (none, one, two), both CLIs, eighteen runs. With `--image-name`, every shape on both sides reports an array of exactly the names given. Without it, the reference reports `["vsc-<workspace>-<hash>"]` for the image-reference and Dockerfile shapes and `"<project>-<service>"` — a bare string — for Compose. deacon reported an array in all nine, so exactly one cell diverged, and only in SHAPE: the two VALUES on that cell are already the same thing, each CLI's own Compose default name, differing by the project-name prefix that `bhv-compose-project-name-robust` already records. **TRANSCRIBED for the cause, which is why the exception is not arbitrary.** In `dist/spec-node/devContainersSpecCLI.js` the build command sets `IA = g && (Array.isArray(g) ? g : [g]) || void 0` from `--image-name` and then branches three ways, each ending `IA ? oA = IA : oA = <computed>`. The Dockerfile and image-reference branches take `<computed>` from their `updatedImageName`, which those functions build as an array (`{updatedImageName:[t]}`, `{updatedImageName: i.length>0 ? i : [c]}`); the Compose branch computes `uA = overrideImageName || service.image || <project>-<service>` — a plain string it never routes through `updatedImageName`. The bare string is a consequence of which function names the image, not a special case anyone wrote. **Adjudicated by the standing authority rule.** `imageName` appears nowhere in `parity/spec/113500f4/`, so this is a spec-silent output surface, the reference is the authority, and the difference is deacon's. Worth recording that this cuts AGAINST the better API: a bare string is the worse shape and consumers must handle both. They already must — the reference emits both, and deacon's own build CLI contract documents `imageName` as `oneOf: string | array` — and parity is the rule the project chose, so the row follows the measurement rather than the preference. **The fix is one branch and one type.** `ImageNameShape::resolve(uses_compose, image_names)` turns the two facts the reference's own branch turns on into the spelling, and `output_result` consults it; both call sites pass it, including the build-cache-hit path, because a cache hit must report the document a fresh build would. `ImageNameOutput::Single` was already present — #310 kept it for deserialization tolerance — so nothing new had to be modelled. **Re-measured after the fix**: all nine cells agree in shape, and `case-build-compose-service-shape` is RE-PINNED a third time — #619 changed the name it asserts, #628 changed it again, #632 changes the shape around it — from `"imageName":["deacon-build:<12 hex>"]` to `"imageName":"deacon-build:<12 hex>"`. The regex distinguishes the two: `\s*` cannot cross the `[`. Watched to fail with the old array pin against the fixed code — exactly that case diverges on `chan-stdout` and no other. Its sibling `case-build-compose-feature-metadata-label` covers the other Compose cell (with `--image-name`, both an array), so the branch is pinned on both sides. The three hermetic tests are the nine-cell matrix as a table (`image_name_shape_is_a_bare_string_only_for_compose_without_a_named_image`) and the two serializer tests, which now assert the array and bare-string spellings as a pair rather than the array alone. |
| A Feature-extended Compose build applies every `build:` key the compose author wrote — `args`, `labels` and `target` — to the image it produces. | matches the reference | 1 scenario + 1 Docker-gated test | Fixes [#629](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/629), found while fixing #628 and filed apart from it because the root cause and the affected path are both different: #628 was the Features-FREE Compose build carrying no `devcontainer.metadata`; this was the Features-DECLARING one silently discarding what the compose author wrote. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on a `build:` service declaring `args: {MARKER: from-compose-args}` over a Dockerfile whose `ARG MARKER=unset` records what it received: the reference's produced image read `from-compose-args`, deacon's read `unset` — from `build` AND from the image `up`'s container actually runs, both sides exiting 0 with `outcome: success` throughout, so only the artifact showed it. `up` shared it because the `build:` arm of `resolve_compose_feature_image` is caller-independent. **The cause was a comment that stopped being true.** That arm passed `&[]` for `extra_build_args` under the note that \"a compose service's `build.args` are applied by compose itself when it renders the service\" — which held when the service was built by `docker compose build`, and did not on a path where Compose does not run the build. `extract_service_shape` never surfaced `args`, so they were not unused so much as unread; `build.labels` and every other `build:` key went the same way, `args` being merely the one with a visible effect. **The fix is a mechanism change, not an enumeration**, and that choice is the whole row. Forwarding `args` would have fixed `args`; the next `build:` key nobody thought of would have been discovered the same way, by measurement, years later. So this path moved onto the mechanism #628 built for its sibling: **Compose drives the build and deacon layers ONE inline override naming only what it adds** — the merged base + Feature Dockerfile as `build.dockerfile`, the stage to stop at as `build.target`, and the Feature staging directories as `additional_contexts`. Everything the author declared keeps applying because Compose still resolves the service. READ FROM THE REFERENCE, not inferred: its generated override for this exact shape names `dockerfile`, `args` (its own four `_DEV_CONTAINERS_*`), and `additional_contexts`, and nothing else — deacon needs no `args` of its own because its generated `FROM` is literal rather than `ARG`-parameterized. **Three things came free with the shape, and one was a latent second defect.** `build.labels` now survive; the produced image is the one COMPOSE names (the service's authored `image:`, else `<project>-<service>`) rather than a deacon-private `deacon-devcontainer-features:<hash>`, which is what the reference reports too; and `build.target` is now HONORED. That last one was wrong before in a way nobody had filed: the old arm called `ensure_dockerfile_has_final_stage_name` and logged a debug line saying it was installing Features on the final stage instead of the requested target. It now calls `base_stage_for_features`, the same helper the single-container path uses, so a service declaring `target: middle` gets its Features on `middle`. Measured against the reference on a three-stage Dockerfile: both produce `middle`. **Cache and platform settings ride the document** (`cache_from`, `cache_to`, `platforms`) because `docker compose build` has no flag for them, while `--no-cache` and `--builder`, which it does have, ride the invocation. Dropping either half would have disabled those options on this path alone, silently. **One scenario, and it asserts the artifact.** `case-build-compose-feature-build-shape-keeps-build-keys` pins three labels on the produced image, each failing independently: `parity.build.arg`, which the fixture's Dockerfile writes as `LABEL parity.build.arg=$PARITY_BUILD_ARG` so the value records whether the service's `build.args` reached the build at all; `parity.authored.label`, the author's own `build.labels` entry; and `devcontainer.metadata`, so a \"fix\" that preserved the author's keys by not installing Features would fail here rather than pass quietly. A build arg is otherwise invisible to every declared channel — `chan-image` captures image config, not the files a `RUN` wrote — and making it observable as a label is what let this be a data-only case. Watched to fail: with the two label values inverted, the `docker-shared` lane reports exactly this case diverging on `chan-image` and no others out of 82. The second assertion is the Docker-gated `integration_compose_features_build::build_compose_with_features_keeps_the_services_own_build_keys`, which adds `target` and the Feature marker to the same four-way check; run against the pre-fix code it reports `arg-was-dropped\\nlast\\ninstalled` — the arg dropped AND the wrong stage built, both defects in one line. AFTER the fix, re-measured against the reference on two fresh workspaces: on the `args`-only shape both images carry `from-compose-args` and `feature-installed`; on the three-stage shape with `target`, `args`, `labels` and a context `COPY`, both carry all four (`from-compose-args`, `context-file-reached`, `middle`, `feature-installed`) and the same `devcontainer.metadata` document. |
| The image a `build` produces carries the CONFIGURATION's own `containerEnv`, and the configuration's value wins where a Feature declares the same name. | matches the reference | 1 scenario + 3 unit tests | Fixes [#641](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/641), mined from the reference's own e2e suite ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 10, `src/test/cli.build.test.ts` at v0.87.0, 'should follow the correct merge logic for containerEnv'). MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on a configuration declaring one Feature, where one variable is declared by BOTH: the reference's image carries `PARITY_OVERRIDDEN=from-config`, `PARITY_CONFIG_ONLY=from-config` and the Feature's own `PARITY_FEATURE_ONLY=from-feature`; deacon's carried the Feature's two and NEITHER of the configuration's — and reported `PARITY_OVERRIDDEN=from-feature`, so the artifact did not merely omit a variable, it CONTRADICTED the document that built it. Both CLIs exited 0, both printed `{\"outcome\":\"success\"}` and both wrote an identical `devcontainer.metadata` label, so the configuration was resolved and merged correctly all along and nothing reading the result document could see the difference — the same shape as [#628](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/628), and for the same reason: what `build` DELIVERS is an image, and an image handed to CI, a registry or any tool that has never heard of deacon carries only what is baked into it. **The scope was measured before the fix was sized, and it is narrower than the issue title suggests.** A configuration declaring NO Features bakes nothing on EITHER CLI — checked on both an `image:` and a `build.dockerfile` shape, where the two agree — because the reference's emission lives in its Feature-install Dockerfile template and no Features means no template. `up` was never affected: it applies `containerEnv` as `--env` at create time, and both CLIs already answered `OVERRIDDEN=from-config CONFIG=from-config FEATURE=from-feature` inside a running container. **Two details of the reference's shape are load-bearing and both are mirrored.** The configuration's `ENV` lines come LAST, after every Feature's install `RUN` and after every Feature's own `containerEnv` — that ordering IS the merge rule, and a fix that emitted them anywhere else would satisfy the missing-variable half while leaving the contradiction. And the configuration's values escape `$` where a Feature's deliberately do not: a Feature's `PATH=\"/usr/local/share/nvm/current/bin:${PATH}\"` must still go through Docker's own `ENV` expansion, while a configuration's `value with $dollar sign` is meant literally and expands to `value with ` without the escape. deacon had emitted the Feature half since the `npm: command not found` fix and had never emitted the other. The emission lives in `DockerfileGenerator` and is wired into BOTH entry points — `generate` (the `image:` shape `up` and Compose use) and `generate_install_stage_from` (the merged single-build shape `build` uses) — because the reference has ONE template for every shape and covering only the one `build` happens to take would have left `up` silently divergent. `case-build-upstream-container-env-merge` pins all seven entries and was watched to fail: with the fix reverted it is the ONLY diverging case out of 84 in the `docker-shared` tier, and its first mismatch is `env[1]` — the overridden variable, not the missing one. After the fix the two CLIs' `Config.Env` arrays are identical entry for entry and in the same order. The three unit tests cover what the scenario cannot see: the ordering on both entry points, the escaping of each awkward value against the Feature form, and that a configuration with no `containerEnv` produces a byte-identical Dockerfile to the one it produced before this existed. |
| `build --config` acts on the document it names rather than the one discovery would find, and resolves that document's `build.dockerfile` against the CONFIG's directory rather than the workspace folder. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 10 (`src/test/cli.build.test.ts` at v0.87.0, 'should build with config in subfolder'). The second half is what makes it worth a case: `fx-upstream-subfolder-config` carries TWO `Dockerfile`s and BOTH documents say `\"dockerfile\": \"Dockerfile\"`, so a workspace-relative resolution reaches one and a config-relative resolution reaches the other — the same config-dir-versus-workspace-dir distinction `ComposeManager::create_project` is built on, on a surface where nothing had asserted it for `build`. Only the subfolder's Dockerfile writes `SUBFOLDER_CONFIG_IMAGE_ENV`, so reading the wrong document, or the right document against the wrong base, fails the assertion; a single-document fixture would have passed all three ways. The fixture was already vendored for the `up` and `run-user-commands` arms of the same upstream shape, so this cost no new fixture — `build` was the third subcommand reading it and the only one with no case. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: both CLIs exit 0 and both produce an image carrying `SUBFOLDER_CONFIG_IMAGE_ENV=true` and nothing from the root document. Asserted by `case-build-upstream-config-selects-document`. |
| `build` with no `--workspace-folder` and no devcontainer configuration in the current directory exits 1 rather than building something arbitrary. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The NEGATIVE arm of `build`'s cwd default, and upstream's own ('should fail gracefully when no workspace-folder and no config in current directory', `src/test/cli.build.test.ts` at v0.87.0), mined in [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 10. The positive arm has had a case since batch 8 and the negative had none — the same asymmetry batch 6 closed for `exec` and batch 9 for `run-user-commands`, and `build` was the third and last consumer subcommand with a cwd default, so the family is now closed on both arms everywhere. Distinct from `bhv-build-missing-config-rejected`, which names a `--workspace-folder` explicitly: this one never passes the flag, so it also asserts that the default resolves to the cwd before failing. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 in an empty directory: both CLIs exit 1 and both name the same absolute path of the document they looked for, deacon as `Configuration file not found: <abs>` and the reference as `Dev container config (<abs>) not found.` `case-build-upstream-no-config-in-cwd-rejected` pins deacon's own answer and only the SHAPE of the document (`{\"outcome\": \"error\"}`), leaving the wording free — a case that froze the sentence would have to be edited by anyone improving it. HERMETIC, and verified rather than assumed: re-run with `docker` removed from `PATH` entirely AND `DOCKER_HOST` pointed at a nonexistent socket, deacon produces the identical document on all three channels, so config resolution demonstrably fails before any daemon contact. It therefore gates every pull request and runs on all three operating systems. |
| `build --label` applies every label it is given to the produced image on EVERY configuration shape; the reference CLI honours the flag on image-reference configurations and silently drops it on `build.dockerfile` ones. | documented choice | 2 scenarios | Mined in [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 10 — upstream's own `--label` test ('should build successfully with valid image metadata --label property', `src/test/cli.build.test.ts` at v0.87.0) uses an IMAGE configuration, so it never touches the shape where its own CLI loses them. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 across all four `build` shapes on fresh workspaces, passing `--label name=label-test --label type=multiple-labels` to each: image-reference and image-reference-with-Features carry both labels on BOTH CLIs; `build.dockerfile` and `build.dockerfile`-with-Features carry both on deacon and NEITHER on the reference. **What narrows this from a guess to a claim** is that the reference's Feature-extended Dockerfile image still carries `devcontainer.metadata`, and that the SAME Dockerfile shape forwards `build.options`-supplied `--label` arguments — asserted, green and untoleranced by `case-build-upstream-target-and-options`. So it is the FLAG's values specifically that are lost on that shape, not labelling, and not that shape's build. **The standing rule on a spec-silent output surface is that the reference is the authority, and this row says out loud why it is not being applied.** There is no answer to match here, only an inconsistency to pick a side of: one documented flag, described by the reference's own `--help` as additional image labels with no shape qualification, reaching the image on one configuration shape and not another. Matching would mean making a documented flag silently do nothing on the commonest configuration shape there is — the silent-fallback class constitution IV rules out — so deacon keeps its behavior and the difference is characterized instead. **ADJUDICATED 2026-08-24: deacon keeps its behavior.** The ruling turns on what the authority rule is FOR — it resolves *which of two answers is correct* on a surface the spec does not describe, by deferring to the implementation everyone else targets, and it presupposes the reference HAS an answer. Here it has two, on the same flag, chosen by configuration shape, with nothing in its own `--help` predicting the split; deferring anyway would copy an inconsistency rather than a behavior. Constitution IV settles what is left. The tolerance stays self-invalidating, which is what makes the ruling cheap to revisit: if upstream ever forwards the flag on this shape the record fails as STALE, and that failure is the signal to reopen it. **Two scenarios, split by what each is for.** `case-build-cli-label-dockerfile-shape-spec` pins deacon's own answer and GATES every pull request — it is also the first case anywhere to pass `--label` twice, which is the half of upstream's assertion nothing had covered (the image shape has been gated since `case-build-image-reference-custom-tag`, which passes it once). `case-build-upstream-cli-label-dockerfile-shape` is the differential that re-measures the divergence every nightly run, with a tolerance naming the two label KEYS rather than `chan-image.labels`, so the fixture's own Dockerfile `LABEL` stays compared and agrees. That tolerance is verified CONSUMED rather than assumed: the run reports `allowed-difference` naming all three paths. Being self-invalidating is the point — if upstream ever forwards the flag on this shape, the record fails as stale instead of quietly outliving the difference it describes. |
| A second `build` over an unchanged workspace still tags the image with the `--image-name` THIS invocation passed. | matches the reference | no differential scenario — not expressible today; asserted by `test_build_cache_hit_applies_a_changed_image_name` | Was an open nonconformance ([#620](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/620)). `--image-name` is an OUTPUT of the build, not an input to its identity — it deliberately does not enter `config_hash` — so caching the build was right and caching the TAGGING was the defect. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-build-flags`, one workspace, two builds, different names: the reference reported and created each name in turn; deacon's second build reported the FIRST invocation's name and created no tag for the second, so `docker inspect --type=image <second name>` failed against an `outcome: success` document that claimed otherwise. Found while measuring [#619](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/619) and filed apart from it because the root causes differ — this one reproduced on the plain Dockerfile shape, where a cold build tags correctly — though they compound, and #619's first symptom was observed through this one. **The fix reconciles rather than rebuilds**: a hit retags the cached image id with every name the CURRENT invocation passed and reports exactly those, keeping the tags the cached build OWNS (deacon's deterministic `deacon-build:<hash>`, Compose's derived `<project>-<service>`) and dropping the names a previous run merely requested — which needs the cache entry to record what WAS requested, so `BuildMetadata` now carries `requested_image_names` (`#[serde(default)]`, so pre-fix entries still load and degrade to treating every recorded tag as build-owned). Earlier tags are left on the daemon; the reference leaves them too. RE-MEASURED on the fix across the shapes the cache actually serves: Dockerfile and image-reference hit it and now report and create the current name; Compose with a `build:` service hits it and creates the new tag; Compose with a plain `image:` service never populates a usable cache at all, because its recorded image id names an image `docker compose build` does not produce, so the entry is invalidated on every run — pre-existing and orthogonal. **NO DIFFERENTIAL SCENARIO, stated rather than implied**: every case runs in a fresh `DockerWorkspace` and `${IMAGE_TAG}` resolves to one per-side tag per case, so 'build twice with two different names' still has no vocabulary in the operation model; building that vocabulary stays batch-9 material on [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480). Per this row's own wording the assertion lands with the fix instead, as the Docker-gated `test_build_cache_hit_applies_a_changed_image_name` — verified to FAIL on the pre-fix behavior — which asserts at the artifact level (the new tag resolves to the image carrying the Dockerfile's marker file, and to the same image id as the first name) rather than on the result document alone. |
| A `--workspace-folder` naming a directory that does not exist is reported as an unresolvable WORKSPACE, on every subcommand that takes the flag — not as a missing configuration FILE. | matches the reference | 1 scenario + 2 hermetic tests | Fixes [#644](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/644). The observable contract already matched and still does — both CLIs exit 1 with an `outcome: error` document — so what this row records is a diagnostic that was **false**, on four of deacon's six subcommands, for as long as they have had the flag. MEASURED at `dec6d19` with `--workspace-folder /tmp/definitely-not-a-real-workspace`: `up` and `exec` said `Failed to resolve workspace path '<dir>': path does not exist or cannot be accessed`, while `read-configuration`, `run-user-commands`, `build` and `down` said `Configuration file not found: <dir>` — naming a directory as a configuration file, and naming a path that had never been searched for, because when the directory is absent no document is ever looked for. **Not a wording preference, and the project had already ruled on the wording**: `case-up-upstream-missing-workspace-rejected`'s note adjudicated deacon's sentence as the more accurate of the two, on the ground that the reference names a file it never looked for. The four printed a third sentence that shares the reference's flaw without naming the file. **An inconsistency inside deacon rather than a design choice**, which is what made the fix small and is the same shape as [#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594), [#615](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/615) and [#635](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/635): `up` and `exec` validate the workspace up front through `shared::workspace::resolve_workspace_folder`; the other four reach `ConfigLoader::discover_config`, whose does-the-workspace-exist guard reused `ConfigError::NotFound` — the variant that names a missing config FILE at its five other call sites. It now has its own `ConfigError::WorkspaceNotFound`, whose Display IS the sentence `up` and `exec` already printed, so one condition has one wording across all six and the older variant goes back to meaning only what its name says. The compiler found every match arm that had to learn the new variant — one, in `up`'s result renderer. **Deliberately invisible to every lane before the fix**, which is why it survived: each negative case here pins exit 1 and `{"outcome": "error"}` and nothing about the wording (batch 9 said so explicitly, to leave this closable without a suite edit), and both CLIs exit 1 with an error document on this input, so no differential could see it either — the same blind spot as [#628](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/628) and [#641](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/641), a wrong artifact behind a correct-looking result document. Re-measured after the fix: all six print the accurate sentence, and the EXISTING-workspace-with-no-config path is byte-unchanged on all six (`Configuration file not found: <ws>/.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`), which is `ConfigError::NotFound` used as intended and is what the two conditions being distinct now means. `case-build-missing-workspace-rejected` is the scenario, hermetic, and it also closes a family: `exec` (batch 4), `up` (batch 7) and `run-user-commands` (batch 9) each had this arm and `build` did not, though it had the other two negatives. Its `run-user-commands` sibling was TIGHTENED in the same commit with the `chan-stderr` assertion its own note had promised. Both were WATCHED TO FAIL against the parent guard: exactly those two cases diverge, both on `chan-stderr` and on no other channel. The two hermetic tests are in `crates/core/src/config.rs` and assert the rendered sentences rather than only the variant, because the sentence is the whole of what a developer sees. `read-configuration` and `down` share the same guard and are covered by those tests rather than by cases of their own — a choice, not an oversight: one guard, and two cases already pin it end-to-end. |
| The disallowed-Features gate runs on `build` as well as `up`, refusing before any daemon work. | deacon extension | 1 scenario + 1 hermetic test | Fixes one arm of [#675](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/675). `build` had no gate at all, so a Feature an operator refused on `up` was installable by building the same configuration — the bypass needed no flag, just a different subcommand. The reference gates both paths (`configContainer.ts:64` for `up`, `devContainersSpecCLI.ts:680` for `build`) from a manifest deacon does not consult; whether it should is [#676](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/676), unruled. | `deacon config substitute` previews variable substitution over a configuration without applying it; the reference CLI has no such subcommand. | deacon extension | no scenario — see notes | A deacon-only diagnostic surface. `devcontainer --help` at 0.87.0 advertises `up`, `set-up`, `build`, `run-user-commands`, `read-configuration`, `outdated`, `upgrade`, `features`, `templates` and `exec`, and nothing that previews substitution — the nearest thing is `read-configuration`, which substitutes as a side effect of printing the resolved document rather than as the operation itself. So there is no reference behavior to compare and no differential to run; the record exists because the LEDGER owes a row for every surface deacon exposes, not because the behavior is in question. Recorded when `deacon --help` was diffed against `devcontainer --help` while mining `cli.test` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 25) and this subcommand turned out to have no section, no row and no mention, while every sibling extension — `down`, `doctor`, host-CA injection, user profiles — had one. **The gap was invisible to the existing ledger guards by construction:** they cross-check the Summary against the file's own row census, so they compare the ledger to itself and cannot see a surface that was never written down. `ledger_covers_every_subcommand` now closes that class — it reads `deacon --help` and fails when an advertised subcommand has no section, with a short justified exemption list for the ones that legitimately owe none. |

### `down`

| A `down` that races another removal of the same container leaves it gone and reports truthfully; a container merely being removed is not treated as one that is already absent. | deacon extension | 1 Docker-gated test + 1 unit test | The reference CLI has no `down`, so this is deacon's own contract, and it was broken in two opposite directions ([#688](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/688)). `down --remove` propagated the daemon's `already in progress` and exited **1** with the container still present; `down --all --remove` classified that same message as "already gone" and exited **0** with the container still present — a teardown reporting completion over a container that was still there, which is the worse half because nothing in the output disagrees with it. Plain `down` was never affected and is **not** a defect: it stops without removing, per the default `shutdownAction: stopContainer`. That distinction was measured, not assumed — the issue as first filed claimed plain `down` was broken, and re-measuring showed a racing `docker rm -f` had removed the container while deacon behaved correctly. The fix waits the removal out one layer down (`CliDocker::run_removal`) and splits the predicate in two: `is_already_gone` (only `no such container`) governs the REMOVE step, while `stopping_is_moot` additionally tolerates `already in progress` for the STOP step, where a container on its way out genuinely needs no stopping. Narrowing the single shared predicate fixed the removal and regressed `--all` to exit 2, which is why both exist. The Docker-gated test asserts the container's ABSENCE rather than the exit code — the `--all` shape already exited 0 while failing, so an exit-code assertion could not fail there — and it deliberately does NOT claim to cover the stop-step race, which is timing-dependent; the unit test pins that distinction deterministically. |

The risk this record exists for is a derivation that does not reproduce what `up` used — teardown then reports success and… |
| `down` over a workspace folder that has no devcontainer configuration reports that there was nothing to tear down and exits 0; it still resolves any container recorded… | deacon extension | 1 scenario | Recorded by ext-teardown-command: the spec defines no teardown command and the pinned reference exposes none, so there is no reference behavior to align with. | `down --remove` stops and removes the workspace's container, so a subsequent command targeting that container fails. | deacon extension | 4 scenarios | The containers.dev spec defines no teardown command and the pinned reference exposes none, so `down` is a deacon surface with no reference analogue — see… |

### `exec`

| Behavior | Status | Evidence | Notes |

| With `--container-id` naming a container deacon did not create and a configuration that authors no `workspaceFolder`, the working directory is the container user's HOME — not a `/workspaces/<basename>` derived from the host. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test (3 cases) | FIXED — was open nonconformance [#655](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/655). Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 13), `src/test/cli.set-up.test.ts` — the file's eight `it`s are four subcommands crossed with two ways of supplying a configuration, and every one of them targets a container the CLI did not create. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on a plain `docker run alpine:3.17` container: `exec --container-id <id> --config <cfg> pwd` printed `/root` on the reference and, on deacon, `OCI runtime exec failed: … chdir to cwd ("/workspaces/<host cwd basename>") … no such file or directory` at rc 127. **The rule was already written down in deacon's own source** — `commands/exec.rs` cites `remoteCwd = remoteWorkspaceFolder \|\| homeFolder` and its no-config branch resolves the home folder correctly. What made the second half unreachable is that `shared::workspace::resolve_container_cwd` always returned a `String`: after the mount lookup and the Compose `/` case it fell through to a host-side derivation that SYNTHESIZES a path, so `remoteWorkspaceFolder` was never absent whenever any config existed. Returning `Option` is the whole fix. The synthesized path is right for a container `up` created — deacon made that mount — which is exactly why nothing caught it: every other Docker-gated test in the crate names an `up` container. **Step 3 is now conditional on the container corroborating it**, by having a mount whose destination IS the derived path; that is what keeps a VOLUME workspace (no bind mount to match, but a mount at that destination) resolving as before, and it is asserted by its own unit test rather than reasoned about. Same failure mode as [#294](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/294)/[#295](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/295), the Compose `chdir` rc 127 that put the `/` branch there in the first place; this is a second trigger for it. The same defect ran through `run-user-commands`, where it was INVISIBLE: `postAttach` is non-blocking, so the hook failed with rc 127 and the command still printed `{"outcome":"success","result":"done"}` and exited 0. That is why the test asserts the ARTIFACT the hook writes and not the exit code — the exit code was already 0 before the fix. Evidence is `integration_container_id_foreign`, docker-shared, gating every pull request, and the three cases are a PAIR plus the silent one: home folder when nothing is authored, `/etc` when `workspaceFolder: "/etc"` is, on the same container shape. Either direction alone passes against a deacon that hard-codes one answer. **No parity case:** a case creates its container with `op-up`, which mounts the workspace — the one shape on which the old behavior was correct — the same reasoning that put [#527](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/527)'s layered branch in a test rather than in `parity/cases/`. |
| exec runs a command inside the target container and streams its stdout/stderr and propagates its exit code, matching the reference. | matches the reference | 10 scenarios | Backed by the declarative exec cases (case-exec-decl-tty, case-exec-decl-user, case-exec-decl-working-directory, case-exec-remote-env-propagation), which replaced the… EXTENDED 2026-08-16 by mining the reference's own e2e suite ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), `src/test/cli.exec.base.ts` at v0.87.0) for the two tails this row asserted generically and no scenario pinned. `case-exec-exit-code-passthrough` runs `sh -c 'exit 123'`: the row has always claimed the exit code is propagated, but every case behind it ran a command that exited 0 or 127, and 127 is the shell's own answer to a missing binary rather than a code the CLI chose to forward — so 'forwards the exact code' and 'reports failure as 1' were indistinguishable. `case-exec-remote-env-empty-value` pins `--remote-env NAME=` as SET-TO-EMPTY rather than not-set, which `printf` cannot tell apart (both print nothing) and `${NAME+present}` can; upstream asserts the same thing as `env.BAZ === ''`. Measured at oracle 0.87.0 over fx-exec-feature-path-ordering (the image… |
| `exec` with no `--workspace-folder` targets the container for the CURRENT DIRECTORY. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The shape a developer actually types — `cd` into the project, `deacon exec …` — and until this row every `exec` scenario passed the flag explicitly, so the default path was unexercised. Mined from [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) (`src/test/cli.exec.base.ts` at v0.87.0), which covers it twice: 'should exec with default workspace folder (current directory)' and the negative 'should fail gracefully when no config in current directory and no container-id'. | `exec --config` acts on the document it names rather than the one discovery would find, and that document's `remoteEnv` is what the executed command sees. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) (`src/test/cli.exec.base.ts` at v0.87.0, 'should exec with config in subfolder'). A single-document fixture would have passed all three ways. Measured at oracle 0.87.0: both CLIs print `config=[subfolder]`. This is the shape that surfaced [#584](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/584) — two documents in one workspace is what exposed the supersede sweep stopping siblings — but that fact is asserted on the container census by `case-up-sibling-config-both-running`, not here; here the second document exists so that naming one is a real choice. |
| A `--workspace-folder` that does not exist fails `exec` with exit 1. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) (`src/test/cli.exec.base.ts` at v0.87.0), which asserts `error.code === 1`. Distinct from `bhv-readconfig-malformed-jsonc-rejected`, already covered for `exec`: that one has a config which exists and will not parse, this one never reaches config loading at all. Asserted by `case-exec-missing-workspace-rejected`, the only HERMETIC `exec` case: the failure happens during workspace resolution, before any config load and before any daemon contact, so it needs nothing and runs on every profile including `dev-fast` and on all three operating systems rather than waiting for a daemon (precedent: `case-errors-decl-bad-config-path-decision`, the same shape for `read-configuration`). Verified under `unshare -rn`, and watched failing there so the lane is known to be carrying it. Both channels pin DEACON's answer rather than compare it, which is what makes the diagnostic assertable at all: the two CLIs word this differently — deacon `Failed to resolve workspace path '<abs>': path does not exist or cannot be accessed`, the reference `Dev container config (<abs>/.devcontainer/devcontainer.json) not found.` Both name the problem and both exit 1, and deacon names the directory it could not resolve rather than a file it never looked for, which is the more accurate of the two — so the wording gap is not a divergence to fix, and pinning deacon's own phrasing stops the diagnostic quietly decaying into a bare exit code. A differential here would need a tolerance to record only that the two disagree about wording. |
| `exec` streams host stdin into the container byte for byte. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) (`src/test/cli.exec.base.ts` at v0.87.0, 'stream binary data'), which builds a 256-byte buffer of every byte value and asserts `res.stdout.equals(stdin)` through `exec … cat`. The payload contains NUL and is invalid UTF-8 throughout — the shapes a lossy `String::from_utf8_lossy` would silently rewrite and a line-oriented copy would reframe. Asserted by `case-exec-binary-stdin-roundtrip` on a CHECKSUM COMPUTED IN THE CONTAINER rather than on the bytes, and that bounds what the row claims: `chan-stdout` is a normalized TEXT channel that 256 arbitrary bytes cannot survive, so `cksum` moves the evidence somewhere it can be carried — `1313719201 256` is the CRC and the length, so a corrupted byte changes the checksum and a lost or added one changes both. That covers the INBOUND half. The outbound half — container stdout back to the caller, byte-exact — is measured good (both CLIs round-trip all 256 bytes with equal `sha256`) but is NOT guarded, because asserting it needs a byte-level channel the model does not have; said out loud so the row is not read as more than it is. The checksum itself was measured rather than derived: host `cksum` and busybox `cksum` in `alpine:3.19` both answer `1313719201 256`. THIS ROW IS WHY [#586](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/586) EXISTED — the case could not be written at all until `Operation.stdinFile` replaced an inline `stdin` string that the executor declared and silently dropped, so any earlier attempt would have passed with the child reading `/dev/null`. |
| `exec` with no `--workspace-folder`, no `--container-id` and NO devcontainer config in the current directory exits 1 rather than attaching to something arbitrary. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The negative arm of the cwd default, and the loose end batch 4 of [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) named as the cheapest remaining item on either queue. Mined from `src/test/cli.exec.base.ts` at v0.87.0 ('should fail gracefully when no config in current directory and no container-id'), batch 6. Distinct from BOTH existing `exec` negatives, which is why it is not redundant: `case-exec-missing-workspace-rejected` names a path that does not exist and fails during workspace RESOLUTION before any config is looked for, and `case-exec-malformed-config-rejected` has a config that exists and will not parse. This one resolves the workspace fine, finds no document, and has no container to fall back on — the path a developer meets by `cd`-ing into the wrong directory. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 in an empty directory: both CLIs exit 1 and both print the SAME sentence, `Dev container config (<abs>/.devcontainer/devcontainer.json) not found.` Asserted by `case-exec-upstream-no-config-in-cwd-rejected`, which pins deacon's own answer rather than comparing, on `case-exec-missing-workspace-rejected`'s precedent — a differential would need the nightly lane, which gates nothing, to assert a sentence the two CLIs already word identically. HERMETIC (no `resourceGroup`), so it is the second `exec` case to gate every pull request and it runs on all three operating systems; the regex is upstream's own and is separator-agnostic because the path inside the parentheses uses backslashes on Windows. Verified hermetic by re-running with `DOCKER_HOST` pointed at a nonexistent socket and getting the identical diagnostic, and watched failing under a perturbed pattern so the lane is known to be carrying it. |
| `exec` gives the container command a terminal exactly when deacon itself has one, and passes the exit code and stdin through it. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test | Mined from [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) (`src/test/cli.exec.base.ts` at v0.87.0) — the three `shellPtyExec` arms, which were the file's last unmined ones. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on an `alpine:3.19` fixture: under a pty both CLIs pass `[ -t 1 ]`, both return 123 from `sh -c 'exit 123'`, and both echo `BARhiBAR` back from an interactive `sh` fed `FOO=BAR\necho ${FOO}hi${FOO}\nexit\n`; without a pty both pass `[ ! -t 1 ]`. **Nothing had ever proved deacon gives a command a real terminal.** `integration_exec_pty.rs` is mock-based and asserts the `tty` flag on the `ExecConfig` deacon builds — the decision, not its effect — and the one smoke test that reached for the behavior, `test_exec_tty_detection`, ran `exec test -t 0` and accepted either outcome, commenting that "both success and failure are valid". That test is replaced by this one. **A Docker-gated test rather than a scenario, ruled 2026-08-24** on #619's precedent: the operation vocabulary has no way to allocate a pty, and adding one is a design decision rather than a data edit — a real one here, because a pty makes stdout and stderr a single fd, so `chan-stdout` and `chan-stderr` stop being separable for any case that used it, and the comparison model rests on eleven separable channels. **No new dependency and no `unsafe`**: `script -qec CMD /dev/null` runs `CMD` on a pty and returns its exit status, where `openpty`+`dup2` in a `pre_exec` hook would need the `unsafe` this workspace denies. That makes the test util-linux-specific, so it is `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]` — a visible non-selection, and the Docker-bearing lanes are Linux. Each assertion was probed for teeth rather than assumed: `--no-tty` under the pty flips `[ -t 1 ]` to 1, and `[ ! -t 1 ]` under the pty fails, so neither half would survive a deacon that hard-coded the flag either way. |
