The reference derives <folder>_devcontainer verbatim, so a folder like `-myproj` yields an invalid --project-name docker compose rejects; deacon's derived name is always valid and always outside the reference's convention, so `devcontainer up` can never adopt a deacon-owned project and then die looking for a `vsc-*` image deacon never builds. The FORM changed by maintainer ruling on [#564](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/564) (2026-08-11): the workspace-folder stem now leads the two hashes. The frequency argument behind the hash-only form was overstated — there is no VS Code integration path for deacon (no extension; the Dev Containers extension bundles and drives the reference CLI, and *Attach to Running Container* does not provision), so the collision needs both tools PROVISIONING one workspace, which is a migration period rather than a steady state — while the cost was paid daily by the terminal-first audience deacon is actually for: `deacon_6fb1205c_532a7bdd-app-1` in `docker compose ps` identifies nothing without computing a hash. Both hashes stay and both are load-bearing (workspaceHash separates `~/work/api` from `~/tmp/api`; configHash is what makes an edited configuration a new generation, which [#371](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/371) and [#551](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/551) are built on). The stem is sanitized to Compose's `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*` — lowercased, disallowed characters replaced with `-`, runs collapsed, leading non-alphanumerics trimmed, truncated to 32 characters — because it is user-controlled input that reaches a `--project-name` argument; an empty result falls back to the hash-only form, which is the `-myproj` case this row was originally written for. Explicit declarations still win ahead of any derivation — `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` verbatim, a compose file's top-level `name:` as Compose interpolates it. That sentence used to name "`COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` in a sibling `.env`" as though the workspace folder's were the only one; it was the middle of three sources and the other two were unread, which is [#580](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/580) (CLOSED — see the row for it). The derivation reached here only because those sources were invisible, so this row's "when nobody said" was claiming more silence than there was. Measured live at oracle 0.87.0 on one compose workspace: reference `site_devcontainer` (volumes `site_devcontainer_app-data`, `site_devcontainer_db-data`), deacon before `deacon_742b6f14_b5e9edc0`, deacon after `deacon_site_742b6f14_b5e9edc0`. The four scenarios TOLERATE this name rather than asserting it, so the ruling moved their rationale text and nothing they check — the assertions that do exist are on the project-RELATIVE resource names, which the normalizer derives by stripping whatever project name it observed. Renaming orphans the previous project's named volumes (compose prefixes them), so `up` emits a one-time diagnostic when it finds this workspace's volumes under an older deacon project or a `<folder>_devcontainer` project: it names the project deacon will use, states that the old volumes are separate and intact, and gives the `docker compose -p <project> down -v` / volume-copy commands. Containers are already handled by `stop_superseded_containers`; volumes are deliberately never swept, which is why they need saying out loud. |

The label carries a DIGEST of an image each… |

Newly RECORDABLE rather than newly true: the retired strip_intentional_labels rule… |
| Both CLIs override the container command with a shell keep-alive that holds the container open for exec/lifecycle work and exits cleanly on SIGTERM; the command STRINGS… | documented choice | 9 scenarios | Classified as intentional ONLY because the observable behavior was measured equal, not because the difference looks cosmetic. `docker stop`: deacon 245 ms… |
| The `devcontainer.metadata` label records the image metadata the configuration and each installed Feature contribute, with configuration values kept in the form the… | matches the reference | 7 scenarios | Surfaced by 024 US5, fixed under #373. Two differences were measured at oracle 0.87.0 and both are now closed: (1) the reference records a `{"id": "<feature>"}` entry… |
| The BYTE FORM of the `devcontainer.metadata` label value — JSON whitespace — as distinct from the entries it records. | documented choice | 3 scenarios | Split out of `bhv-container-metadata-label-content` when #373 closed the CONTENT difference and left the byte form as the only thing still differing. Two causes were recorded and one is now closed: KEY ORDER was a deacon defect, not a choice — `containerEnv`/`remoteEnv` were `HashMap`, so the label's bytes changed run to run and were never the authored order. #394 made both `IndexMap`; measured by `docker inspect` across repeated `up` runs over one configuration, the label is now byte-identical and in authored order. WHAT REMAINS IS WHITESPACE ALONE: the reference writes a spaced `[ {…}, {…} ]` only when the value reaches the container through an image build, and deacon, which always writes the container label directly, always writes compact `[{…}]`. The Compose case joined it when #437 closed the CONTENT difference there too: the Feature layer forces the reference to build the service image, and a value that reaches the container through a build is written spaced. ADJUDICATED 2026-08-03 and KEPT on that whitespace residual, which is now the record's whole claim. The ruling argues from the evidence already gathered rather than from a new measurement: the reference's byte form varies with its own build routing rather than with anything about the configuration, and once #373 closed the content and #394 closed the key order the two values parse to identical JSON with identical keys in identical order — so there is nothing here a consumer can observe, and matching it would mean varying deacon's label formatting by whether a build happened. |
| The `devcontainer.local_folder` and `devcontainer.config_file` identity labels — and `${devcontainerId}`, which is a hash of them — carry the NORMALIZED spelling of the path on Windows: separators folded to `\`, dot segments collapsed, drive letter lowercased. | matches the reference | no scenario — the normalization is the identity off Windows and a case cannot choose the drive-letter spelling of `${WORKSPACE}`; 5 hermetic unit tests over a measured 12,000-vector table, plus 1 Windows-lane test on the real `ContainerIdentity` | CLOSED — [#682](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/682), mined from the reference's own `src/test/labelPathNormalization.test.ts` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 18). deacon stamped `absolutize()` verbatim, so on Windows it wrote `C:\Users\me\ws` where the reference writes `c:\Users\me\ws` (`normalizeDevContainerLabelPath`, `spec-node/utils.ts:617`, applied on both the write and the search side in `findContainerAndIdLabels`). Because the reference LOWERCASES and Windows hands users `C:\`, this diverged on the ORDINARY spelling rather than an exotic one: every Windows workspace, in both directions — a container the VS Code Dev Containers extension created was invisible to `deacon exec --workspace-folder` and vice versa, `down --all` and the superseded-container sweep both filter on these values, and one folder got two different `${devcontainerId}`s depending on which tool ran, which is the one property the spec requires of that id (`devcontainer-id-variable.md:25`). **Scoped by measurement, not by reading.** Separators and dot segments were ALREADY normalized — `absolutize`'s `normalize_lexically` rebuilds through `PathBuf::push`, which emits `\` on Windows — and deacon's own hash labels were already case-insensitive on the drive, because `Path`'s `Hash` hashes the parsed `Prefix` and std uppercases the drive when parsing it (std's own doc example, `library/std/src/path.rs:427`). So the reachable delta was the label STRING alone. The whole of `path.win32.normalize` is ported anyway, because the reference also applies it to label values read back off FOREIGN containers, which never went through `absolutize` — and deacon now does the same in the supersede sweep's `config_file` comparison. The port is verified DIFFERENTIALLY against node rather than read off the page: 12,000 adversarial vectors, zero mismatches, and disabling the CVE-2024-36139 guard produces 1,611 — the port covers node 22's rewrite (reserved device names, the `.\` guard), which is a moving target older node versions answer differently for, none of them shapes a workspace path can take. Three existing tests spelled their expectation as `absolutize(...).display()` and would have FAILED on the Windows lane after the fix: on that platform a temp dir is `C:\Users\RUNNER~1\…`, so those assertions were quietly pinning the un-normalized drive letter on the one platform where it mattered — the watch-to-fail this row would otherwise have had no way to run. `--id-label` values stay untouched, matching the reference, which returns early on `providedIdLabels`. **The Windows lane earned its keep on the first attempt at this fix:** the supersede sweep's document test normalized the CANDIDATE's label and compared it against a `ContainerIdentity::config_file` that is only normalized when it was set through `with_config_file`, so five `supersede_tests` went red there and none could have gone red on Linux — off Windows both calls are the identity and a one-sided comparison is indistinguishable from a symmetric one. The comparison is now a named helper taking the platform as a PARAMETER, so its regression test runs on every host; with the asymmetry reinstated it fails on Linux too. NOT ported: the reference's client-side fallback scan (`findDevContainerByNormalizedLabels`) that lists every container carrying the host-folder label and compares normalized values. deacon's equivalent queries are daemon-side `--filter label=k=v` exact matches, which succeed once both tools normalize at write time; the scan only reaches containers written by a tool that did NOT normalize, including deacon before this fix. |

### `read-configuration`

| Behavior | Status | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `read-configuration --config <path>` with no `--workspace-folder` anchors the workspace at the CURRENT DIRECTORY, as the reference does — `--config` selects which document to read, not where the workspace is. | matches the reference | 1 scenario + 1 hermetic test | RULED and FIXED — [#660](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/660), closed by [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 15 with the maintainer ruling *follow the spec*. deacon used to re-anchor the workspace at the config file's PARENT directory, which for the standard layout is `.devcontainer`. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 from an empty directory against a config elsewhere: the reference reports `workspaceFolder: /workspaces/<cwd basename>` with the CWD as the mount source; deacon reported the config's own parent in both. The reference's answer is the consistent one — its workspace folder is `--workspace-folder` or the cwd on every subcommand — and deacon's re-anchoring made one flag do two things. The old behavior was not accidental but had never been adjudicated: the [#615](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/615) row recorded it as a boundary of that change ('`--config` alone still anchors the workspace at the config file's directory (#66)'), describing deacon rather than weighing it, and [#66](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/66)'s own body asserted a third thing that measurement refutes — that the reference 'treats `workspace` as undefined when absent'. It does not; it defaults to the cwd. **An issue body is a claim, not a measurement.** The fix is one deleted branch: the cwd is materialized at the entry point for a `--config`-alone invocation exactly as it already was for a bare one, so the anchor reaches `${localWorkspaceFolder}`, local feature-path resolution and `ContainerIdentity` consistently. Container-only mode is untouched — a container selector still yields no workspace (`bhv-readconfig-container-id-no-workspace`, #659). |
| `read-configuration --include-merged-configuration` against a container with no `devcontainer.metadata` label merges the supplied `--config` alone, rather than refusing. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test | FIXED — was open nonconformance [#657](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/657). Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 13), `src/test/cli.set-up.test.ts` — the file's eight `it`s are four subcommands crossed with two ways of supplying a configuration, and every one of them targets a container the CLI did not create. Upstream's arm asserts `response.configuration.postAttachCommand` and `mergedConfiguration.postAttachCommands.length === 1`; deacon produced neither. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on a plain `docker run alpine:3.17` container with an explicit `--config`: the reference exited 0 with both blocks; deacon exited 1 with `Container '<id>' does not have required 'devcontainer.metadata' label. Cannot compute merged configuration without container metadata.` and wrote nothing to stdout. The label is a SOURCE of configuration, not a precondition for having one: the merge is the label's entries plus the config entry, and with no label there is simply nothing to prepend. **The inconsistency is what makes it unambiguous** — thirty lines up the sibling helper already tolerates a label that is present and UNPARSEABLE (`… proceeding without it`), so deacon was lenient about a corrupted record and fatal about a missing one. The permissive branch was already written; this one now defaults to no entries. A label that is present and will not parse stays fatal, deliberately: that is a corrupted record, not an absent one. NOT changed: the sibling error for a selector that matches no container at all, which the reference also fails. |
| `read-configuration`'s `mergedConfiguration` picks the metadata merge branch from the CONTAINER's labels: on a container carrying this workspace's identity label the metadata label is the complete lifecycle record, and on any other container its entries are layers BENEATH the caller's own configuration. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test (2 shapes), 2 hermetic tests | FIXED — was open nonconformance [#658](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/658). Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 13), `src/test/cli.set-up.test.ts` — the file's eight `it`s are four subcommands crossed with two ways of supplying a configuration, and every one of them targets a container the CLI did not create. This is the SAME `Tr`/`Tt` discrimination [#527](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/527) landed for `exec` and `run-user-commands`; `read-configuration` never got it and stated the complete-record rule as an unconditional fact, in a comment whose own premise — *when the container's id-labels match* — was never tested. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 with a `--config` authoring `postAttachCommand` over a label authoring `postCreateCommand`, on three container shapes: with `devcontainer.local_folder` present both CLIs report `postAttachCommands: []`; with only the metadata label, and with no labels at all, the reference reports `["touch /postAttachCommand.txt"]` and deacon reported `[]`. `customizations` follows the same split and was measured alongside: `[{label}]` on the first shape, `[{label},{config}]` on the second, both CLIs agreeing after the fix. **Why #527's own measurement missed it, which is the reusable part:** that row records this surface as byte-identical before and after, and it was — measured against a container `up` created, the one shape on which the hard-coded branch is the correct branch. A premise that is true of every container you test with is indistinguishable from a fact. The fix selects the branch through the same predicate the other two consumers use (`shared::container_metadata::carries_workspace_identity_labels`) so the three cannot drift apart again. The pre-existing unit test for the complete-record branch had the same defect as the code — its fixture carried no identity label, so it was asserting the branch the container did not select; it now carries one, which makes its stated premise true, and a sibling asserts the layered branch. **No parity case**, for the reason [#527](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/527)'s row already gives: `op-up` stamps both label locations identically and masks the difference by construction. |
| `read-configuration --container-id` with no `--workspace-folder` reports an EMPTY `workspace` block, rather than deriving one from the `--config` path. | matches the reference | 1 scenario, 1 Docker-gated test | FIXED — was open nonconformance [#659](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/659). Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 13), `src/test/cli.set-up.test.ts` — the file's eight `it`s are four subcommands crossed with two ways of supplying a configuration, and every one of them targets a container the CLI did not create. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 across all four selector shapes, which is what scopes the change to exactly one of them: `--container-id` + `--config` → `{}` on the reference and, on deacon, a full block naming the config directory as a bind-mount source; `--container-id` + `--workspace-folder` → the derived block on both; `--workspace-folder` alone → the derived block on both; `--container-id` ALONE → the key omitted entirely on both. The reference's rule is simply that the block describes the workspace the CALLER named, and a `--config` path is a document location, not a workspace. Not cosmetic: `workspaceMount` is a real mount specification, and reporting `source=<config dir>,target=/workspaces/<config dir basename>` for a foreign container invites a consumer to act on a mount that does not exist and nobody requested. The distinction between an OMITTED key and a present-but-empty one is the reference's own and is preserved rather than approximated, which is why `WorkspaceConfig::workspace_folder` became `Option<String>` with `skip_serializing_if` — the CLAUDE.md rule for every modeled optional, applied here so an empty block can serialize as `{}` at all. Backed by `case-readconfig-container-id-has-no-workspace`, the one claim in this batch expressible over an `up`-created container and therefore the only one that reaches `parity_docker` and the release gate. Its second operation is the teeth: naming `--workspace-folder` on the SAME container brings the block back, so the case is a claim about the SELECTOR rather than about `read-configuration` having stopped reporting a workspace. |
| `read-configuration` resolves `${containerEnv:*}` against a RUNNING container for the workspace, rather than leaving the token literal because no container was named on the command line. | matches the reference | 1 scenario, 2 hermetic tests | Fixes [#636](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/636). Deferring the token until a container exists is correct and separately recorded (the `runArgs` row above); what was missing is the second half — once one exists, resolve it. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on a root `.devcontainer.json` declaring `remoteEnv: {LOCAL_PATH: ${localEnv:PATH}, CONTAINER_PATH: ${containerEnv:PATH}}`. With NO container the two CLIs agree byte for byte, both leaving `CONTAINER_PATH` as `${containerEnv:PATH}`. After `up` on that same workspace they parted: the reference reported the container's own PATH in both `configuration.remoteEnv` and `mergedConfiguration.remoteEnv`, deacon reported the literal token in both. **The capability was already there and the DISCOVERY was what was missing**, which is what sized the fix: `deacon read-configuration --container-id <cid>` on the same workspace and the same container produced the reference's answer, so the probe, the substitution pass and the merge were all correct — deacon simply never went looking. Third in a family the project has now closed three times, and `read-configuration` was the last subcommand out of step: [#608](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/608) closed it for `up --include-configuration`, [#616](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/616) for `set-up`, and all three run the one shared pass (`shared::container_substitution`), whose module doc had already named the pattern. **Discovery is DEMAND-GATED and that is a design decision worth reading**: `read-configuration` touches no daemon today, which is why every one of its other cases is hermetic, so probing on every invocation would spend a `docker ps` on the overwhelming majority of runs with nothing to gain. The gate asks the SERIALIZED document whether it contains `${containerEnv:` rather than walking fields, because the token is legal in `containerEnv`, `remoteEnv`, `mounts`, `customizations` and any unmodeled key the `extra` passthrough carries — a field-by-field predicate would be a second enumeration to keep in step with `apply_variable_substitution`, the drift CLAUDE.md already has a checklist about. Measured: 78 ms per run unchanged for a configuration without the token, 102 ms for one with it and no container. **Best-effort and silent on every failure path**, which is a requirement and not a convenience — no daemon, `docker` off `PATH`, no container for this workspace, an inspect that does not answer, all leave the token deferred and exit 0, and that is what the reference does too (measured both ways at 0.87.0). A `read-configuration` that started failing because a daemon was down would be a worse regression than the defect. SCOPED to `${containerEnv:*}` and nothing else: the pass deliberately keeps `resolve_devcontainer_id = false` and does not set `containerWorkspaceFolder`, both of which have their own characterized answers on this path, so discovering a container cannot silently start resolving a different token. `case-readconfig-upstream-containerenv-resolved-against-container` is the first case in the suite to run `read-configuration` after another subcommand, and asserts BOTH blocks as two operations because `mergedConfiguration` is synthesized rather than echoed — a fix that resolved only `configuration` would leave the merged block wrong and a one-operation case green on the half that worked. Being that first also cost ONE normalizer change: its result document carries the `workspace` block, whose `workspaceFolder` and mount `target` are container-side paths derived from the per-side temp workspace BASENAME, which the full-path token map cannot reach — an artifact of the runner's own isolation rather than a difference between the CLIs (measured directly on two identically-named directories, where both blocks agree). `workspace_basename_token` already existed for exactly this and was scoped to `chan-container-state` only because that was the only channel that had ever carried such a path; `chan-structured-output` now shares it. A tolerance would have been the wrong instrument — it would record a deacon-vs-reference difference that does not exist. All four lanes were re-run green afterwards, and a unit test pins both halves: two basenames normalize equal, and `/workspace/` versus `/workspaces/` still diverges, which is the [#273](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/273) regression the rule's original channel relies on it to keep catching. Mined from the reference's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 9), `src/test/cli.test.ts` — 'should replace environment variables' and its `--include-merged-configuration` twin, two `it`s that both failed against deacon. |
| When a link of an `extends` chain declares a Feature its base already declared at a different version, the later link's version REPLACES the base's entry rather than… | deacon extension | 1 scenario | Fixes #411. A Feature's map key carries its version, so a child bumping an inherited version writes a DIFFERENT key; under the previous key-wise merge both survived and… OPEN QUESTION, carried forward 2026-08-03 and NOT yet measured: whether this override rule is itself spec-supportable after #430. The rule is untouched by #430 — it fires only when one link overlays another — but it now sits on top of a document that may LEGALLY declare one Feature twice (`feature-dependencies.md` §Feature Equality, §Round Stable Sort), so a base declaring two versions and an overlay declaring a third replaces the FIRST match and silently keeps the second. `extends` is a deacon extension with no reference equivalent, so this cannot be settled by measuring the oracle; it needs its own argument from the spec's merge and equality text. Recorded here rather than as an issue because [#411](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/411) is closed and this is a question about the rule it landed, not a defect in it. |
| A single configuration document whose `features` map declares one Feature at two versions installs BOTH, in oldest-tag-first order. | matches the reference | 3 scenarios + 1 Docker-backed install test | Closed #430 on 2026-08-03. Recorded until 2026-08-02 as `spec: unspecified` + `intentional-divergence` — deacon REJECTED the document — on the reasoning that one Feature at two versions has no coherent meaning and that nothing broke the tie. `feature-dependencies.md` breaks it in three passages: §Definition: Feature Equality makes OCI equality depend on the manifest DIGEST (two tags are two Features, not one declared twice), §Feature authorship states that "a single Feature may be installed more than once", and §Definition: Round Stable Sort supplies the tie-break, "Compare and sort each Feature from oldest to newest tag". MEASURED against oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-readconfig-duplicate-feature-one-document` (`git:1.3.2` declared before `git:1.3.1`): `read-configuration` output is now byte-identical, and both sides' `--include-features-configuration` resolve `git:1.3.1` then `git:1.3.2` with `consecutiveId` `git_0`/`git_1` — oldest first, against the document's order. The reference's `features resolve-dependencies` agrees, returning `git@sha256:6d953e8c…` (`:1.3.1`) before `git@sha256:63c96e8a…` (`:1.3.2`). Removing the parse-time rejection alone would have replaced a loud error with a SILENT DROP — Feature identity was version-independent, so the second entry overwrote the first and one `install.sh` ran with exit 0 — which is why the row's evidence includes `integration_up_duplicate_features`, a real `build` asserting both Features' users inside the produced image and their UID order. The retired `wvr-features-duplicate-in-one-document` is deliberately not replaced. |
