| The pre-`customizations` VS Code properties declared by a FEATURE (`extensions`, `settings` in `devcontainer-feature.json`) are migrated into `customizations.vscode` on the resolved Feature. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | Was [#487](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/487), closed 2026-08-05: deacon modeled neither legacy key and DROPPED both, so a consumer of `featuresConfiguration` could not recover what the Feature author declared from any location — the inverse of `bhv-readconfig-unknown-field-preserved` applied to `FeatureMetadata`. The migration now happens at the single metadata-parse chokepoint (`parse_feature_metadata`), before typing, so every consumer sees one shape: `featuresConfiguration`, the `devcontainer.metadata` label and lifecycle merging alike. The merge rules were read off the reference's own `Ob()` and are reproduced exactly — legacy `extensions` are APPENDED after any already-authored `customizations.vscode.extensions`, legacy `settings` are merged UNDER them (an authored key wins), and the legacy keys are removed. Measured at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-local-feature-inside-devcontainer` and `…-outside-devcontainer`: every resolved Feature object is byte-identical to the reference's modulo the `cachePath` normalization, and an `up` on a Feature declaring the legacy pair stamps the migrated block on the container's `devcontainer.metadata` label. This is the FEATURE-level twin of the CONFIGURATION-level question ([#486](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/486)), which was ruled a fix on 2026-08-05 and now shares this implementation — see the row below. Backed by `case-readconfig-upstream-local-feature-inside-devcontainer` and `…-config-at-workspace-root`, which now also prove the claim they were WRITTEN for — that a local Feature path resolves against the folder containing the active `devcontainer.json`. |
| A local Feature whose source resolves OUTSIDE the `.devcontainer/` folder is rejected. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was [#488](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/488), closed 2026-08-05: deacon ACCEPTED a document the specification forbids — the fail-fast direction of constitution IV, and the inverse of every other characterized read-configuration divergence here. `devcontainer-features-distribution.md` §Locally Referenced Features: "A local Feature's source code **must** be contained within a sub-folder of the `.devcontainer/ folder`." The predicate is now one function, `core::features::resolve_local_feature_dir`, called from every local-Feature ingress (`read-configuration`, the shared resolver and its `dependsOn` closure, `up`/`build` staging and its `dependsOn` closure): the path resolves against the ACTIVE config's directory, then the canonicalized result must sit under the canonicalized `<workspace folder>/.devcontainer` — the anchor the reference uses, which is the workspace root and NOT the config's own folder. That distinction is what keeps a config at `.devcontainer/<subfolder>/devcontainer.json` legal. Measured at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-local-feature-missing-folder` (no `.devcontainer` folder at all, Features under `./local-features/`): both sides now exit 1. The two legal anchorings — config inside `.devcontainer/` naming `./localFeatureA`, and config at the workspace root naming `./.devcontainer/localFeatureA` — stay green on the sibling cases, which is what proves the fix did not over-correct. |
| A local Feature referenced by ABSOLUTE path is rejected. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was [#495](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/495), ruled and closed 2026-08-05 on spec authority. `devcontainer-features-distribution.md` §Locally Referenced Features: "A local Feature may **not** be referenced by absolute path." This is the OTHER clause of the section [#488](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/488) implemented, and closing it REMOVES a capability deacon added deliberately in [#126](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/126) — absolute paths were accepted at every local-Feature dispatch site, for parity with `read-configuration`'s own dispatch — so it is a strictness ruling rather than a bug fix. #488's containment predicate narrowed the divergence but left absolute paths landing INSIDE `.devcontainer/` accepted. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: the reference exits 1 with `An Absolute path to a local feature is not allowed.` from an unconditional `path.isAbsolute(userFeatureId)` that fires before it computes the containment root and before it touches the filesystem; deacon exited 0 and resolved the Feature. The rejection now sits at the same single chokepoint, `core::features::resolve_local_feature_dir`, ahead of the containment check, so `up`, `build`, `read-configuration` and the `dependsOn` closure are covered by one branch. The reference also rejects an absolute path in `installsAfter` and `overrideFeatureInstallOrder` (both measured); deacon matched on the latter with a misleading message and SILENTLY NON-MATCHED on the former, and both now name the real cause. deacon's own message is precise rather than a copy of the reference's — it names the offending path, cites the spec clause, and gives the copy-pasteable `./`-relative migration. Also measured and deliberately NOT affected: `${localWorkspaceFolder}/.devcontainer/x` — neither side substitutes `features` map keys, so it stays literal, is not lexically absolute, and falls through to OCI parsing where both sides exit 1. Backed by `case-readconfig-local-feature-absolute-path`, whose first draft declared the absolute path in `devcontainer.json` and AGREED with the rejection compiled out (the temp workspace cannot appear in fixture file contents, so both sides failed on the missing directory instead); it was rewritten to pass the path through `--additional-features`, where `${WORKSPACE}` resolves and the Feature really exists, and then diverges on both channels when the check is removed. |
| A `dependsOn` key or `installsAfter` entry naming a Feature by a BARE id is canonicalized at ingress exactly as a `features` map key is — mapped when it is one of the deprecated v1 ids, rejected otherwise, including when it happens to match a declared local Feature's metadata `id`. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | Was [#505](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/505), measured and closed 2026-08-05. `feature-dependencies.md` gives both properties "the same syntax as the `features` object", and the reference enforces that literally: every non-path entry goes through `getBackwardCompatibleFeatureId` during feature-set assembly, so a bare single-segment id is the deprecated v1 (GitHub Release era) form and only [#491](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/491)'s eighteen-id table survives. Surfaced while fixing [#493](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/493): two examples named local `dependsOn`/`installsAfter` targets by the sibling's metadata id, which [#488](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/488)'s containment failure had been masking. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on four minimal `.devcontainer/`-contained workspaces, and the four probes together are what make this ONE rule rather than a judgment call. (1) `dependsOn: {"base": {}}` with `./base` a sibling — BOTH sides already exited 1 with `Legacy feature 'base' not supported`, because #491/#497 had routed `dependsOn` keys through `canonicalize_user_feature_id` at ingress; nothing owed. (2) The same reference in `installsAfter`, with `./base` DECLARED — the reference exits 1, deacon exited 0 and actively ordered `base` before `app`, because deacon's dependency matcher resolves a reference by canonical id, source **or metadata-id alias**, a form neither the spec nor the reference has. (3) `installsAfter: ["nope-not-here"]`, matching nothing — the reference exits 1, deacon exited 0; this is the half that hid the divergence, since `installsAfter` is the SOFT edge and an unresolved entry is ordinary and extremely common (every real Feature's `installsAfter` on `common-utils` is one), so an ILLEGAL entry was indistinguishable from a legitimate non-match — the same failure mode [#495](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/495) found for absolute paths in this exact field. (4) `dependsOn: {"terraform": {}}` and `installsAfter: ["terraform"]` — BOTH sides exit 0, the former auto-mapping to `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/terraform:1` (and pulling in its own `github-cli` dependency identically on both), the latter canonicalizing, matching nothing and being soft-skipped. Probe 4 is why the rule is "canonicalize", not "reject every bare id", and why the fix is the SAME call the `features`-map ingress already makes rather than a second rule for dependency references: `core::features::validate_feature_dependency_references` runs `canonicalize_user_feature_id` over both properties at the three sites that build a Feature set from a configuration, ahead of the ordering pass. The matcher's metadata-id alias is deliberately LEFT IN PLACE and is now unreachable from a configuration — it still serves `overrideFeatureInstallOrder`, a separate surface this measurement does not touch. deacon's message names the referring Feature and the property, then carries the reference's own diagnostic verbatim, including its `prepend your Feature name with "./"` migration. Backed by `case-readconfig-installsafter-bare-metadata-id` (target declared, so the alias resolved and enforcement is the only thing between deacon and exit 0) and `case-readconfig-installsafter-unmatched-bare-id` (target absent, pinning the soft-skip path separately); both diverge on exit code and structured output when the validation is compiled out. Probe 4's accept-direction resolves nothing and would only add a registry fetch, so it is pinned hermetically by a unit test instead of a case. |
| Local Features are identified by contents AND options, so two dependents requesting the same local Feature with different options get their own instances. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was [#489](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/489), closed 2026-08-05: deacon COLLAPSED them onto one node carrying the CONFIGURATION's option set, so the four dependents that asked for `./b` with their own options silently received someone else's. `feature-dependencies.md` §Definition: Feature Equality — equal only if the Features "point to the same exact contents **and are executed with the same options**"; §(B1) skips a node only if "the **exact** Feature … has already been added". Measured at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-dependson-local-with-options`, where `./b` is requested with five distinct option sets: NINE installation nodes (`b_0`…`b_4`, then `./d`, `./e`, `./c`, `./a`), and deacon now produces the same nine with identical option sets and `consecutiveId`s. The option set is now half the Feature's identity everywhere it is decided — the `dependsOn` accumulator, the dependency graph's nodes and edges, and §Round Stable Sort's third comparison — which is the equality rule #430 settled for duplicate OCI tags, one level deeper. Identical option sets still dedup (measured: two nodes, not three), and #430's rule that an unpinned hard dependency is satisfied by the user's pinned declaration is unchanged. Proven end-to-end by a Docker build asserting one marker per instance inside the produced image, not merely by the JSON. |
| A bare (v1-era) Feature id either resolves the way the reference resolves it, or is rejected at ingress as a deprecated identifier. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was an open nonconformance ([#491](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/491)): deacon did NEITHER — it derived `ghcr.io/devcontainers/<id>`, one path segment short, and surfaced the resulting 401 as an authentication failure against a repository that does not exist, so NO bare id resolved to anything published. The reference's rule was read off the oracle rather than inferred: `getBackwardCompatibleFeatureId` is a FIXED TABLE of 18 deprecated ids plus two renames (`golang`→`go`, `common`→`common-utils`), each mapped to `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/<name>:1`; anything else bare is rejected with `Legacy feature '<id>' not supported`. Measured at 0.87.0, the mapping is applied UNCONDITIONALLY — `--skip-feature-auto-mapping` does not gate it, which a run with and without the flag confirmed produced identical output. `core::feature_ref::canonicalize_user_feature_id` now implements exactly that and is applied at every Feature ingress (`read-configuration`, `up`/`build` feature staging, the `dependsOn` closure, `upgrade`); a bare id outside the table now fails at ingress with the reference's own guidance instead of a misleading 401. The reference reports the id the CONFIGURATION wrote — `userFeatureId` and `userFeatureIdWithoutVersion` both stay `terraform` — so the original is carried through to the report rather than the resolved reference. Verified on `fx-upstream-feature-digest-and-v1-ids`: exit 0, `terraform`/`node` resolving to `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/<id>` at version `1`, and every `featureSet` byte-identical to the reference's. Closing it exposed one residual on the SAME case that the exit-1 had been hiding — a digest-pinned reference carried `featureRef.tag` where the reference emits `featureRef.digest` — now keyed on the form the id used, since a tag cannot contain the digest algorithm's `:`. `case-readconfig-upstream-feature-v1-shorthand-ids` is green against the pinned oracle with no tolerance. |
| `--skip-feature-auto-mapping` is accepted and changes nothing. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was a flag-surface divergence ([#498](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/498)): the two CLIs spelled the same flag and meant different things. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 rather than inferred from the name. The reference declares the flag on `up`, `build`, `read-configuration`, `exec` and `run-user-commands`, all hidden and all self-described as "Temporary option for testing." — and never reads it. The value is threaded into the internal options object and dropped there: the one gate that would consume it is `processFeatureIdentifier`'s sixth parameter, which suppresses `getBackwardCompatibleFeatureId`, and `generateFeaturesConfig` calls that function with FIVE arguments. All thirteen occurrences of the identifier in the bundle are writes or forwards; there is no read. Two runs confirmed the consequence: a config declaring `terraform` and `golang` produced identical output with and without the flag — deprecation warnings and the `golang`→`go` rename included, differing only in a timestamped temp path — and a config passing `--additional-features` resolved the overlay Feature either way. deacon's flag meant "drop `--additional-features`" (007 FR-004, read off the flag's NAME), and in `read-configuration` it additionally suppressed the `"features": {"x": "1.2"}` shorthand's expansion to `{version: "1.2"}` — a second invention the reference performs unconditionally. Both gates are gone. The flag is now inert on all three subcommands and emits a `warn!` saying so, because an accepted-but-ignored flag that says nothing is a silent fallback. The behavior was RENAMED, not removed: `--ignore-additional-features` carries it, so no capability was lost. `case-readconfig-skip-feature-auto-mapping-inert` is the same fixture and argv as `case-readconfig-overlay-dockerfile-depends` with the flag appended, and it was watched to fail — under the old semantics it resolves one featureSet instead of two. |
| The pre-`customizations` VS Code properties declared by the CONFIGURATION (`settings`, `extensions`, `devPort` at the top level of `devcontainer.json`) are migrated into `customizations.vscode`. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was [#486](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/486), filed for a ruling rather than as a defect and ruled a FIX on 2026-08-05. deacon echoed all three where they were authored and synthesized no `customizations` block at all, reaching them through the unmodeled `#[serde(flatten)] extra` passthrough, whose contract is to preserve rather than rewrite. The pinned spec mandates no migration — `supporting-tools.md` says only where such properties belong, `devcontainerjson-reference.md` models none of the three, and `devPort` appears nowhere in `parity/spec/113500f4/` — so this is the standing rule that on a spec-silent OUTPUT surface the reference is the authority, plus an internal consistency argument: the same properties on a FEATURE were already migrated ([#487](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/487)), and migrating on one document while echoing on the other is worse than either answer applied to both. There is now ONE migration in the codebase, called from the configuration-parse chokepoint (`ConfigLoader::load_from_path`) and the metadata-parse chokepoint (`parse_feature_metadata`) alike, before typing in both — so `read-configuration`, the merged configuration, the `devcontainer.metadata` label and every document of an extends chain see the one shape. The rules are the reference's own `Rb()`: legacy `extensions` APPENDED after any authored `customizations.vscode.extensions`, legacy `settings` merged UNDER them, the legacy keys removed, and no empty `customizations` created for a document that authors none of them. `devPort` is the one property `Rb()` reads and the Feature reader `Ob()` does not, and it moves only when `customizations.vscode.devPort` is absent — an authored one wins and the top-level key then STAYS, measured rather than assumed. Verified at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-devport-userenvprobe`: the reference maps `devPort` to `customizations.vscode.devPort` and the whole output document is now byte-identical to it. `case-readconfig-upstream-legacy-vscode-properties` is green with no tolerance. |
| `featuresConfiguration…sourceInformation.userFeatureId` reports the Feature id the CONFIGURATION wrote, for every reference form, and `userFeatureIdWithoutVersion` is that id with its version component stripped. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | Was [#490](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/490), filed for a ruling rather than as a defect and ruled a FIX on 2026-08-05. A Feature authored `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/git` was reported as `…/git:latest`: a RESOLVED value in a field whose contract is the DECLARED one, the same class as the `outdated` report-key divergence [#407](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/407) closed but on a surface that fix did not reach. `parity/spec/113500f4/` says nothing about `userFeatureId`, so the reference is the authority on its own output contract; the deciding argument was consistency, deacon having already committed to the declared reference on `outdated` (#407) and for bare v1 ids (#491). The authored id is now carried for EVERY Feature through the one canonical → authored map #491 introduced — the same mechanism `up`'s staging path already keeps for the lockfile, which upstream `generateLockfile` also keys by the authored form — and `userFeatureIdWithoutVersion` is derived from it by the reference's own `/[:@][^/]*$/` strip (leftmost-first, so a `:tag@sha256:…` loses both) instead of being re-derived from the resolved reference. Canonicalization stays internal to resolution; only the report changed. Verified at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-feature-no-version-tag` — both sides report `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/git` on both fields, with the same `computedDigest` and `consecutiveId` as before — and on `fx-upstream-feature-digest-and-v1-ids`, where a bare v1 id, a digest-pinned id and a tagged id all still agree. `case-readconfig-upstream-feature-no-version-tag` and `case-readconfig-upstream-feature-v1-shorthand-ids` are green with no tolerance. |
| With no `--workspace-folder`, no `--config` and no container selector, `read-configuration` resolves its configuration from the CURRENT DIRECTORY. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | FIXED — was an open nonconformance against [#615](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/615), and the LAST subcommand out of step on this axis. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 in a directory holding a devcontainer document, at the parent commit: the reference exited 0 and printed a result document; deacon exited 1 with `Missing required argument: One of --container-id, --id-label, --workspace-folder, or --config is required.` and never looked at the cwd. It was an INTERNAL inconsistency as much as a parity one — `exec`, `build`, `down` and `run-user-commands` already defaulted to the cwd and `up` was fixed by [#610](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/610), leaving `read-configuration` alone in demanding a flag. The default is materialized ONCE at `execute_read_configuration`'s entry, exactly where `execute_up` materializes its own, and the two now share one helper (`shared::workspace::resolve_workspace_folder`); placing it there rather than leaving it to the `unwrap_or(".")` fallback further down is what makes the defaulted run identical to `--workspace-folder $(pwd)` on every consumer of the path — `resolve_workspace_configuration`, the `ContainerIdentity` behind `${devcontainerId}`, local feature-path anchoring and every `SubstitutionContext` the command builds — instead of leaking a bare `.` into `configFilePath` and into the not-found diagnostic. NARROW BY CONSTRUCTION: the default applies only when NO selector is given. `--config` alone still anchors the workspace at the config file's directory (#66) and `--container-id` / `--id-label` alone still mean container-only mode, where inventing a workspace would silently start reading the cwd's configuration instead of the container's metadata. Two scenarios: `case-readconfig-upstream-cwd-default-workspace`, hermetic and therefore gating every pull request on all three operating systems, which asserts the fixture's own `name` and `image` so that it is a claim about WHICH document was read rather than about exit 0; and `case-readconfig-upstream-cwd-default-workspace-differential`, the nightly comparison, whose recorded `deacon.stdout` and `oracle.stdout` are BYTE-IDENTICAL at oracle 0.87.0 — the same `configuration`, `configFilePath` and `workspace` block, git-root workspace mount included. |
| `read-configuration` with no selector and NO devcontainer config in the current directory exits 1 for the RIGHT reason — a missing config, not a missing flag. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The NEGATIVE arm of the cwd default, landed with the fix for [#615](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/615) and not before it, on the precedent its `up` and `exec` twins set. Before the fix deacon also exited 1 here, so an exit-code assertion would have been GREEN while deacon failed on a missing FLAG and the reference on a missing CONFIG — the vacuous assertion `docs/PARITY.md` warns about. The `chan-stderr` half is what separates the two reasons and could not have passed at the parent commit. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 in an empty directory: both CLIs exit 1, and there the agreement ends — the reference prints NOTHING on either stream beyond its version banner, while deacon names the document it looked for (`Configuration file not found: <abs>/.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`). deacon's is the more useful of the two answers and not a divergence to fix, so `case-readconfig-upstream-no-config-in-cwd-rejected` pins DEACON's answer rather than comparing — the same shape as `bhv-up-no-config-in-cwd-rejected` and `bhv-up-missing-workspace-rejected`, and the reason no differential twin exists for this arm: a comparison could only carry the exit code, which is already `case-errors-decl-missing-config`'s job with the flag passed explicitly. DISTINCT from that row's behavior, which never resolves a workspace at all: this one resolves the workspace from the cwd first and then finds no document, the path a developer meets by `cd`-ing into the wrong directory. HERMETIC — config resolution touches no daemon and no registry — so it gates every pull request on all three operating systems, over `fx-errors-missing-config`, a directory holding only a `.gitkeep`. |
| `${localEnv:NAME:default}` takes only the FIRST segment after the name as the default: `${localEnv:NAME:default:a:b:c}` falls back to `default`, and everything past it is discarded. The `${env:*}` alias and `${containerEnv:*}` follow the same rule. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios, 2 hermetic tests | Fixes [#668](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/668). deacon split the lookup body on the FIRST colon only and folded the rest into the default, so an unset variable reported `default:a:b:c`. The reference splits the whole token on `:`, treats `parts[0]` as the variable and `parts[1..]` as arguments, and `lookupValue` reads only `args[0]` and `args[1]` (`spec-common/variableSubstitution.ts:85-89`, `:137-155`); its own unit suite asserts exactly this shape (`src/test/variableSubstitution.test.ts:122`). **The spec is what makes this deacon's difference rather than a coin flip**: it documents no `:default` suffix at all — `${localEnv:VARIABLE_NAME}`, *"Unset variables are left blank"*, is the whole of it (`devcontainerjson-reference.md:155`) — so defaults are a reference-CLI extension and the standing spec-silent rule hands the authority to the reference with no second answer to weigh. The fix is four lines in `split_env_default`, whose doc comment had asserted the OPPOSITE rule in as many words (*"the default may itself contain colons"*) — a wrong invariant written down confidently, which is why it survived. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 before and after on a fixture probing five shapes; after, the two CLIs' `containerEnv` blocks are identical. **Four of those five keys are control arms and are the reason a wrong fix cannot pass**: a single-segment default still resolving, a missing variable with no default still being empty, `${localEnv:}` (an empty NAME, which the reference resolves rather than throwing) still being empty, and the `${env:*}` alias resolving through the identical rule. Mined from the reference CLI's own unit suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 16) — the first batch to mine a UNIT test file rather than an e2e one, on batch 14's rule: a unit-test file is a rich seam when the function it tests feeds an output surface. |
| An authored `workspaceFolder` that is itself a template seeds `${containerWorkspaceFolder}` with its RESOLVED value, so `"workspaceFolder": "/workspaces/${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}"` answers the token rather than leaving it literal. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios, 4 hermetic tests | Fixes [#669](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/669). deacon refused to seed any value containing `${` — a DELIBERATE guard, with a comment saying so, in both the shared loader (`config.rs`) and `read-configuration` — so `${containerWorkspaceFolder}` and `${containerWorkspaceFolderBasename}` survived as literals for exactly the commonest idiom there is. The reference resolves that one value in `substitute()`'s prologue before it substitutes the document (`spec-common/variableSubstitution.ts:30-32`) and tests the shape at `src/test/variableSubstitution.test.ts:60`. **The sharp part is what was already right**: `configuration.workspaceFolder` itself was substituted correctly before the fix, on both CLIs. The defect was never in the document, it was in the CONTEXT built from it — so a case asserting the resolved field alone would have been green throughout, which is why the case asserts the field and the two probe tokens together. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 across all three shapes: the templated one diverged, and the two neighbours — a literal `workspaceFolder` seeded verbatim, and an unauthored one falling back to `/workspaces/<basename>` — already agreed and now have a hermetic test each so that "seed whatever is authored" and "always use the default" both fail. The `up` surface was measured too and agrees after the fix (both CLIs stamp the same `containerEnv` onto the container). One guard is kept on purpose: a value that is empty, or that RESOLVES to empty, still does not seed — answering the token with `""` would be worse than deferring it to the container-aware pass. `set-up` had the authored-folder half of this right since [#513](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/513); this is the same defect one level down, on the templated variant, and both now run through one helper. |
| `${localEnv}` / `${env}` / `${containerEnv}` with NO variable name are left literal and the command exits 0. The reference CLI exits 1 and prints nothing at all — not the result document, and not the diagnostic its own code composes. | documented choice | 2 scenarios | MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-readconfig-env-name-missing`: the reference exits 1 with an empty stdout and nothing on stderr past its version banner, so a user gets a silent failure of the WHOLE configuration because one token in it was malformed. Its `lookupValue` does throw a `ContainerError` naming the token and the file (`spec-common/variableSubstitution.ts:156-158`), but the message never reaches the caller on this path. **The standing spec-silent rule is not applied here, and this row exists to say why.** That rule resolves *which of two answers is correct* by deferring to the implementation everyone targets, and it presupposes the reference HAS an answer; a silent non-zero exit is a failure mode, not a behavior. deacon's side is not a new decision either — an unrecognised variable is left unchanged everywhere else in the engine, and `${localEnv}` names no variable. Matching would mean failing a `read-configuration` with no message, the silent-failure class constitution IV rules out. **One colon separates this from agreed behavior**, which is the reason it is worth recording rather than leaving to be rediscovered: `${localEnv:}`, with an EMPTY name, resolves to the empty string on both CLIs and is pinned by the row two above. Allowlisted as `bhv-readconfig-env-name-missing-left-literal`, with the tolerances on the differential twin so they are SELF-INVALIDATING — the day upstream surfaces that diagnostic, the record fails as stale and that is the signal to reopen the choice. |

### `run-user-commands`

| Behavior | Status | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `run-user-commands --container-id <id>` with no `--config` takes its configuration from the container's `devcontainer.metadata` label, and ignores any document in the current directory. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test | FIXED — was open nonconformance [#656](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/656). Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 13), `src/test/cli.set-up.test.ts` — the file's eight `it`s are four subcommands crossed with two ways of supplying a configuration, and every one of them targets a container the CLI did not create. Upstream's arm is 'Command run-user-commands / should succeed and run postCreateCommand from metadata'. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 from an empty directory against a container whose label declares a `postCreateCommand`: the reference exited 0 and ran the hook; deacon exited 1 with `Configuration error: Configuration file not found: <cwd>/.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` and never looked at the container. **The last subcommand out of step in a family already closed three times** — measured on the same container, `set-up`, `read-configuration` and `exec` all handled it correctly, and `set-up` had the helper for it (`load_optional_config`). The container-metadata fold `run-user-commands` needed was already written and already ran (`container_metadata::resolve_config_against_container`, #405); `load_config` failed several hundred lines earlier and it never got there. Both halves are asserted because the old behavior failed each differently: it EXITED 1 when the cwd held no document, and SILENTLY USED that document when it did — measured, the reference ran the label's hook and left the cwd's alone. The test's temp directory therefore holds a devcontainer document with a different marker, so 'ignored it' is a claim rather than an absence nothing can see. The gate is narrow by construction: with no container selector a missing document is still an error (`case-runusercommands-upstream-no-config-in-cwd-rejected` pins that and stayed green), and naming `--config` / `--override-config` / `--workspace-folder` still means the caller chose a configuration. Shipped with [#655](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/655) rather than after it, because the two are coupled: reaching the container's hooks is what then exposed them running in a directory that does not exist. |
| A lifecycle hook that exits non-zero fails the run rather than being reported as a successful setup. | deacon follows the spec, the CLI deviates | 7 scenarios | The failing hook arrives as a command-line override so the preceding `up` can succeed: a hook failure during creation is a different case, and conflating the two would… |
| Lifecycle hooks run in spec order — onCreate, updateContent, postCreate, postStart — and a Feature-contributed hook runs exactly once alongside the configuration's own. | matches the reference | 6 scenarios | Observed by having each hook append its own name to a file inside the container, so the file IS the order. `grep -c` rather than a presence check pins the Feature hook's… |
| The running container's `devcontainer.metadata` contributes `remoteUser`, `remoteEnv` and lifecycle hooks to `run-user-commands`, as it already does to `up` and `exec`. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes #405. (The SOURCE of that label, and the composition it takes part in, were both corrected by [#527](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/527) — see the row below. What this row records is unaffected: an image-contributed hook still reaches `run-user-commands`, because `up` stamps the image's entries onto the container it creates.) Measured against the pin on a base image whose label carried all three and a devcontainer.json that declared none: the reference ran the image's `postCreateCommand`, as `metauser`, with `META_ENV` set; deacon ran nothing, as `root`, with neither — the exit code was 0 on both sides, so only the marker file showed it. `exec` had done this merge inline since #223 and `run-user-commands` loaded the workspace config alone, which is the whole asymmetry. The merge is now the one shared `container_metadata::resolve_config_against_container`, and `remoteEnv` is layered over `containerEnv` for the hook environment the way `up` layers it. Backed by `case-run-user-commands-image-metadata-differential` (nightly; its `assertion` also pins deacon's side) and by the Docker-gated `run_user_commands_feature_lifecycle::run_user_commands_honors_image_metadata_user_env_and_hook` on the PR lane. `up`'s COMPOSE path never called the merge either; that was tracked on #448 and is now closed (see the `up` section). |
| `exec` and `run-user-commands` read `devcontainer.metadata` off the CONTAINER inspect, and the container's IDENTITY LABELS pick the composition: on the dev container for this workspace that label is the COMPLETE lifecycle record, so neither the caller's own hooks nor its resolved Features are added; on a foreign or raw-labeled container the label is extra layers beneath both. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test, 1 parity case, 6 hermetic tests | Was an open nonconformance ([#527](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/527)), closed 2026-08-06. Found while adjudicating [#524](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/524), whose own premise was refuted: `set-up`'s label read was measured CORRECT on all three probe shapes, and the same probe found the INVERSE bug where nobody had looked. The shared `container_metadata::resolve_config_against_container` (#405) called `merge_image_metadata_after_image_ready` on `container.image`, so both consumers read a label off the IMAGE. Measured live at oracle 0.87.0 on a fixture built to make the two sources DISAGREE — an image whose label's `postCreateCommand` writes `image`, and a container created with `docker run --label devcontainer.metadata=…` whose hook writes `container`: the reference wrote `container`, deacon wrote `image`. **The source was only half of it, and the half that was easy to see.** Swapping it alone made every lifecycle hook run TWICE — measured on `fx-ruc-image-feature` after `up --skip-post-create` + `run-user-commands`, where the reference writes one each of `config-onCreate` / `config-updateContent` / `feature-postCreate` / `config-postCreate` / `config-postStart` and deacon wrote two of every one — because `up` stamps the container label as `[…image entries, …one entry per installed Feature, the config entry]` while `run-user-commands` ALSO resolves the Features itself (#137) and ALSO carries the config's own singular hooks (#467/#477). Reading the IMAGE label had never collided only because a feature-extended image inherits nothing but the BASE image's entries. The maintainer ruled for the reference's own architecture rather than a suppression heuristic, and the branch is derived from the bundle, not invented: `Tr` returns `[…labelEntries, SI(config, rG)]` when `Object.keys(pt(idLabels)).every(k => container.Config.Labels[k])`, where `rG` is exactly `["remoteUser","userEnvProbe","remoteEnv"]` — **no lifecycle command**, which is what makes the label the sole source of hooks there — and otherwise `Tt`, which layers `[…labelEntries, …featureEntries, SI(config, SV)]`. `bg` builds `idLabels` as `[devcontainer.local_folder]` plus `devcontainer.config_file` only when the container already carries that key, so the `every` test reduces to a non-empty `devcontainer.local_folder`; values are never compared. Both branches were measured before being implemented, and the second one refutes the obvious guess that the branch tracks how the container was ADDRESSED: a container the reference `up` created runs each hook exactly once even when named with `--container-id` (identity labels present ⇒ complete record), while a raw `docker run --label` container under the same `--container-id` runs the label's hook AND the config's own, label first. One more line of `Tr` is load-bearing and is easy to drop: `if (!labels[RI]) return Tt(…)` — a container with the identity labels but NO metadata label takes the LAYERED branch, without which clearing the caller's hooks would leave a deacon-created container with nothing to run at all. deacon renders the complete-record branch by clearing the caller config's five singular hooks before the fold and reporting `MetadataComposition::suppresses_caller_features()` so `run-user-commands` skips `resolve_features_ordered`; the scalars still last-win to the caller's config, which is the same outcome upstream gets by appending its three-property pick last. Skipping that resolution also skips its fail-fast, and that is correct on this path — the Features are already installed, so what the configuration declares NOW cannot change what runs. `exec` is unaffected by the hook clearing: it runs no lifecycle phase and reads only `remoteUser` / `remoteEnv`. NOT changed: the three remaining image-label reads, each correct for its caller — `up`'s pre-container merge (there is no container yet), `container_metadata_entries` (building the label to STAMP, which needs the base image's own entries), and `read-configuration`'s no-container path (upstream's `inspectDockerImage`, which pulls on demand). The two branches are guarded on different lanes because no single one reaches both: `case-run-user-commands-feature-contributed-hook` (`parity_docker`, gates every PR and the release) pins the complete-record branch end-to-end at `grep -c` == 1, and the Docker-gated `run_user_commands_feature_lifecycle::run_user_commands_reads_metadata_from_the_container_not_the_image` pins the layered branch over a raw-labeled container — which no parity case can express, since `op-up` stamps both label locations identically and masks the difference by construction. Watched to fail at both levels: forcing the layered branch everywhere (the naive source swap) turns `case-run-user-commands-feature-contributed-hook: chan-stdout: Diverge` red again and fails the two complete-record hermetic tests; dropping only the no-label fallback fails `identity_labels_without_a_metadata_label_stay_layered` with `left: CompleteRecord`. |
| `run-user-commands --skip-post-attach` runs every phase except `postAttachCommand`. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from `src/test/cli.exec.base.ts` at v0.87.0, [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 6. The flag is a `run-user-commands` flag as well as an `up` flag, and until this row NOTHING in the suite passed it to either — it appeared only in the prose of three `notes` fields, each reciting it as the opt-out that keeps `run-user-commands` usable as an explicit re-run. A flag argued from in three rationales and exercised by no scenario is the exact shape this suite exists to close. The shape mirrors upstream's own, which drives the flag through `run-user-commands` after an `up --skip-post-create`; deferring first is what makes the observation clean, because `up --skip-post-create` leaves the marker file ABSENT (#476's case) and every line the file ends up holding was therefore written by the pass under test. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-up-skip-post-create-phases`: both CLIs exit 0 from both operations, both leave no file after the deferred `up`, and both leave exactly `onCreate\nupdateContent\npostCreate\npostStart\n` after the flagged `run-user-commands`. Asserted by `case-run-user-commands-upstream-skip-post-attach`, which carries the content in addition to the differential because over-reach is the plausible defect here — the flag's twin on `up` was wrong in precisely that direction. Deliberately NOT asserted: what a PLAIN `run-user-commands` re-runs, which is the separately characterized `wvr-ruc-completed-hooks-not-rerun`. |
| `run-user-commands` prints a result document on stdout on every terminating path — `{"outcome":"success","result":"…"}` on success, `{"outcome":"error",…}` on failure — and `result` reports how far the run actually got. | matches the reference | 3 scenarios, 3 hermetic tests | Fixes [#635](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/635). deacon wrote NOTHING to stdout here — not an empty document, no output at all — on success and on failure alike, while the exit codes already agreed. The gap was the document, not the information: the failure diagnostic was on stderr and was perfectly good. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on a root `.devcontainer.json` with an `alpine:3.19` image and a `postCreateCommand`, each CLI against its own container after `up --skip-post-create`: the reference printed `{"outcome":"success","result":"done"}` and deacon printed an empty stdout AND an empty stderr, exit 0 on both. **An inconsistency inside deacon rather than a design choice**, which is what made the fix small: `up` has emitted this document since 001, `set-up` emits it, and `build` was brought into line by [#594](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/594) — `run-user-commands` was the last subcommand out of step, the same shape as [#615](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/615) closing the cwd-default family. It follows #594's structure exactly: an `execute_run_user_commands` wrapper around an `_inner` that now returns how far it got, with the error document rendered from the anyhow chain (outermost context as `message`, the causes beneath it as `description`). UNCONDITIONAL rather than gated on an output-format flag, because `run-user-commands` has none — on either CLI. **`result` is not decoration**, which is why the three cases pin its VALUE and not merely the presence of a document: the reference's chain returns one of four words — `skipNonBlocking`, `prebuild`, `stopForPersonalization`, `done` — and every one of them is exit 0, so `result` is the only thing that distinguishes "ran everything" from "stopped early because you asked me to". Three of the four were already truthful for deacon and only the document was missing (`--prebuild` and `--skip-non-blocking-commands` were measured on the same fixture: identical hooks run on both sides); the fourth was not, and is the row below. The ORDER of the four stops is the content of the decision and is pinned by a hermetic table — `--skip-non-blocking-commands` beats `--prebuild` only when it cuts off earlier, and `--stop-for-personalization` sits between `postCreate` and `postStart`, so three of the table's rows ask two flags to stop at different points and only one answer is the reference's. All three scenarios were WATCHED TO FAIL against the parent commit, where `chan-structured-output` verdicts a divergence because stdout is not valid JSON at all. Mined from the reference's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 9), `src/test/cli.test.ts`, whose `JSON.parse(res.stdout).outcome` could not run against deacon. |
| `run-user-commands --stop-for-personalization` stops the run after `postCreateCommand`: neither `postStartCommand` nor `postAttachCommand` executes. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#637](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/637). deacon parsed the flag and dropped it — the field carried `#[allow(dead_code)] // Future feature: stop for personalization` — so both later hooks ran anyway. A flag accepted and ignored is the silent fallback constitution IV rules out, and this one was invisible from the outside: the command exits 0 either way and the only trace is the side effects the extra hooks leave in the container. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-ruc-lifecycle-phases` from a CLEAN slate, before the fix: the reference left `postCreate` and deacon left `postCreate`, `postStart` and `postAttach`. After: byte-identical documents and identical marker files. **Landed with [#635](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/635) rather than after it, deliberately** — `result: "stopForPersonalization"` is reported on exactly this path, and reporting it while deacon had in fact run `postStart` and `postAttach` would be a worse defect than the silence it replaced. The two now come from ONE decision (`run_outcome`, whose value both fills the field and gates the `postStart` block), which is the structural reason they cannot drift apart again. Its scenario asserts the MARKER SET rather than the outcome, and that is the whole point — both CLIs exited 0 before the fix and would have gone on doing so, so a case built on `chan-exit-code` would have been green against the defect. The `exec rm` operation between `up` and `run-user-commands` is load-bearing for the same reason: `up --skip-post-create` still runs `postStart` and `postAttach`, so without the truncation the assertion would be reading `up`'s work. Mined from the reference's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 9). |
| `run-user-commands` accepts a `--config` naming a document nested in a subfolder, runs the hook that document declares, and the same `--config` threaded through `up --skip-post-create` defers it. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Upstream's 'should run with config in subfolder', RUN rather than parsed. `fx-upstream-subfolder-config` was vendored in batch 1 and covered only by a `read-configuration` case, so its `postCreateCommand` — the whole reason the upstream test exists — had never been executed here. That is the batch-7 lesson restated on a second fixture: **vendoring a fixture from an execution test and covering it with a parse case records the fixture, not the claim.** Three things have to be right at once and only the third is new: the named document wins over the discoverable one, `build.dockerfile` resolves against the NAMED config's directory (the subfolder has its own Dockerfile, so resolving against the workspace root silently builds the wrong image), and the hook `up --skip-post-create` deferred is the one `run-user-commands` then runs. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0, both CLIs: marker absent after the deferred `up`, `{"outcome":"success","result":"done"}` from `run-user-commands`, marker present after. Both observations route through `sh -c '… && echo present || echo absent'` rather than a bare `test -f`, because a failing operation is a case failure the runner reports as such — an absent-marker assertion written as a non-zero exit would be indistinguishable from the case breaking, which is what would have made the DEFERRED half unassertable. Mined from the reference's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 9), `src/test/cli.test.ts`. |
| `run-user-commands` with no `--workspace-folder`, no `--config` and no container selector resolves its configuration from the CURRENT DIRECTORY. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | A COVERAGE gap rather than a defect, and the last one in this family. deacon already defaults here; the family was closed subcommand by subcommand — `exec` and `build` always did, `up` was [#610](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/610), `read-configuration` [#615](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/615) — and each of those four carries a case pinning it. `run-user-commands` carried none, so nothing would have reported the day it started demanding the flag. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: both CLIs exit 0 from a cwd holding the fixture, both print `{"outcome":"success","result":"done"}`, both leave the `postCreate` marker. The case asserts the MARKER as well as the document, which is what makes it a claim about WHICH workspace was resolved — a run that resolved some other directory, or fell through to an empty base configuration, exits 0 and prints the same document but cannot have run this configuration's hook. It costs nothing to express (the runner anchors each operation's cwd to the case's workspace, so omitting the flag is the whole test) and that is also its one fragility, invisible in an empty `argv` and recorded on all five siblings. Mined from the reference's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 9), `src/test/cli.test.ts`. |
| `run-user-commands` in a current directory with no devcontainer document, and no selector of any kind, exits 1 with an `outcome: error` document. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The NEGATIVE arm of the cwd default, and upstream's own. **It could not have been written before [#635](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/635)**, which is why it lands with it rather than in a mining-only commit: deacon exited 1 here before too, so an exit-code assertion alone would have been green for the wrong reason — the trap `docs/PARITY.md` names. `chan-structured-output` is what makes it a real assertion, and it could not have passed at the parent commit, where deacon printed nothing on stdout and the channel verdicts a divergence. It pins deacon's answer and only the SHAPE of the document, not its wording: the two CLIs phrase it differently and freezing deacon's exact sentence would make any improvement to the message an edit to this suite, which is not an opinion the suite should hold. HERMETIC, and the lane is a claim rather than a convenience — config resolution fails before any daemon contact, verified by re-running with `docker` off `PATH` and getting identical output on all three channels. Mined from the reference's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 9), `src/test/cli.test.ts`. |
| `run-user-commands` against a `--workspace-folder` that does not exist exits 1 with an `outcome: error` document. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Upstream asserts this on `JSON.parse(error.stdout)`, which could not run against deacon at all until [#635](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/635) — there was no stdout to parse. DISTINCT from the `no-config-in-cwd` row above, which resolves a real directory and finds no document; this one names a directory that is not there, and the `up` and `exec` families keep the same pair for the same reason. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: both CLIs exit 1 and both print an `outcome: error` document. **The wording differs, deacon's is the worse of the two here, and it is deliberately NOT pinned so it stays free to improve**: the reference resolves the relative path and names the document it looked for, while deacon reports `Configuration file not found: path-that-does-not-exist` — the raw argument, described as a configuration file when it is a workspace directory. deacon's own `up` says the accurate thing (`Failed to resolve workspace path …`) because it routes through `shared::workspace::resolve_workspace_folder`, which validates the directory; `run-user-commands` reaches its cwd default incidentally, through the config loader, and never validates. An internal inconsistency worth closing on its own terms, and pinning only the outcome and the exit code leaves it closable without editing this case. **CLOSED, and the case is now TIGHT — [#644](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/644), batch 11.** The promise the paragraph above makes is what came due: the wording was left free to improve, it improved, and `case-run-user-commands-upstream-missing-workspace-rejected` gained a `chan-stderr` assertion holding it there. deacon now prints the same sentence on all six subcommands. The gap was never to the reference — the two still word this differently and that difference is adjudicated on the `up` row as not-a-divergence — it was inside deacon: four of its six subcommands agreed with NEITHER sentence, because `ConfigLoader::discover_config`'s does-the-workspace-exist guard reused the `ConfigError::NotFound` variant that means a missing FILE everywhere else in the codebase. See the `build` section's companion row for the fix and the measurement. HERMETIC, verified with `docker` off `PATH`. Mined from the reference's own e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 9), `src/test/cli.test.ts`. |
