| `set-up --include-merged-configuration` emits `mergedConfiguration` with the collected plural lifecycle arrays, as `read-configuration` does. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was an open nonconformance ([#483](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/483)), closed 2026-08-05. deacon emitted its flat configuration under a snake_case `merged_configuration` key, with the five lifecycle hooks as SINGULAR fields — three defects in one block, and none of them visible to a test of what set-up RAN, because #477 had already made the EXECUTION correct. Re-measured live at oracle 0.87.0 on a container `up` stamped from a workspace config plus a differently-worded `--config` layered on top: the reference reports `onCreateCommands` `["echo base-onCreate", "echo overlay-onCreate"]` and `postCreateCommands` `["echo base-postCreate", "echo overlay-postCreate"]`, with `updateContentCommands`/`postAttachCommands` as `[]`, `init`/`privileged` as `false`, and a `configFilePath` whose `scheme` is `file` (not the `vscode-fileHost` the same reference uses for `read-configuration`, which keys the scheme off whether the CALLER NAMED the path). deacon emitted `merged_configuration` carrying `onCreateCommand: "echo overlay-onCreate"` — one command per phase, under a key no consumer of the reference's output looks for. It now agrees on every key after normalization; the only elisions are the three synthesized empties (`remoteEnv`, `containerEnv`, `portsAttributes`) that `drop_absent_optional` drops on BOTH sides under the standing `bhv-readconfig-merged-computed-empties-omitted` characterization, so the case declares no tolerance at all. The fix is a routing change, not a second serializer: `set_up.rs` now calls `read_configuration`'s `apply_upstream_merge_shape` + `normalize_merged_configuration_shape` + `insert_config_file_path`, ordering the collected entries exactly as `aggregate_lifecycle_commands` replays them (label layers in label order, `--config` last), so what the block REPORTS and what set-up RUNS are built from one input. Backed by `case-set-up-merged-configuration-shape` — the first parity case to invoke `set-up`, which is why the subcommand joined the harness's `CONSUMER_SUBCOMMANDS`/`CONTAINER_SUBCOMMANDS` vocabulary here — plus three hermetic unit tests on the PR lane that pin the plural arrays, the camelCase key and the `configFilePath` presence rule. Watched to fail: with the product fix reverted and the case left in place, the `docker-shared` differential group reports exactly one divergence, `case-set-up-merged-configuration-shape: chan-structured-output: Diverge`, out of 61 cases. One difference remained on this surface and was NOT this row: the `configuration` block, where deacon folded the container's image metadata in and emitted no `configFilePath`. It was untouched by this change, filed as [#502](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/502) and closed on 2026-08-05 with the scenario it was waiting on — see the row below. |
| `set-up --include-configuration` echoes the `--config` document AS AUTHORED, with its `configFilePath`, rather than the metadata-merged configuration set-up resolved. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Was an open nonconformance ([#502](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/502)), closed 2026-08-05. Same authored-vs-resolved contract as #490 and #407: `configuration` reports what the CALLER SUPPLIED, `mergedConfiguration` (the row above) reports what set-up resolved. Measured live at oracle 0.87.0 on a container `up` stamped from a workspace config authoring `remoteUser: root` and `containerEnv: {FROM_BASE: "1"}`, with an overlay `--config` authoring NEITHER: the reference's block is the overlay alone — `name`, `postCreateCommand`, and a `configFilePath` whose `scheme` is `file` — while deacon's carried `remoteUser` and `containerEnv` lifted off the container's `devcontainer.metadata` label, as though the caller had written them, and no `configFilePath` at all. Both shapes now agree key-for-key. The no-`--config` shape was measured on the same oracle and needed no change: both CLIs emit a bare `"configuration": {}`, which falls out of the same rule rather than being special-cased — `config_file_path_value` keys the `file` scheme off whether the caller NAMED the path, and a set-up with no `--config` has no config file to name. The fix is a two-line routing change plus reuse, not a second serializer: `set_up.rs` stopped cloning the merged config into the reported block and now substitutes the authored `--config` document, then calls `read_configuration`'s existing `insert_config_file_path` — the same helper #483 wired into the merged block. Backed by `case-set-up-configuration-authored`, a pure data edit (fixture + case) on the vocabulary #483's case opened, plus two hermetic unit tests on the PR lane pinning the no-fold rule and the caller-named `configFilePath`. Watched to fail: with the product fix reverted and the case left in place, the differential lane reports exactly one failing case, `case-set-up-configuration-authored` — the `jsonSubset` assertion missing `configuration.configFilePath` AND `chan-structured-output: Diverge` on the folded keys — out of 62. One difference remained on this surface and was NOT this row: variable substitution CONTEXT — deacon anchored set-up's `SubstitutionContext` at the process cwd as a stand-in for the absent workspace, so `${localWorkspaceFolder}` and `${devcontainerId}` resolved to cwd-derived values where the reference leaves both literal. It was untouched by this change, filed as [#510](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/510) and closed on 2026-08-05 with the scenario it was waiting on — see the row below. |
| `set-up` leaves the workspace-derived variables — `${localWorkspaceFolder}`, `${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}` and `${devcontainerId}` — LITERAL, in the blocks it reports AND in the lifecycle commands it execs, because it takes no `--workspace-folder`. `${localEnv:*}` still resolves. | matches the reference | 1 scenario, 2 hermetic tests | Was an open nonconformance ([#510](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/510)), closed 2026-08-05. `set-up` adopts an already-running container and has no `--workspace-folder`, so deacon anchored its `SubstitutionContext` at the PROCESS CWD as a best-effort stand-in and then answered the workspace variables from it — inventing a workspace the caller never named. Measured live at oracle 0.87.0 with an overlay `--config` authoring the probe variables in `remoteEnv` plus a `postCreateCommand` echoing three of them, run from a cwd deliberately outside the fixture: the reference resolves `${localEnv:HOME}` (`/home/vscode`, both sides agree) and leaves `${localWorkspaceFolder}`, `${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}` and `${devcontainerId}` LITERAL; deacon emitted the cwd, the cwd's basename, and a computed 12-char id. `${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}` was not in the issue's table and was found by widening the probe. The EXECUTION surface was measured too and was the same defect, which no test of the reported JSON could see: the reference hands the literal tokens to the container shell, which expands the unset names to empty (`ws=[] base=[] id=[]`), while deacon substituted the cwd INTO the command string it exec'd (`ws=[/tmp/…/scratchpad] base=[scratchpad] id=[55a0e24e02bb]`). After the fix deacon's hook writes `ws=[] base=[] id=[]` byte-for-byte. The fix REUSES the mechanism `${devcontainerId}` already had rather than adding a second one: `resolve_devcontainer_id` (#219) proved the shape — a per-pass gate that makes `resolve_variable` return `None` so the token survives — so a sibling `resolve_workspace_folder_vars` gates the two `localWorkspaceFolder*` variables, and `SubstitutionContext::without_workspace` sets both flags false in one named constructor. That constructor still takes the cwd, but as a MECHANICAL anchor only: `container_lifecycle.rs` roots lifecycle phase markers and the host-side working directory at `local_workspace_folder`, so blanking the field would have broken marker persistence. Naming the constructor `without_workspace` is what makes the absence explicit — the anchor is still there, it is just no longer reachable by any variable, and a test asserts it never leaks into a substituted value. One neighbour was re-measured and changed deliberately: the `containerEnv`-only pass in `container_env_probe.rs` builds a context with an EMPTY `local_workspace_folder`, where resolving those tokens would silently BLANK them; it now sets the same flag false, so a surviving token is preserved rather than emptied. Backed by `case-set-up-substitution-scope`, a pure data edit (fixture + case) on the vocabulary #483 opened, plus two hermetic tests on the PR lane — one on the engine (literals survive while `${localEnv:*}` resolves) and one at the set-up layer asserting the SAME literals in the reported `remoteEnv` and in the `postCreateCommand` string, which is the surface #510 said a JSON-only test would miss. The case observes both surfaces: `chan-structured-output` for the two reported blocks and `chan-file-content` for the hook's artifact, written into a bind mount the base fixture pins at `/work` so it is host-visible. It passes `--container-data-folder` because `op-up` leaves the reference's lifecycle markers in the default folder and a marked phase does not re-run — without it the hook is silently skipped and the execution channel proves nothing, which is a trap any future set-up case that wants to observe execution will hit. Watched to fail: with the product fix reverted and the case left in place, the `docker-shared` differential group reports exactly one failing case, `case-set-up-substitution-scope`, diverging on BOTH declared channels out of 63 — `chan-structured-output` first mismatching at `configuration.remoteEnv.PROBE_LOCAL_WS`, and `chan-file-content` at `setup-substitution.txt`, so the reported blocks and the EXECUTION are each independently pinned. With the fix restored the group is 63/63 green with an empty diverging set. One assertion is written in POST-NORMALIZATION vocabulary and must stay that way: `devcontainer_id_token` rewrites the literal `${devcontainerId}` to `<ID>` inside `configuration`/`mergedConfiguration`, so the absolute claim on `PROBE_DEVCONTAINER_ID` is `<ID>` — and since that same rule maps both a literal token AND a substituted 12-char hex id onto `<ID>`, the differential on that one key cannot pin it, which is why the two un-rewritten `localWorkspaceFolder*` keys and the execution channel carry the sharp claims. One difference remains on this surface and is NOT this row: `${containerWorkspaceFolder}`, which deacon never resolves in the reported blocks (even when the `--config` authors `workspaceFolder`, where the reference emits it) and invents as `/` at exec time when it is unauthored (where the reference leaves it literal). It is a different defect in kind — the value EXISTS when authored, so the fix is to read it from the authored config rather than to stop answering — and it needs a seam in the shared `ContainerLifecycleConfig` to separate the substitution value from the exec cwd, so it was left out and filed as [#513](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/513) — closed on 2026-08-06 with the pair of scenarios it was waiting on, see the row below. The case is built blind to it on purpose: its overlay authors no `${containerWorkspaceFolder}`, so this row's three variables are pinned without depending on the unsettled one. |
| `set-up` resolves `${containerWorkspaceFolder}` and `${containerWorkspaceFolderBasename}` from the `--config` document's OWN `workspaceFolder` — in the blocks it reports AND in the lifecycle commands it execs — and leaves both LITERAL when that document omits one, rather than inventing `/`. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios, 2 hermetic tests | Was an open nonconformance ([#513](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/513)), closed 2026-08-06. This variable is the one workspace-shaped token that CAN have an answer in `set-up` — which is why it was not fixed by [#510](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/510), whose three variables have no answer at all — and deacon was wrong in BOTH directions while disagreeing with itself. Re-measured live at oracle 0.87.0 across all four cells (authored/unauthored × reported-block/hook-exec), with an overlay `--config` authoring both tokens in `remoteEnv` plus a `postCreateCommand` echoing them into a bind-mounted file. AUTHORED `workspaceFolder: /work`: the reference reports `/work` and `work` in both blocks and its hook writes `cwf=[/work] base=[work]`; deacon's hook agreed but its reported blocks emitted the raw `${containerWorkspaceFolder}` — the `SubstitutionContext` #510 introduced carried `container_workspace_folder: None` and nothing ever filled it. UNAUTHORED: the reference leaves both literal on both surfaces, so the container shell expands the unset names and the artifact is `cwf=[] base=[]`; deacon wrote `cwf=[/] base=[]`, an invented `/` reaching the command text from `merged_config.workspace_folder.unwrap_or("/")` — a DEFAULT for the exec cwd, not a value anyone authored. After the fix all four cells agree byte-for-byte. The SOURCE was measured too, and it is the `--config` document ALONE, not the merged configuration: on a container whose `devcontainer.metadata` label authored `workspaceFolder` with an overlay that did not, the reference still left both tokens literal — its config reader seeds the context with `containerWorkspaceFolder: <the config document's raw workspaceFolder>`, undefined when the document omits it. So deacon reads `base_config`, and `case-set-up-container-workspace-folder-unauthored` is built to FAIL a naive fix that reads the merged config. The fix is a seam, not a special case: `ContainerLifecycleConfig` now carries `substitution_workspace_folder: Option<String>` beside the `container_workspace_folder: String` cwd, because the two are different uses of one path and only the cwd is guaranteed to exist — `docker exec -w` needs a path, so set-up's cwd still falls back to `/`, while the substitution value is simply absent. The config field is AUTHORITATIVE over the caller's context (`Some` sets it, `None` clears it), which is what lets `set-up` express absence at all; `up` and `run-user-commands` both have a real workspace and pass `Some` of the same path they pass for the cwd, so they are unchanged by construction — proven by running their Docker binaries, not argued. set-up now reads its substitution value back OFF the context it already built, so the reported blocks and the commands it RUNS can no longer disagree the way they did here. Backed by two parity cases (a pure data edit — fixture + case — on the vocabulary #483 opened): `case-set-up-container-workspace-folder-authored` pins the resolution and `…-unauthored` pins the literal, each observing BOTH surfaces — `chan-structured-output` for the two reported blocks and `chan-file-content` for the hook's artifact — plus two hermetic tests on the PR lane. One drives the SEAM directly through the mock runtime: with `None` the token survives verbatim into the exec'd command and with `Some` it resolves, while `working_dir` is the same path in both runs, which is the separation stated as an assertion. The other is at the set-up LAYER, asserting the same authored/unauthored pair in the reported `remoteEnv` and in the `postCreateCommand` string built from the same context, which is where the two halves used to disagree. Both cases pass `--container-data-folder`, the trap #510 documented: `op-up` leaves the reference's lifecycle markers in the default folder and a marked phase does not re-run, so without a fresh one the hook is silently skipped and the execution channel proves nothing. Watched to fail: with the product fix reverted (the cases and the seam left in place) the `docker-shared` differential group reports exactly two divergences out of 65 cases, and they land on DIFFERENT channels, so each half of the defect is independently pinned — `case-set-up-container-workspace-folder-authored: chan-structured-output: Diverge` (the reported blocks leaving the token literal) and `case-set-up-container-workspace-folder-unauthored: chan-file-content: Diverge` (the invented `/` reaching the hook). With the fix restored the group is 65/65 green with an empty diverging set, and the full `--profile parity` run is 8/8. One neighbour was measured and is NOT this row: on that same metadata-only probe deacon reports `mergedConfiguration.workspaceFolder: "/work"` where the reference reports `null`. First attributed to a label-SOURCE difference and filed as [#524](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/524); the adjudication measurement (2026-08-06, ruling packet on that issue) REFUTED the attribution — both CLIs read the same source, the container inspect's `Config.Labels`, into which Docker already merges the image's labels with run-time `--label` winning, confirmed on all three probe shapes (diverging, image-only, container-only). The real cause is the merge PROPERTY SET: the reference's `mergeConfiguration` folds an enumerated metadata property list where deacon's `merge_configs` folds everything — seven leaked fields (`workspaceFolder`, `name`, `runArgs`, `appPort`, `workspaceMount`, `features`, `overrideFeatureInstallOrder`), tracked as [#526](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/526) — closed on 2026-08-06, see the row below, where the census turned out to be a floor rather than a total (the label is arbitrary JSON, so any property NOT on upstream's enumerated list leaked, not only the seven a fixture happened to author). The same measurement surfaced the INVERSE source bug where nobody had looked: `exec`/`run-user-commands`' shared `resolve_config_against_container` read the IMAGE label where the reference reads the container inspect ([#527](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/527)) — load-bearing because deacon's own `up` stamps the container only. That one closed on 2026-08-06 and carries its own row in the `run-user-commands` section; the source turned out to be only half of it, and fixing it needed the reference's id-label BRANCH as well, or every lifecycle hook ran twice. No set-up case reaches either defect (every set-up case builds its container with `op-up`, which stamps both label locations identically). |
| `set-up --include-configuration` / `--include-merged-configuration` resolve `${containerEnv:*}` against the adopted container in the blocks they REPORT, and leave the tokens LITERAL in the lifecycle commands set-up EXECS. | matches the reference | 1 scenario, 1 Docker-gated test, 2 hermetic tests | Was an open nonconformance ([#616](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/616)), closed 2026-08-22. The same defect as [#608](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/608) on the same output surface, on the subcommand [#613](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/pull/613) did not reach — and known to be there the whole time: `set_up.rs` carried a `## Deferred (post-PR-6c)` note naming exactly this pass. `set-up` ran ONE substitution pass, built before the function knew anything about the container's environment, so every `${containerEnv:*}` reached the caller as a template. Measured live at oracle 0.87.0 on a container whose `Config.Env` carries `SETUP_PROBE_VAR=from-container-env` and an overlay `--config` reading it back through `remoteEnv`: the reference reports `from-container-env` in BOTH blocks and the empty string for a key the container does not define; deacon reported the raw `${containerEnv:SETUP_PROBE_VAR}` and `${containerEnv:NO_SUCH_VAR_XYZ}` in both. **The EXECUTION half was measured too and needed no change — which is the sharp part of this row.** The reference hands the raw token to the container shell, so an overlay hook printing `'${containerEnv:SETUP_PROBE_VAR}'` writes `cenv=[${containerEnv:SETUP_PROBE_VAR}]`, and deacon's writes the same, before and after the fix. So the reference REPORTS a hook resolved (`'from-container-env' ''`) and RUNS it literal, from one authored string — and a fix that folded the pass into what set-up execs would have looked greener on the JSON while diverging in the container. The fix is a move plus one call: #613's helper left `up` for `commands/shared/container_substitution`, where it grew a second entry point taking the caller's OWN `SubstitutionContext` — because `set-up` must not use the workspace-anchored constructor, whose `${localWorkspaceFolder}` / `${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}` / `${devcontainerId}` must stay literal ([#510](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/510)) and whose `${containerWorkspaceFolder}` comes from the `--config` document ([#513](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/513)). The pass therefore adds the ONE thing pass 1 could not know and changes nothing else about the context, and the hermetic test asserts those two neighbours survive it rather than trusting that they do. No second inspect and no probe: `set-up` already inspects the container in phase 2 and `ContainerInfo::env` IS `Config.Env`, the canonical source for the variable. Fail-safe is #613's, unchanged: an unreadable environment SKIPS the pass, because `resolve_variable` answers `Some("")` for a missing key once `container_env` is `Some` — so running it blind would collapse every reference to the empty string and the caller could not tell "the container has no such variable" from "deacon could not read the container". Backed by `case-set-up-container-env-substitution`, a pure data edit (fixture + case) on the vocabulary #483 opened, which declares BOTH surfaces — `chan-structured-output` for the two reported blocks (including the resolved `postCreateCommand`) and `chan-file-content` for the literal artifact the hook wrote — plus one Docker-gated test on a raw `docker run -e` container (the shape that proves the value comes from the CONTAINER rather than from a `containerEnv` the `--config` could have authored) and two hermetic tests on the PR lane. It passes `--container-data-folder`, the trap #510 documented and every set-up case since has paid. Both sides agree on every key after normalization, with `portsAttributes` elided on both by the standing `bhv-readconfig-merged-computed-empties-omitted` characterization, so the case declares no tolerance. Image pinned to alpine:3.19. |
| A lifecycle hook contributed by the CONTAINER's `devcontainer.metadata` label runs ALONGSIDE the ones other entries of that label — and a `--config` — declare for the same phase, rather than only the last one running. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test — no parity case, see notes | Was an open nonconformance ([#477](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/477)), closed 2026-08-04. Both collapse points are gone and all three shapes are byte-identical to oracle 0.87.0, re-measured live on the issue's own repro: with `--config` over a single-entry label, `img-onCreate` / `ws-onCreate` / `img-postCreate` / `ws-postCreate` (deacon previously wrote only the two `ws-` lines); against a three-entry `[image, feature, config]` label with NO `--config`, `e0-onCreate` / `feat-onCreate` / `e2-onCreate` / `e0-postCreate` / `e2-postCreate` / `feat-postStart` (deacon previously wrote only `e2-onCreate` / `e2-postCreate` / `feat-postStart`); and the same label plus a `--config`, which interleaves `ws-onCreate` / `ws-postCreate` last in their phases. The FEATURE entry's hook running at all is new: `set-up` never called `aggregate_lifecycle_commands`, so a Feature-contributed hook ran only when no other entry declared that phase. The fix reuses #467's machinery rather than adding a parallel one — `config_from_metadata_label` lifts each fragment's hooks onto `DevContainerConfig::metadata_lifecycle_layers` and leaves the five singular fields EMPTY, so `aggregate_lifecycle_commands` replays them in label order ahead of the `--config`'s. Leaving a hook in both homes is the double-run trap #467 documented, and it is observed rather than argued: with the clearing removed the Docker-gated test reports `e2-onCreate` / `e2-postCreate` / `feat-postStart` each appearing twice. **No parity case:** `set-up` was not in the harness's `CONSUMER_SUBCOMMANDS` vocabulary when this closed, and that fix was not a reason to grow the case model — the evidence is `test_set_up_collects_container_label_hooks_alongside_the_config_hook` (docker-shared, gates every PR), which asserts the WHOLE marker log on both shapes because a `contains` assertion cannot see an appended duplicate. Two neighbours were re-measured and are clean, so nobody re-checks them: `read-configuration --container-id --include-merged-configuration` is byte-identical before and after the change (it collects through `apply_upstream_merge_shape`, never through this helper), and `exec --container-id` — the other caller of `config_from_metadata_label` — reads only the LAST-WINS `remoteUser` / `remoteEnv` off it and runs no lifecycle phase at all, so it was never exposed and still recovers both across a multi-entry label. One divergence remained on this surface and was NOT #477: `set-up --include-merged-configuration` emitted deacon's flat config under a `merged_configuration` key, where the reference emits `mergedConfiguration` carrying the collected `onCreateCommands` / `postCreateCommands` plural arrays. That block was already the wrong shape before this change and was untouched by it; it was filed as [#483](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/483) and closed on 2026-08-05 — see the row above. **No parity case** was true of this row only at the time: `set-up` joined the harness's vocabulary with #483's case, so a future set-up scenario is now a pure data edit. This row also disposes of [#475](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/475), filed against the same surface while #477 was in flight and closed on 2026-08-06 as already fixed rather than as work: re-measured at oracle 0.87.0 on a raw-labeled container whose TWO fragments each declare `onCreateCommand` and `postCreateCommand` with a `--config` declaring a third `postCreateCommand` — three same-phase contributors — both CLIs' hook logs read `A-ONCREATE` / `B-ONCREATE` / `A-POSTCREATE` / `B-POSTCREATE` / `CONFIG-POSTCREATE` / `A-POSTSTART`, identical including order, and both reported blocks carry the same three-element `postCreateCommands`. The fix #475 proposed (`route the label-fragment fold through aggregate_lifecycle_commands + LifecycleHookLayer`) is verbatim what #477 landed. The surface gained a SECOND guard with #526, which is not redundancy: that change round-trips the metadata config through JSON and `metadata_lifecycle_layers` is `#[serde(skip)]`, so a lost re-attach would stop every label-contributed hook silently — see the row below. |
| A container's `devcontainer.metadata` label contributes only the ENUMERATED properties upstream's `mergeConfiguration` reads off a metadata entry — to `mergedConfiguration` AND to what `set-up` runs — never every property the label happens to author. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test, 2 hermetic tests — no parity case, see notes | Was an open nonconformance ([#526](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/526)), closed 2026-08-06. The reference's merge is not a fold of two configurations, which is the assumption deacon was built on: it is `{...config minus the eight collected properties}` PLUS one expression per ENUMERATED metadata property (bundle `Xi` at `dist/spec-node/devContainersSpecCLI.js`, `mergeConfiguration` in `devcontainers/cli/src/spec-node/imageMetadata.ts`). The list is upstream's `pickConfigProperties` (bundle `SV`) plus `entrypoint` — 25 names — and a property with no expression is simply never read from a label, so the base config is its only source. deacon's `set_up.rs::merge_configs` folded EVERYTHING through `ConfigMerger::merge_configs([metadata, --config])`. Re-measured live at oracle 0.87.0 on a container created raw (`docker run --label devcontainer.metadata=…`) so the label is exactly as authored — which is the only shape that reaches this, see below — carrying #524's seven-field census plus `image`, `service`, `runServices` and `initializeCommand`: the reference's `mergedConfiguration` reports NONE of the eleven and deacon's reported ALL eleven, while the two agreed key-for-key on every enumerated property (`remoteUser`, `containerEnv`, `forwardPorts`, `userEnvProbe`, the collected hook arrays). The census in the issue is therefore a floor, not a total: the label is arbitrary JSON, so anything a base image writes and upstream does not enumerate leaked. **It was never only a reporting defect**, and that is what the JSON-only framing missed: `execute_lifecycle_hooks` passes `merged_config.workspace_folder` as the `docker exec` CWD, so a label authoring `workspaceFolder: /meta-ws` — a directory that does not exist in the container — made deacon fail EVERY hook with exit 127 and exit non-zero, where the reference ran all four. The fix is one projection at the chokepoint #524's ruling packet named, not a second merger: `restrict_to_metadata_properties` retains `METADATA_MERGE_PROPERTIES` on the SERIALIZED metadata document before `ConfigMerger` ever sees it. Applying it to the serialized form is deliberate — the list is upstream's, in upstream's camelCase spelling, and that is the only form in which the two can be compared by eye — and it disposes of `DevContainerConfig::extra` for free, where a field-by-field copy would have carried an unmodeled label key through untouched. Projecting after the fragment fold rather than before it is equivalent, because `ConfigMerger` folds field-wise. `metadata_lifecycle_layers` is re-attached across the round-trip because it is `#[serde(skip)]` merge provenance: losing it would silently stop every label-contributed hook from running, undoing the row above, which is why a test pins it directly. **No parity case, and that is measured rather than assumed.** Through `op-up` the reference stamps a run-time `--label devcontainer.metadata` carrying only its own picked entries, and a run-time label overrides the image's, so a hand-written base-image entry is invisible to `set-up` on both sides: on a Dockerfile fixture whose base `LABEL` authored `name`, `runArgs` and `overrideFeatureInstallOrder`, the reference's container label read `[ {"overrideCommand":false} ]` — the config entry alone. `docker run --label` is the only way to put an arbitrary metadata document on a container, and it is outside the harness's operation vocabulary, so the evidence is `test_set_up_folds_only_the_enumerated_metadata_properties` (docker-shared, gates every PR), which asserts BOTH halves — the eleven keys absent and the enumerated ones present, because an absence assertion alone would pass for a restriction that dropped everything — plus the whole hook log. Watched to fail, on each surface independently: with the projection neutered the test fails first on EXECUTION (`chdir to cwd ("/meta-ws") … no such file or directory`, exit 127), and with the label's `workspaceFolder` pointed at an existing directory so the run survives, it fails on the absence assertion at `workspaceFolder`. The two hermetic tests were broken separately too: neutering the projection fails `merge_configs_folds_only_the_enumerated_metadata_properties`, and removing only the layer re-attach fails `restricting_metadata_properties_preserves_the_lifecycle_layers` together with the pre-existing #477 unit test — which is the double-guard that row describes. One neighbour was measured on the same probe and is NOT this row: `customizations`. The reference reports it as per-tool ARRAYS in `mergedConfiguration` (`{"vscode": [ {...} ]}`) and set-up emits the deep-merged object; `read-configuration` already builds the array form with `apply_customizations_shape` and set-up simply never reaches it. It is a different shape of fix — the helper needs one entry per CONTRIBUTOR, so the label's fragments must be threaded down the way `metadata_lifecycle_layers` threads the per-fragment hooks — so it was left out and filed as [#532](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/532), closed on 2026-08-07; see the row below. |
| `set-up`'s `mergedConfiguration.customizations` is one array SLOT PER CONTRIBUTING METADATA ENTRY, keyed by tool — never the deep-merged object. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test, 4 hermetic tests — no parity case, see notes | Was an open nonconformance ([#532](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/532)), closed 2026-08-07. Found on #526's own probe and deliberately left out of it, because the fix is a different shape. Upstream's `mergeConfiguration` does not fold `customizations` at all: the property is on the `kV` DELETE list, stripped from the base config, then rebuilt as `entries.reduce((acc, e) => { for (let tool in e.customizations) tool in acc ? acc[tool].push(…) : acc[tool] = [ … ] }, {})` and emitted only when a key survives (`Object.keys(t).length ? t : void 0`) — one slot per contributor, with each consuming tool reconciling its own slots. deacon reported whatever `ConfigMerger` had deep-merged into a single object, which reads as ONE contributor and silently resolves the conflicts the reference hands over intact. Re-measured live at oracle 0.87.0 on a raw-labeled container whose two fragments both author `vscode.settings` — `{"a": 1}` and `{"b": 2}` — plus a `--config` authoring a third: the reference reports `{"vscode": [{"settings":{"a":1},"extensions":[…]}, {"settings":{"b":2}}, {"settings":{"fromConfig":true}}], "jetbrains": […], "codespaces": […]}` and deacon reported one `vscode` object carrying `{"a":1,"b":2,"fromConfig":true}`. Five probe shapes now agree byte-for-byte (single fragment, multi-fragment, multi-fragment + `--config`, `--config` only, and neither), and the whole `mergedConfiguration` document diffs clean against the reference on a mixed probe modulo the standing `portsAttributes` elision (`bhv-readconfig-merged-computed-empties-omitted`). The fix REUSES `read_configuration::apply_customizations_shape` — the array builder that has been correct since #407 — rather than growing a second one; the reason set-up never reached it is that `merged_configuration_document` routed through `apply_upstream_merge_shape` alone, by which point `ConfigMerger` had already collapsed the fragments. So the per-fragment objects ride down on `DevContainerConfig::metadata_customizations_layers`, the exact carrier `metadata_lifecycle_layers` gave the hooks in #477: `config_from_metadata_label` captures each fragment's `customizations` BEFORE the fold, `merge_two_configs` concatenates rather than overwrites, and `restrict_to_metadata_properties` re-attaches across #526's JSON round-trip — the same `#[serde(skip)]` trap that row documents, and losing it would be silent because the fold's own `customizations` key survives the restriction. Entries are ordered `[…label fragments in label order, --config]`, which is upstream's `Tt` (`[...labelEntries, pick(config, pickConfigProperties)]`), so the `--config`'s slot carries the document AS AUTHORED and not the merge — a hermetic test asserts that directly, because reading `merged.customizations` there would still pass every single-contributor case. The layers are variable-substituted with the same context set-up applies to the configuration, because the reference substitutes them the same way (`Tr` → `IG`: `i.map(e)`); measured on a label carrying both tokens, `${localEnv:*}` resolves and `${localWorkspaceFolder}` stays literal on both sides, so the fix could not trade one divergence for another. **No parity case, for the reason #526 measured**: through `op-up` the reference stamps a run-time `devcontainer.metadata` label carrying only its own picked entries, which overrides the image's, so a hand-written base-image entry is invisible to `set-up` on both sides — and `docker run --label` is outside the harness's operation vocabulary. The evidence is `test_set_up_reports_customizations_as_per_tool_arrays` (docker-shared, gates every PR) plus four hermetic tests. Watched to fail: with the `apply_customizations_shape` call removed and everything else in place, the Docker-gated test fails with the deep-merged `{"vscode": {"settings": {"a":1,"b":2,"fromConfig":true}}}` against the three-slot expectation, and all four hermetic tests fail independently — the array shape, the omit-when-empty rule, the authored-config slot, and the substituted layer. |

### `up`

| Behavior | Status | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| On podman, a Compose configuration that declares Features resolves the Feature-extended image deacon just built, instead of trying to pull it. | matches the reference | 1 unit test + 1 Docker-gated test, restored to the podman lane | CLOSED by [#706](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/706). Podman files locally built images under the `localhost/` repository, so an unqualified tag does not resolve locally and podman reads it as a registry reference; docker resolves the same tag fine, which is why this stayed invisible until the podman lane ran deacon's build tests for the first time ([#705](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/pull/705), advancing [#30](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/30)). MEASURED under `DEACON_CONTAINER_RUNTIME=podman`: `integration_compose_features_build::compose_features_image_shape_installs_feature` failed with `app Error pull access denied for deacon-devcontainer-features, repository does not exist or may require 'docker login'` — a pull attempt for an image that was never pushed anywhere. deacon already implemented the qualification (`CliRuntime::qualify_image_ref` / `is_already_qualified`) and the single-container path used it, but `grep -n "qualify_image_ref\|localhost/" crates/deacon/src/commands/up/compose.rs` returned nothing. **The fix does not reuse `qualify_image_ref`, and the difference is deliberate.** That helper probes `podman image exists localhost/<ref>` to decide whether a bare name means a local build or a registry image, falling back to `docker.io/library/<ref>` when it cannot tell. At this site the caller *built the tag*, so a probe can only introduce a wrong answer — a transient failure would emit a `docker.io` reference for an image that exists on no registry. The new `qualify_local_image_ref` prefixes unconditionally, which is also what the reference does: ``BASE_IMAGE=${params.isPodman && !hasRegistryHostname(imageName) ? 'localhost/' : ''}${imageName}`` (`containerFeatures.ts:470`, citing microsoft/vscode-remote-release#9748). deacon's `is_already_qualified` is a superset of that `hasRegistryHostname`, additionally recognizing `host:port/…` and bare `sha256:` digests. Qualification is applied where the two service shapes converge — one `image_tag`, written into the override the same way for `image:` and `build:` services — so neither arm can drift from the other. **Scope of the claim:** `qualify_local_image_ref` has a unit test, but nothing hermetic proves the override calls it; the restored podman-lane test is the only wiring guard, and the CI comment says so. Not verifiable in this dev container — podman is installed but cannot create user namespaces, so it runs nothing here. |
| An image that is merely absent from the local store is recognized as absent on both runtimes, so it gets pulled rather than reported as an error. | matches the reference | 1 unit test | CLOSED alongside [#706](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/706), whose text listed this as "a second symptom in the same run, same root cause". **That attribution was wrong, and re-measuring is what found the real one.** It is a second, independent podman defect on the same path, and `localhost/` qualification does not touch it. `CliRuntime::inspect_image` recognized only docker's wording — `stderr.contains("No such image")` — before returning `Ok(None)`. Podman words it `Error: docker.io/library/debian:bookworm-slim: image not known`, which matched nothing, so an absent image returned `Err`. The cost lands one layer up: `ensure_image_available` calls `self.inspect_image(image_ref).await?`, so on podman the `?` propagated that `Err` and **the pull that was supposed to follow never ran**. The caller fell back to resolving `_REMOTE_USER` / `_CONTAINER_USER` from devcontainer.json alone, warning about an image that was simply not pulled yet. The measurement is the CI run's own text: the prefix `docker image inspect failed:` is deacon's format string from the `return Err` branch, so that branch is where podman went. Both wordings are now matched case-insensitively by `is_image_not_found`, and the unit test pins four real failure strings (daemon unreachable, unauthorized, podman socket, permission denied) as things that must STAY errors — misreading one as "absent" would turn a broken daemon into a silent empty result. |
| A cached Compose Feature-build produces the SAME image, so a second `up` on a stopped devcontainer reuses its container and anything written into that container survives. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test | CLOSED by [#700](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/700), found mining `cli.up` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 24). MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 across `up` → `docker compose stop` → `up`: the reference returns the SAME `containerId` and a STABLE image id; deacon returned a different container each time, the original was GONE, and a file written into it was lost — with `outcome: success` on both sides. **The cause was image identity, not reuse logic.** BuildKit attaches a fresh provenance attestation to every build, so a FULLY CACHED rebuild still yields a new image id: measured on two consecutive builds of one Dockerfile, layers identical, `Config` identical and `created` identical, yet ids `99483d1aab35` then `519839eb5424`; with `BUILDX_NO_DEFAULT_ATTESTATIONS=1` both produced `c0b6b3e6d519`. Compose keys container recreation on the service's image, so the churn silently discarded the container. deacon now sets that variable on its Compose `build` invocation only — the narrow scope keeps it away from anything that publishes an image, where provenance is worth having — and an explicit user setting always wins. **An earlier attempt fixed the wrong layer and is worth recording:** resuming the stopped project with `docker compose start` restored reuse but routed it into the already-running reconnect branch, which deliberately runs no lifecycle, so the restart ran NO phases where the reference re-runs `postStart` and `postAttach`; `case-up-restart-timing-compose` caught it, and the PR was closed unmerged rather than patched. Fixing image identity needs no control-flow change, so the lifecycle path is untouched — verified against that same fixture, which now yields exactly `onCreate updateContent postCreate postStart postAttach postStart postAttach`. That fixture declares no Features and therefore never diverged, which is why the defect survived: a Feature-less Compose image is already stable. The regression test declares one for that reason — sabotage confirmed a Feature-less fixture passes with the defect present — and asserts a marker written to `/` rather than into a mount, which would survive a recreate and pass either way. |
| The container runtime is selected from BOTH knobs on every subcommand that runs one (`build` takes the binary but not yet the flavor — see below): `--runtime` picks the flavor, `--docker-path` picks the binary, and with no explicit flavor the binary itself decides. | matches the reference | 2 unit tests + 2 Docker-gated tests | CLOSED by [#692](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/692), found mining `cli.podman` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 22) — both of whose `it`s invoke `devcontainer up --docker-path podman`. Selection was built THREE ways that honored different flags: `shared::resolve_runtime` honored both (`down`, `exec`, `run-user-commands`, `set-up`); `RuntimeFactory::create_runtime` takes no path, so `up` honored `--runtime` and silently dropped `--docker-path`; and `CliDocker::new()` honored NEITHER, which is what `build` used at four sites and `up/ports.rs` at one. MEASURED: `up --docker-path /nonexistent/…` exited **0** and created a real container through the real daemon; a shim binary placed at that path was never executed once across a full `up`; `doctor --docker-path /nonexistent/…` reported `Docker version 29.7.2-2`, the version of a binary the user never named. The reference has no runtime flag at all and detects podman by running `<dockerPath> -v` and looking for `podman` (`isPodman`, `src/spec-shutdown/dockerUtils.ts:288` at v0.87.0, threaded as `params.isPodman` into the `localhost/` qualification and the rootless `--userns` handling), so `--docker-path podman` is not merely *a* way to select podman — it is the ONLY one the reference has, and the one its own podman suite uses. deacon now resolves through one helper (except `build`, which takes `--docker-path` but still ignores `--runtime` — split out deliberately because refusing podman there turns the required Podman lane red, and those `build` cases only ever passed BECAUSE build silently used docker; tracked separately), detects the flavor from the binary when no flavor is given, and keeps an explicit `--runtime` authoritative (flag > env > detection, per the standing rule that clap owns precedence). Verified against a REAL podman 4.9.3 installed for the measurement: `up --docker-path podman` now actually runs podman — it fails inside this container on podman's own rootless `newuidmap` error, which is the environment's limit and not deacon's, and is itself the proof that the podman binary was selected. Binary-probe detection additionally catches the `podman-docker` shim, where even the DEFAULT `docker` path is podman. |
| `up --remove-existing-container` succeeds when another process is already removing the same container, rather than failing on the race. | matches the reference | 1 Docker-gated test | CLOSED by [#688](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/688), found mining `dockerUtils` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 20) — upstream's own `protects against concurrent removal` test is this scenario. deacon issued a single `docker rm -f` and treated every failure as fatal. The daemon answers a second concurrent removal with `removal of container <id> is already in progress`, and — measured 6/6 trials — **the container is still present when it says so**, so the message is not a variant of `no such container` but its opposite. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on identical fixtures, each racing one concurrent `docker rm -f` against a container made slow to remove (800 MB written into its writable layer): the reference exits **0** with `{"outcome":"success"}`; deacon exited **1** with `{"outcome":"error"}` and `Remove command failed: … is already in progress`. The reference retries up to 7 times (`removeContainer`, `src/spec-shutdown/dockerUtils.ts` at v0.87.0, citing microsoft/vscode-remote-release#6509), waking on a `docker events` `destroy` filter or a 1s delay, and returns only once the removal has completed; `up` reaches it at `singleContainer.ts:297` and `dockerCompose.ts:57`. deacon now retries the same number of times but polls rather than tailing an events process — the same guarantee (the call returns only once the container is absent) without a second long-lived child per removal. Termination rests on a measured fact rather than an assumption: `docker rm -f` naming an ALREADY-ABSENT container exits **0**, unlike `rm` without `-f`, so the loop ends as soon as the container is genuinely gone no matter which process removed it. **Scope, stated because it is narrower than the row's title suggests:** the retry matches docker's exact phrase, so under podman the same race still fails hard. Podman was not available to measure when this landed, and inventing a second pattern for an error string nobody has observed is how a matcher ends up silently matching nothing — the Docker-gated test skips podman for the same reason rather than pretending to cover it. |
| Re-entering a workspace whose configuration has CHANGED since its container was created provisions a new container for the new configuration rather than reattaching to… | documented choice | 2 scenarios | Measured directly at oracle 0.87.0 against fx-up-stale-config: two `up` runs whose documents differ only in `name` and `containerEnv` return the IDENTICAL containerId… Ruled KEEP 2026-08-02. ENFORCEMENT IS NOW DIFFERENTIAL AND SELF-INVALIDATING (2026-08-03): `case-up-stale-config-reentry-differential` runs both CLIs over `fx-up-stale-config-lifecycle` and consumes `wvr-up-changed-config-recreates`. WHERE the divergence is read is the durable part. A containerId is incomparable across sides by construction, and reading it from the created container did not work either: the workspace label matches BOTH generations (deacon keeps the superseded one — see the row below), so which one a probe observed was a coin flip on their hex ids, and an earlier draft observing `chan-container-state` failed about one run in three for exactly that reason. It is read instead from the host workspace, where a `postCreateCommand` marker records each generation that was actually created: deacon's `generations.txt` reads `first\nsecond`, the reference's `first`, because it reattached and the new document's work never ran. That is the choice's own safety argument, measured. The ambiguity itself is resolved as of [#371](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/371) — exactly one generation is live and the runner observes the RUNNING container — but the host-marker read is KEPT: it does not depend on which container a probe picked, and it states the divergence's cost where a user would actually meet it. |
| A successful `up` leaves exactly one running container **per document**: a container an earlier `up` created from the SAME `devcontainer.json` is STOPPED — not removed — once the current one is live. A second document brought up in the same workspace with `--config` is a sibling, not a generation, and keeps running. | deacon extension | 2 scenarios | The necessary counterpart to the recreate above, and only meaningful because of it. deacon's identity includes a configHash, so editing `devcontainer.json` provisions a new container instead of reattaching; until [#371](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/371) the previous one was left RUNNING, so a workspace accrued one live container per configuration edit, each holding its ports and memory. Measured on `fx-up-stale-config` before the fix: two `up` runs, `docker ps -a --filter label=devcontainer.local_folder=$W` reporting two containers BOTH `Up`. The reference CLI has no equivalent to compare against — it reattaches, so it never creates a superseded container at all (verified at oracle 0.87.0: identical containerId from both runs of the same sequence). STOPPED, NOT REMOVED, is a maintainer ruling (2026-08-07): stopping releases the ports and memory while the container's local state and volumes survive, so a generation stays recoverable with `docker start` — and it is what makes editing a document and editing it back reattach to the original container rather than rebuild it. `--remove-existing-container` remains the explicit removal path, and lifecycle markers are untouched (only #117's explicit replace clears those). The sweep is scoped to containers carrying BOTH `devcontainer.source=deacon` and this workspace's `devcontainer.local_folder`, filtered daemon-side, so a container the reference CLI or the VS Code extension created in the same folder is never touched. Compose is covered symmetrically: its project name embeds the configHash too, so a changed document orphans a whole project, and that project is swept through `com.docker.compose.project` — the only label its dependency services carry. That project expansion is driven by what the CANDIDATES are, never by what the current `up` is: [#551](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/551) found it gated on the current `up` being compose itself, so a workspace whose document changed SHAPE (compose one day, a plain `image` the next) stopped the superseded project's primary — which carries deacon's labels — and stranded its `depends_on` sidecars, which do not. Measured directly before the fix: `deacon_63651e03_532a7bdd-app-1 exited` beside `deacon_63651e03_532a7bdd-db-1 running`, the project network still reporting one attached container; after, both `exited` and the network at zero. Asserted by `case-up-stale-config-reentry` on `chan-temporal`'s `workspaceContainers` census (`total: 2, running: 1`), which read `running: 2` before the #371 fix. The cross-shape transition is asserted OUTSIDE the parity suite, in the Docker-gated `integration_up_supersede_shape`, and deliberately so: that census is a `devcontainer.local_folder` label count and the stranded sidecar carries no such label, so the one container the regression is about is exactly the one the census cannot see. SCOPE CORRECTED 2026-08-15 ([#584](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/584)). This row used to read "any container a previous `up` created for the same workspace", and the code matched it: the sweep selected on `devcontainer.source=deacon` AND `devcontainer.local_folder` and stopped everything else it found. That is broader than every justification above it, all of which are about GENERATIONS OF ONE DOCUMENT — and the extra breadth caught siblings. Measured at oracle 0.87.0: one workspace, two documents (`.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` and `.devcontainer/subfolder/devcontainer.json`), `up` the first then `up --config` the second, and the reference leaves BOTH running where deacon left the first `exited`. The user meets it one command later — `deacon exec --workspace-folder $W echo hi` answered `No running container found` where `devcontainer exec` printed `hi`. Multiple configs per workspace is a first-class reference workflow (`--config` exists for it, and upstream's own e2e suite drives a subfolder config), so this was a divergence with no argument behind it rather than a deliberate extension. FOUND BY MINING ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480)) the reference's `exec` e2e fixtures, which is where two documents in one workspace came from; nothing in deacon's own suite had ever put them there. The discriminator is now `devcontainer.config_file`, chosen because it is COARSER than `configHash`: editing a document changes the hash and keeps the path, so generations still match, while a sibling never does. `--override-config` is the one case the path cannot decide — it names a different file and means a replacement — so the caller states the scope (`SupersedeScope::{Document,Workspace}`) instead of the sweep inferring it; `up --override-config` still stops what it replaced, verified live. KNOWN GAP, written down rather than left to be rediscovered: an `--override-config` container outlives its override, because a later plain `up` sees a candidate naming the override file and spares it. Closing that needs a durable record of HOW a container's configuration was supplied — a label, not an inference. Asserted by `case-up-sibling-config-both-running` (`total: 2, running: 2`), which is the same channel and census as `case-up-stale-config-reentry`'s `total: 2, running: 1` and the opposite answer: read together the pair says what the sweep is scoped to, and either alone reads as a policy about the workspace. It was watched RED at 14ec091 on `chan-temporal` — never on `chan-exit-code`, which stayed 0, because `up` reported success while stopping the container.|

The coverage hole was real; the behavior behind it was not. |
