| The Compose service image's `devcontainer.metadata` contributes `remoteUser`, `remoteEnv` and lifecycle hooks to a compose `up`'s own lifecycle run, as it already does on the single-container path. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes #448, the second instance of the #405 shape and the last known call site. `merge_image_metadata_after_image_ready` had exactly two callers — `up`'s single-container path and the shared `container_metadata::resolve_config_against_container` that `exec` and `run-user-commands` use — and `up`'s COMPOSE path called neither. Measured against oracle 0.87.0 on a compose service whose image label carried all three and whose devcontainer.json declared only its own `remoteEnv`: the reference wrote `metauser from-image-metadata from-workspace-config`; deacon wrote no file at all, exit 0 on both sides, so only the marker showed it. The merge now runs on the compose path at the same point in the flow — image ready, before lifecycle — against the image the container actually runs (the Feature-extended tag when Features were built, else the service's own `image:`), reusing the one implementation. Making it observable also required the compose post-create exec to stop passing `user: None` and an EMPTY env: it now resolves both through the shared `resolve_env_and_user`, which is why the workspace's own `remoteEnv` had been missing too — the identical third miss #405 found. Backed by `case-up-compose-image-metadata-differential` (nightly; its `assertion` also pins deacon's side) and by the Docker-gated `integration_compose_config_mounts::test_compose_up_merges_service_image_metadata`, which runs under the `default`/`full` profiles (`make test-nextest`) — that binary is selected by neither PR lane, `dev-fast` excluding it and `mvp-integration` not listing it. The parity case measures all four cells, but only did so from its THIRD revision, and the two before it are the useful part of the record. The first declared `remoteUser` and passed locally solely because the dev container's uid and the image user's were both 1000; on GitHub's runners it failed with `Permission denied`, which is how #462 was found. The second dropped `remoteUser` so a #448 regression stayed distinguishable from a uid coincidence, parking the user cell in the Docker-gated test. The third took it back once #462 landed: with the remap in place, and the fixture image pinning its user to uid 1234, no plausible host uid matches by accident, so the coincidence is gone from the verdict rather than merely averaged over. The marker records `whoami` and not `id -u` for the same reason in reverse — the remapped uid is the HOST's and would make a fixed assertion a statement about the runner. The tail this row carried — that the compose post-create still ran `postCreateCommand` in its STRING form only and no other phase — was closed by [#460](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/460); see the full-lifecycle-phase-set row. |
| A compose `up` remaps the container user's uid to the host's (`updateRemoteUserUID`), so a non-root `remoteUser` can write the bind-mounted workspace. | matches the reference | 1 scenario + 2 Docker-gated tests | Fixes [#462](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/462), the second of the two compose-path gaps that closing #448 surfaced. deacon called `apply_user_mapping` on `up`'s single-container path only, so a compose service's non-root `remoteUser` kept the IMAGE's uid and any hook writing into the bind-mounted workspace died with `Permission denied` unless the host's uid happened to match. Re-measured against oracle 0.87.0 on the issue's repro — a compose service whose image pins its user to uid 1234, a host at uid 1000: before, the reference's hook reported `user=metauser uid=1000` and wrote while deacon's wrote nothing; after, deacon reports the same `user=metauser uid=1000`. The two CLIs reach that observable by different MECHANISMS and it is worth writing down which: the reference builds a derived `<image>-uid` image whose final `RUN` layer rewrites `/etc/passwd`/`/etc/group`, then points the service at it through its generated `docker-compose.devcontainer.containerFeatures-*.yml` override (verified by reading both the override file and `docker history` of the image it names); deacon remaps in the running container, which is what `apply_user_mapping` already did for the single-container path. Reusing it keeps ONE uid-remap implementation and inherits every spec rule it encodes — default true on Linux, no-op for a root `remoteUser` (#90) or a root host user, and an explicit `false` still opts out. The fix is a call site, not an algorithm. Its cost was the accuracy of the two assertions that now cover it, and both were broken once to prove they can fail: with the call removed, `test_compose_up_merges_service_image_metadata` fails exactly as CI did (`Permission denied`, no marker); with the `updateRemoteUserUID` knob forced true, `test_compose_up_update_remote_user_uid_false_keeps_image_uid` fails `uid=1000` against `uid=1234`. That first test also DROPPED the `chmod 0777` it carried while this row was open — the workaround existed to take permissions out of what a #448 test measured, and with the remap in place the uid-1234 pin makes the write itself the evidence. The parity fixture `fx-up-compose-image-metadata` took its `remoteUser` back at the same time, so `case-up-compose-image-metadata-differential` now measures all four cells of #448's matrix directly — hook ran, hook USER, image `remoteEnv`, workspace `remoteEnv` — against the live oracle. That restoration was broken once too, and it is the strongest of the three: with the call removed, the whole `differential_group_docker_shared` lane run against the pinned 0.87.0 reports exactly ONE diverging case, `case-up-compose-image-metadata-differential: chan-file-content: Diverge` on `up-compose-image-metadata.txt`, and passes clean with the call in. A restored cell that could not fail would be worse than the empty one it replaced. |
| A compose `up` runs the full lifecycle phase set in every command form, as the single-container path does. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | Fixes [#460](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/460). `up`'s compose path carried its OWN post-create exec — `if let Some(cmd_str) = post_create_cmd.as_str()` — so it ran `postCreateCommand` only in its STRING form and queued no other phase; the array and object forms, `onCreate`/`updateContent`/`postStart`/`postAttach`, Feature-contributed hooks and the `.devcontainer-state` phase markers were all dropped on compose while the single-container path ran them. Measured against oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-up-compose-lifecycle-phases`, whose five phases span all three command forms: the reference ran six commands and wrote three marker files; deacon ran NONE and wrote NO file, exit 0 on both sides — so the markers are the whole observation. After the fix deacon's three files are byte-identical to the reference's, including the phase ORDER (`onCreate-string`, `updateContent-array`, `postCreate-array`, `postAttach-string` in one appended log) and both named commands of the object form (their own files, because the spec does not order named commands). The fix deletes the compose-specific exec and calls `up::lifecycle::execute_lifecycle_commands` — the same engine, so phase set, ordering, aggregation of Feature commands, `waitFor`, dotfiles, markers and the skip flags come from one implementation. Two inputs are still compose's to resolve and are: the lifecycle user/env via the shared `resolve_env_and_user` (#448), and the lifecycle CWD via `resolve_container_cwd`, read from the RUNNING container's mounts because deacon injects no workspace mount on compose and `docker exec -w` hard-fails on a directory the service never mounted (#294/#295). The `if !args.skip_post_create` guard that wrapped the old call is gone with it, because the decision belongs to `InvocationContext` rather than to a call site — which is what let the flag's own meaning be corrected later, in ONE place, when [#476](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/476) measured it to defer every phase rather than postCreate onward. A second, unlooked-for conformance gain came with the CWD: the old wrapper was `cd '<dir>' 2>/dev/null; …`, which SHRUGGED OFF a `workspaceFolder` the compose service never mounts, and `docker exec -w` does not. Measured at oracle 0.87.0 on that exact shape (explicit `workspaceFolder: /workspace`, no volume providing it, container kept alive): the reference emits `chdir to cwd ("/workspace") set in config.json failed: no such file or directory`, the hook exits 127 and `up` exits 1 — character for character what deacon now emits, where deacon previously ran the hook from the image's default directory and exited 0. The leniency was the divergence; `smoke_compose_override_command::test_compose_override_command_lifecycle_runs` had encoded it, and its fixture — which declared `/workspace` while mounting nothing there — now declares no `workspaceFolder` at all, resolving to the compose default `/`. Backed by `case-up-compose-lifecycle-phases` (spec-expectation, so it runs on the PR- and release-gating Docker lane) and `case-up-compose-lifecycle-phases-differential`, plus three Docker-gated tests in `integration_compose_config_mounts` covering the image-metadata-contributed phase, `--skip-post-create` and `--skip-non-blocking-commands`. An adjacent divergence measured while doing this is NOT compose's and is not fixed here: when the image's `devcontainer.metadata` and the workspace config declare the SAME phase, the reference runs both (image first) and deacon runs only the workspace's — identically on the single-container path, so it is a config-merge issue, filed as [#467](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/467) and given its own row. |
| A lifecycle hook contributed by the image's `devcontainer.metadata` runs ALONGSIDE one the configuration declares for the same phase, rather than being replaced by it. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios + 2 Docker-gated tests | Fixes [#467](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/467), found while closing #460 and deliberately out of its scope: a config-MERGE defect, not a lifecycle-execution one, reproducing IDENTICALLY on the single-container and compose paths — which is what identified it. The spec's image-metadata Merge Logic table gives each of the five lifecycle hooks a "Collected list of all `<phase>Command`s", with "the devcontainer.json is considered last"; every OTHER row of that table is last-wins, and that is how deacon folded the label in — through `ConfigMerger`, where a hook is an ordinary scalar the higher-precedence layer overwrites. Precedence was right, accumulation was missing. Measured at oracle 0.87.0 on `onCreateCommand` and `postCreateCommand` declared in BOTH the image label and the devcontainer.json: the reference's marker log read `img-onCreate` / `ws-onCreate` / `img-postCreate` / `ws-postCreate`, deacon's only the two `ws-` lines; after the fix deacon's log is byte-identical to the reference's on BOTH paths. Both sides exit 0 throughout, so the marker file is the entire observation. The fix reuses the aggregation that already existed rather than adding a second one: `aggregate_lifecycle_commands` had always run Feature-contributed hooks ahead of the configuration's, and image-metadata hooks now join that same list ahead of both, carried from the merge on `DevContainerConfig::metadata_lifecycle_layers` (not serialized — it is merge provenance, not an authored property). Because the carrier is the config itself, the one merge site reaches all four consumers that share it: `up`'s single-container path, `up`'s compose path (#448) and `container_metadata::resolve_config_against_container`, which `exec` and `run-user-commands` use (#405). `run-user-commands` was verified directly rather than assumed — on a metadata-carrying container its log reads `img-onCreate` / `ws-onCreate` / `img-postCreate` / `ws-postCreate`, byte-identical to the reference's. The half that is easy to get wrong, and that the first draft DID get wrong, is the non-colliding case: an image hook in a phase the config leaves unset was already correct (#448), and recording it as a collected layer while the merge ALSO left it in the singular field ran it TWICE. The merged config's five singular fields therefore carry only what the devcontainer.json itself declared, and every assertion here spans the WHOLE marker log rather than checking for one line — a `contains` assertion cannot see an appended duplicate. Collecting the hooks does NOT make the scalar rows collect: `remoteUser` declared on both sides still resolves last-wins to the config's, measured by the hook's own `whoami`. Backed by `case-up-image-metadata-same-phase-differential` and `case-up-compose-image-metadata-same-phase-differential` (nightly; their `assertion`s also pin deacon's side), and on the PR lane by the Docker-gated `integration_feature_lifecycle::test_up_collects_image_metadata_and_config_hooks_for_the_same_phase` and `integration_compose_config_mounts::test_compose_up_collects_image_metadata_and_config_hooks_for_the_same_phase`, one per path because the defect is path-independent and a single-path test would misattribute a regression. All four were broken once and watched to fail, in both directions: with the layer aggregation removed the docker-shared differential lane reports exactly four diverging cases (the two new ones plus the two existing image-metadata cases, which the same break also kills), and with the singular-field restore removed every assertion reports the doubled `img-postStart`. |
| Lifecycle hooks contributed by installed Features and by the configuration are collected and run TOGETHER in all five phases, with the Feature hooks ordered by the Feature installation order. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e test fixtures at tag `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 1) — coverage chosen by the reference's authors rather than by ours. This is the shape [#467](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/467) and [#477](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/477) were both defects in, and the reference keeps a fixture that covers the whole matrix at once: two local Features ordered by `installsAfter`, each declaring all five phases in the string form and the array form, plus the configuration declaring the same five itself. The observable is an append-only `lifecycle-order.log`, so an implementation that collects only one source per phase, orders the two sources the other way, or skips a phase writes a different file while still exiting 0. Measured at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-lifecycle-hooks-inline`: identical. The fixture's base is `debian:bookworm-slim` deliberately and must stay a base with no `remoteUser`: on `mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/base:bookworm` both CLIs uid-remap, and the reference stages its remap Dockerfile at a version-scoped global path (`/tmp/devcontainercli-runner/updateUID.Dockerfile-<version>`), so concurrent reference `up` runs race on the rename and one exits 1 with `ENOENT`. That made the case red for a reason indicting neither side, and only in CI — a host uid of 1000 matches `vscode` and skips the remap entirely. uid-remap coverage lives with the one case that claims it. |
| Object-form (parallel) lifecycle hooks declared by a Feature AND by the configuration both run, and a configuration hook whose command substitutes a binary a Feature installed resolves it. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e test fixtures at tag `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 1) — coverage chosen by the reference's authors rather than by ours. The harder forms of the same matrix: object-form `postCreateCommand` on both sources with one member a string and the other an array, array-form `postStart`/`postAttach` from the Features, and configuration hooks containing command substitution of Feature-installed binaries — which can only succeed if `install.sh` already ran and the hook's PATH carries its output. Sixteen marker paths are observed rather than an ordered log, deliberately: the parallel members of an object-form hook have no defined order between them, so presence is the only honest observable, and absence is captured as `exists:false` rather than as a gap. Measured at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-lifecycle-hooks-advanced`: identical. The fixture's base is `debian:bookworm-slim` deliberately and must stay a base with no `remoteUser`: on `mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/base:bookworm` both CLIs uid-remap, and the reference stages its remap Dockerfile at a version-scoped global path (`/tmp/devcontainercli-runner/updateUID.Dockerfile-<version>`), so concurrent reference `up` runs race on the rename and one exits 1 with `ENOENT`. That made the case red for a reason indicting neither side, and only in CI — a host uid of 1000 matches `vscode` and skips the remap entirely. uid-remap coverage lives with the one case that claims it. |
| On the single-container Dockerfile path, a `remoteUser` whose uid the image fixes is remapped to the host's, so a lifecycle hook running as that user can write to the bind-mounted workspace. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e test fixtures at tag `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 1) — coverage chosen by the reference's authors rather than by ours. The single-container analogue of the Compose case that found [#462](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/462). The image pins `foo` to uid 1234 — not a plausible host uid — so neither half of the claim can pass by coincidence: the marker exists only if the hook ran AS `remoteUser` and that user's uid was remapped. It records the NAME rather than `id -u`, because the remapped uid is the host's and a fixed assertion on it would be a statement about the host. Measured at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-updateuid-remoteuser`: both sides write `foo`. |
| `up --skip-post-create` defers EVERY lifecycle phase — `onCreate` and `updateContent` as well as `postCreate`, `postStart`, `postAttach` and dotfiles — to a later `run-user-commands`, which then runs each of them exactly once. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios + 1 Docker-gated test + 5 hermetic tests | Fixes [#476](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/476), surfaced while verifying #467. deacon ran the two base phases and deferred `postCreate` onward — what the flag's NAME says, and what deacon's own 008 FR-005/SC-003 had written down; the reference defers all five. **Adjudicated by the standing authority rule, not by a waiver ruling**: the flag is spec-silent (a CLI surface; `grep -r skip-post-create parity/spec/113500f4/` returns nothing), so the reference is the authority and the difference is deacon's. Transcribed from the pinned bundle rather than inferred: `up` puts `postCreateEnabled: !skipPostCreate` into its params, `EN(postCreateEnabled, …)` turns that into `lifecycleHook.enabled`, and the lifecycle runner is reached only through `params.lifecycleHook.enabled && await runPostCreateCommands(…)` — the ENTIRE chain (`onCreate` → `updateContent` → `postCreate` → dotfiles → `postStart` → `postAttach`) lives inside that one call, so the flag cannot reach a subset of it. The CLI's help text says the same in prose: "Do not run onCreateCommand, updateContentCommand, postCreateCommand, postStartCommand or postAttachCommand and do not install dotfiles". **The full matrix**, measured at oracle 0.87.0 against deacon at 66294dc on a fixture whose five hooks each append their phase name to one file — reference first, deacon second: plain `up` → all five / all five (agree); `up --skip-post-create` → NOTHING / `onCreate`, `updateContent` (the divergence); `up --skip-non-blocking-commands` → `onCreate`, `updateContent` / same (agree); `up --skip-post-create` then `run-user-commands` → all five once / the two base phases then all five again. That the two skip flags AGREE while only `--skip-post-create` diverges is the measurement that rules out the tempting shortcut of treating them as one rule: `--skip-non-blocking-commands` is a `waitFor` cutoff, `--skip-post-create` is an on/off gate over the whole runner. **The `run-user-commands` pairing** is the half that makes deferral safe, and it needed no change: a deferred `up` writes no completion markers, so the following `run-user-commands` runs all five exactly once on both sides. The marker mechanisms differ without changing that — deacon's are host-side (`{user_data_folder}/state/<workspace-hash>/`), the reference's container-side (`{containerDataFolder}/.<phase>CommandMarker`, keyed on the container's `createdAt`) — because neither side records a phase that never ran. The separately characterized difference on this pairing (deacon RE-runs a completed hook where the reference skips it, `wvr-ruc-completed-hooks-not-rerun`) is untouched, and is why the resume assertion is taken after a DEFERRED `up` rather than a plain one. **The fix is one function**: `LifecyclePhase::is_skipped_with_skip_post_create` now returns true for every phase, written as an exhaustive `match` so a phase added later must be classified deliberately. Everything else already routed through `InvocationContext::should_skip_phase`, so the single-container and compose paths changed together and no call site re-decides it — the property #460 bought when it deleted compose's local copy of the guard. NOT changed: `run-user-commands`'s own `--skip-post-create`, a deacon extension the reference has no counterpart for (its `run-user-commands` hard-codes `postCreateEnabled: true`), so there is no reference behavior to align it to; and `set-up --skip-post-create`, which already meant "skip everything" and already matched. **Two scenarios, split by an observation-timing constraint** worth writing down: `chan-filesystem` / `chan-file-content` are captured ONCE, after every operation of a case has run (`runner::execute_ops`, then the `capture_channel` loop) — the `operation` field on a filesystem expectation labels the evidence, it does not pick a point in time. The deferral itself therefore has to be observed by a case with ONE operation: `case-up-skip-post-create-defers-every-phase` (spec-expectation, so it runs on the PR- and release-gating `parity_docker` lane) runs only `up --skip-post-create` and asserts the log file is ABSENT. The resume half is `case-up-skip-post-create-run-user-commands-resume-differential`, where the end-of-run capture is exactly the right instant: it adds `run-user-commands` and asserts the log holds exactly the five phases — the line COUNT is what catches a leak, since before the fix deacon wrote seven. A first draft put both operations in ONE case and asserted `absent` on `op-up`; it failed on the first live run because by capture time `run-user-commands` had written the file — an assertion that looked like coverage and could only ever have passed by accident. **Watched to fail at three levels** before landing. Restoring the old two-phase classification turns `case-up-skip-post-create-defers-every-phase` red (`chan-filesystem: Diverge`); fails the compose-path Docker test `integration_compose_config_mounts::test_compose_up_skip_post_create_defers_every_phase` with `left: Some("onCreate-string\nupdateContent-array\n"), right: None`; and fails four of the five hermetic tests — the classification table (`core::lifecycle::tests::test_lifecycle_phase_helpers`), both orchestrator tests, and `up::lifecycle::tests::test_build_invocation_context_skip_post_create_mode`, which asserts the exact loop `execute_lifecycle_commands` walks. The three `up_dotfiles.rs` `--skip-post-create` tests were corrected in the same commit but stay `#[ignore]`d for the pre-existing reason their attribute gives (dotfiles are not in the MVP), so they are not counted as evidence. |
| `up --frozen-lockfile` accepts an on-disk lockfile that agrees SEMANTICALLY with the freshly-resolved Feature set, whatever serialisation it happens to use — and the lockfile deacon WRITES is byte-identical to the one the reference writes for the same input. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#557](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/557), which had two halves and closed with both, deliberately in one change: a tolerant comparison alone would have left the files deacon writes different from the reference's, and an emission change alone would have rewritten every existing deacon-written lockfile while the byte comparison was still in place. **The comparison.** `up::helpers::compare_lockfile_frozen` compared the on-disk file to a re-serialisation of the resolved lockfile BYTE FOR BYTE, so any key order, indentation or trailing-newline difference was reported as `Lockfile does not match.` — and the consequence was not hypothetical: deacon rejected every lockfile the reference CLI writes. It now parses both sides and compares them as DOCUMENTS (`deacon_core::lockfile::lockfile_text_matches`), which is the question the flag asks. The reference normalises the same way before comparing (`JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(raw), null, 2) + '\n'`) and carries a dedicated test for it, `frozen lockfile matches despite formatting differences`. Text that is not valid JSON still never matches, mirroring the reference's `try { … } catch {}`. **The emission order.** deacon alphabetised a Feature entry's keys; it now writes `version, resolved, integrity` (then `dependsOn`), which is the order the reference's `generateLockfile` builds its entry literal in and the order every example in `parity/spec/113500f4/devcontainer-lockfile.md` uses. `devcontainer-lockfile.md` prescribes the three properties and says nothing normative about their order, so this was a spec-silent output surface of the class [#486](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/486) and [#490](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/490) were; the maintainer ruled MATCH on 2026-08-11, and no tolerance was authored because there is now nothing to tolerate. All writers share one serialiser (`deacon_core::lockfile::canonical_lockfile_json`), so a lockfile's bytes do not depend on whether `up`, `build` or `upgrade` produced it — three copies of a recursive key-sort were the reason they could have. Feature ids stay SORTED, matching the reference's `.sort((a, b) => a.id.localeCompare(b.id))`; deacon sorts by code point, which agrees with `localeCompare` on every legal lowercase OCI reference measured, and chasing ICU exactly would pin the output to one ICU version and one host locale. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0, before and after, on `fx-upstream-lockfile-frozen`, whose committed `.devcontainer-lock.json` IS the reference's own output copied verbatim: before, the reference exited 0 and left it unchanged while deacon built the Feature image and exited 1; after, both exit 0 and leave it byte-unchanged. Byte-identity of the WRITE path was measured separately, on a fresh workspace (`up` writes the fixture's exact 327 bytes) and on a four-Feature config resolved through `upgrade --dry-run` (`common-utils`, `git-lfs`, `git`, `node` — declared in a different order, emitted `diff`-clean against the reference's file). `case-up-upstream-lockfile-frozen-matching` asserts exit 0 and compares `chan-file-content` across both CLIs, so a frozen run that started REWRITING the file would also be caught. Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e fixtures at `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 2). |
| `up --frozen-lockfile` on a configuration that declares no Features neither refuses nor writes a lockfile — the container comes up and the flag is simply never reached. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 against a freshly built `main` at `e107a92`, on a three-line `{"image": "debian:bookworm-slim"}` with no `.devcontainer-lock.json` on disk: BOTH CLIs exit 0, both print a `success` result document naming a container they created, and NEITHER writes `.devcontainer-lock.json`. This is the carve-out [#565](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/pull/565) introduced incidentally while fixing [#556](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/556) — before it, deacon exited 1 with `Lockfile does not exist.` and created nothing, a refusal the reference never makes. The change was disclosed and the with-Features rows above were updated, but they document the WITH-Features case; the featureless path had no row and no scenario until this one, so the ledger's own rule (every measured behaviour gets both) was owed a debt here. TRANSCRIBED rather than inferred, because an exit code does not say why: `--frozen-lockfile` is consulted only inside `writeLockfile` (`mQ`), whose single caller `generateFeaturesConfig` (`UQ`) opens with `let o = BV(xQ(t, i), r); if (!o) return;`, and `xQ` (`userFeaturesToArray`) returns `undefined` when the configuration's `features` and the `additionalFeatures` overlay are both empty. With no Features declared the reference returns before the lockfile is read, so the flag is never reached rather than satisfied. deacon spells that early return once, as `commands/shared/lockfile::declares_features`, shared by `up` and `build` so the two cannot drift. `case-up-frozen-lockfile-no-features` is `spec-expectation` on purpose and the LANE is the reason: a `live-differential` case routes to `parity_differential`, which runs in the nightly ONLY and gates no pull request, and a case whose whole point is that nothing gated this behaviour belongs where it gates — `docker-shared` + `spec-expectation` puts it in `parity_docker`, selected by the release gate and by every Docker-provisioned profile. Its three channels are each load-bearing: `chan-exit-code: 0` is the regression signal itself, `chan-structured-output`'s `outcome: success` is what makes that 0 mean the container came up rather than something quietly declining to happen, and `chan-filesystem` `absent` catches the other failure shape — exiting 0 by WRITING a lockfile would satisfy both other assertions while doing the one thing the flag exists to prevent. One adjacency is claimed by the row below rather than here: the reference's early return keys off the UNION of the configuration's Features and `--additional-features`, and deacon's gate once read the configuration alone, so Features supplied by the flag were refused only after the Feature image had been built ([#569](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/569), CLOSED — the classification arrived and the ruling was that it is a defect). |
| `up --frozen-lockfile` refuses a workspace with no lockfile, creates no container, and writes no lockfile. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-lockfile-no-lockfile`: both CLIs exit 1 before creating anything and neither writes `.devcontainer-lock.json`. deacon validates pre-build in `commands/up/mod.rs` and reports `Lockfile does not exist.` with the expected path and how to generate one; the reference reports the same sentence as its result document's `message`. The error ENVELOPES differ in shape (deacon puts the sentence in `description` under a generic `message`), so `chan-structured-output` is deliberately not declared — it would report an envelope difference this case is not about. The filesystem claim is what makes the case more than an exit code: a run that refused AFTER writing the lockfile would satisfy `equals: 1` having already done the thing the flag exists to prevent. This row was the green control that gave [#556](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/556) its shape — `up` implemented the gate `build` did not, which is what made that defect a missing wire rather than a missing capability. Both subcommands now reach the gate through one helper (`commands/shared/lockfile`), so this row and its `build` sibling can no longer drift apart. Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e fixtures at `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 2). |
| `up --frozen-lockfile` refuses BEFORE any daemon work when the Features arrive via `--additional-features` rather than the configuration. | matches the reference | no scenario (see below) + 1 hermetic test | Fixes [#569](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/569), filed with a measurement and held open for a maintainer classification; the ruling is that it is a defect. MEASURED on current `main` at `1897806` against oracle 0.87.0, on a three-line `{"image": "debian:bookworm-slim"}` with no `.devcontainer-lock.json`, both sides run with `--frozen-lockfile --additional-features '{"ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/git:1.3.2":{}}'`. BEFORE: both exit 1 and neither writes a lockfile, but deacon resolved the Feature and BUILT the Feature-extended image first (`naming to docker.io/library/deacon-devcontainer-features:8778b3ce`), where the reference emitted ZERO `Start: Run: docker …` lines. AFTER: deacon's stderr contains no mention of Docker at all. TRANSCRIBED for the cause: the reference consults `--frozen-lockfile` only inside `writeLockfile` (`mQ`), reached from the single caller `generateFeaturesConfig` (`UQ`), whose early return tests `userFeaturesToArray` (`xQ`) — and `xQ` returns early only when the configuration's `features` AND `additionalFeatures` are BOTH empty, i.e. it keys off the UNION. deacon's mirror of that early return was handed `config.features()` alone. The fix is an ORDERING change, not a new gate: `up` now applies the `--additional-features` merge before `ensure_lockfile_usable`, so the gate is handed the same union. `build` already ordered these two this way and was already correct — measured, contradicting the issue's assumption that both subcommands shared the shape — so this brings `up` into line with its sibling rather than changing both. NO PARITY CASE, stated plainly rather than papered over: exit code, the result document's substance and the workspace bytes ALL agreed before the fix, and the only thing that differed — an intermediate image left on the daemon — is observable on no declared channel. `chan-image` captures the `.Config` of the image an operation PRODUCED, and this operation produces none, so it is `not_captured` on both sides. Rather than invent a channel or assert something weaker and call it covered, the ordering is pinned by a hermetic Rust test (`up_lockfile_frozen::frozen_lockfile_refuses_additional_features_before_touching_docker`) that runs `up` with an EMPTIED `PATH`: any daemon access then fails loudly with `Docker is not installed or not accessible`, so reaching `Lockfile does not exist.` proves nothing touched Docker first. It was watched to fail — before the fix it received exactly that Docker error. |
| A top-level `name:` AUTHORED in the Compose file is used verbatim as the project name, overriding either CLI's own derivation. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-compose-with-name` and `fx-upstream-compose-with-name-custom-yaml`: both CLIs report `custom-project-name` and `custom-project-name-custom-yaml` respectively, taken straight from the compose document. This is the row that keeps `bhv-compose-project-name-robust` (#265, refined by #564) honest: deacon's `deacon_<folderStem>_<workspaceHash>_<configHash>` is a DEFAULT for when nobody said, never a right to overrule an author. `derive_project_name` consults an explicit `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` in a sibling `.env` and then the compose file's `name:` BEFORE any derivation; the reference reaches the same answer through its `Rp` resolver, which reads `name` off `docker compose config`'s output. Different mechanisms, same answer — which is what a differential is for. The distinction needed its own scenarios because the existing Compose differential (`case-up-compose-project-resources-differential`) TOLERATES `composeProjectResources.project`, its fixture authoring no name, so nothing in the suite said what happens when one is authored. The two new cases tolerate neither project path — that omission IS the claim — and additionally pin deacon's side to the literal name with a `jsonSubset` assertion, because a differential alone would pass if both CLIs regressed to deriving and happened to derive the same thing. The `-custom-yaml` fixture carries the same claim through a document a general YAML reader cannot parse (`ports: !reset []`, a Compose-specific tag): neither CLI parses it — the reference never did, and since [#572](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/572) deacon reads the `name:` LINE only to establish that a name was AUTHORED and takes the VALUE from `docker compose config`, so Compose does the understanding on both sides. Both fixtures were re-measured after that change and neither name moved. Only THREE of the five `ext-container-identity-labels` keys are tolerated — `devcontainer.name` and `devcontainer.config_name` derive from an authored `"name"` property these configs do not declare, so declaring them would be stale bookkeeping; measured, not assumed. Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e fixtures at `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 3). The batch-2 inventory carried this group as blocked on a maintainer ruling, on the assumption that it collided with the namespaced-name choice; it does not, and the reclassification is what unblocked it. |
| A Compose file's top-level `name:` is INTERPOLATED before use, so `name: ${VAR}` becomes the variable's value. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | [#572](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/572), CLOSED. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-compose-with-name-env-var` (`name: ${CUSTOM_NAME}`, with `CUSTOM_NAME=custom-name-with-env-var` in a sibling `.env`): the reference exits 0 with `composeProjectName: "custom-name-with-env-var"`; deacon exited 1 before creating anything — `invalid project name "${CUSTOM_NAME}": must consist only of lowercase alphanumeric characters, hyphens, and underscores…` — having passed the raw template through as `--project-name`. Re-measured after the fix, deacon brings the project up as `custom-name-with-env-var`, the reference's answer exactly. The cause was structural rather than a missed case: `compose.rs::parse_compose_file_for_project_name` is a LINE-WISE reader — scan for an unindented `name:`, strip quotes, return the rest — and there was no interpolation because there was no evaluator. **The fix does not build one.** An authored name is now resolved by asking `docker compose config` for its `name`, which is where the reference's `Rp` resolver has always taken it from (step 3 of four). The line-wise reader survives in a strictly narrower job — deciding whether a name was AUTHORED at all — because Compose's answer cannot distinguish an authored name from its own project-directory default, and adopting that default would overrule deacon's namespaced derivation (`bhv-compose-project-name-robust`, #265/#564). The reference has the same problem and solves it the same way, by re-reading the compose files whenever Compose answers with the literal `devcontainer`. **The issue left the design open on one question — what happens when Compose is unavailable — and it was settled by measuring rather than by argument.** `docker compose config` is a CLIENT-SIDE call: with `DOCKER_HOST` pointed at a nonexistent socket it still exits 0 and still reports the interpolated name, so the dependency added is on the `docker` binary and its compose plugin, both of which every compose flow already requires, not on a reachable daemon. And the reference's compose `up` fails outright when that call fails — measured with `CUSTOM_NAME` unset, where Compose itself refuses with `project name must not be empty` and the reference exits 1 with "An error occurred retrieving the Docker Compose configuration." deacon now does the same, naming the authored template and the mechanism that failed. There is no fallback on purpose: passing the template through is the defect this replaced, and quietly deriving a name instead would ignore what the author wrote. Two consequences fell out and were measured too. Compose NORMALIZES the name it reports (`Custom.Name Upper` → `customnameupper`), which the reference reproduces by hand in its `Rg` sanitizer, so deacon now gets that agreement for free on interpolated names. And `--env-file` participates, because a `--env-file` replaces Compose's default `.env` discovery: the env files are threaded into project creation rather than attached afterwards, so `up`, `exec` and `down` cannot land on different names. `case-up-upstream-compose-with-name-env-var` was RED ON PURPOSE asserting exit 0 and turned green on the fix with its assertion unchanged; it declares `chan-exit-code` alone, which was sufficient rather than a compromise (the container cannot start unless the project name is legal, and the only legal name reachable from this document is the interpolated one) and which is why `chan-container-state` was left to the new spec-expectation twin `case-up-upstream-compose-with-name-env-var-spec`. The twin needs no oracle, so it runs on the GATING `parity_docker` lane rather than the nightly differential, and with no oracle there is nothing to tolerate — it pins `composeProjectResources.project` to `custom-name-with-env-var` outright. It was watched RED against a pre-fix binary, failing because no container state was captured at all. Mined from the reference CLI's own e2e fixtures at `v0.87.0` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480), batch 3). |
| An explicit `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` is honored from every source Compose reads it from, in Compose's order: the process environment, then the workspace folder's `.env`, then the compose project directory's. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios + 6 hermetic unit tests | [#580](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/580), CLOSED. deacon looked in exactly ONE place — a `.env` in the workspace folder — which turned out to be the MIDDLE of three. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 against deacon at `f6b7c10`, on a compose document authoring no `name:`, setting the variable in one place at a time: process environment → reference `env-wins`, deacon derived `deacon_proc_*`; workspace `.env` → both `from-workspace`; `.devcontainer/.env` → reference `from-configdir`, deacon derived `deacon_cfg_*`; both `.env` files → both `from-workspace`; process env AND workspace `.env` → reference `env-wins`, deacon `from-workspace`. Re-measured after the fix, all five rows agree. **That last row is why the fix is an ORDERED search and not three independent reads**, and it is the part the issue did not have: the two missing sources sit on either side of the one deacon had, so adding them without the order would have traded a missing-source defect for a precedence defect. The third source is Compose's OWN default `.env` discovery — Compose reads a `.env` from the project directory, which absent `--project-directory` (deacon never passes one) is the directory of the first `-f` file, i.e. `.devcontainer` for the standard layout. That is the same file `fx-upstream-compose-with-name-env-var` already relies on to interpolate `${CUSTOM_NAME}`, which is worth stating plainly: Compose was reading a file deacon could not, on a path deacon never asked it about. **The obvious fix was the wrong one.** Delegating the question to `docker compose config`, as the authored-`name:` branch does, answers `from-configdir` when both `.env` files are present and would therefore be wrong in exactly the case the configdir scenario alone would call correct — Compose cannot see the workspace `.env` at all. So the sources are read directly, in order, and the no-authored-name path still spawns no subprocess (`bhv-compose-project-name-robust` depends on that). `--env-file` REPLACES the default discovery rather than adding to it, so supplied env files are searched instead of the project directory's `.env`, last-wins as Compose merges them; that rule is reasoned from Compose's documented behavior rather than measured, and is marked as such in the code. Values are taken verbatim from every source, matching the reference and matching what deacon already did with the workspace `.env`. **One of the three symptoms is not expressible as a scenario and is deliberately not claimed by one**: an `Operation` declares `argv`, `fixtures` and `stdin` and has no field for process environment variables, so both that the variable is honored and that it OUTRANKS a workspace `.env` are pinned in Rust instead — which is why `derive_project_name` takes the value as a parameter rather than reading `std::env::var` itself (`unsafe_code = "deny"` rules out `set_var`, and a global read would make the precedence unassertable). The two scenarios are `case-up-compose-project-name-env-configdir-spec`, watched RED against a pre-fix binary where it failed on the project name and not the exit code, and `case-up-compose-project-name-env-workspace-wins-spec`, which passed BEFORE the fix as well — that is what makes it worth having, since its job is to stop the new lower-priority sources from being inserted above the one that already worked. Both are `spec-expectation` on `docker-shared`, so they gate. |
| `up --skip-post-attach` defers EXACTLY ONE phase — every other lifecycle hook still runs. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Mined from `src/test/cli.exec.base.ts` at v0.87.0 ('should not run postAttachCommand when --skip-post-attach is given'), [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 6. The narrow sibling of `--skip-post-create`, and worth measuring rather than inferring precisely because the WIDE one was a defect: deacon read `--skip-post-create` off its name and ran the two base phases anyway (#476), so the family's other member deserved its own measurement. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-up-skip-post-create-phases`, the same five-phase fixture #476 uses: both CLIs exit 0 and both leave `onCreate\nupdateContent\npostCreate\npostStart\n` — four lines, `postAttach` absent. deacon needed no fix; this row is the record that the narrow flag is narrow. Asserted by `case-up-upstream-skip-post-attach`, which carries the exact content IN ADDITION to the differential (FR-043) so that the flag over-reaching and the flag under-reaching are distinguishable failures rather than one blurred one. Upstream's restart arm needed a container stop/start primitive the operation vocabulary did not have; it does now. EXTENDED by [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480): `case-up-restart-skip-post-attach` composes this gate with a restart, which is upstream's own second arm of the same `it` and was unexpressible until the stop/start primitive landed. It matters because the two gates are implemented in different places and the obvious defect is one silently disabling the other — a restart path that re-derived which phases to run, rather than reusing the gated list, would run `postAttach` on the second `up` while the first correctly skipped it, and every single-`up` case in the suite would stay green. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: both CLIs leave `onCreate\nupdateContent\npostCreate\npostStart\npostStart\n` — `postStart` twice (the restart phase working), `postCreate` once (the restart gate holding), `postAttach` never (this flag holding across both runs). |
| `up --skip-non-blocking-commands` stops after the phase `waitFor` names — reading the field, not hardcoding its `updateContentCommand` default. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | The third member of the lifecycle-gate flag family and the one with NO upstream test behind it at all: `grep -rn 'skip-non-blocking\|waitFor' src/test/` at v0.87.0 returns nothing, so neither CLI's own suite exercises it. Found while mining the other two, because THE TWO HELP TEXTS DO NOT SAY THE SAME THING — the reference's reads "Stop running user commands after running the command configured with waitFor or the updateContentCommand by default", deacon's reads "Skip non-blocking commands (postStart & postAttach phases)". Those coincide only when `waitFor` is `postCreateCommand`, so under the spec default the two wordings predict different files. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 before anything was filed: on the default, both CLIs exit 0 and both leave exactly `onCreate\nupdateContent\n`; on a fixture authoring `"waitFor": "onCreateCommand"`, both leave exactly `onCreate\n`. deacon implements `waitFor`; only its `--help` string is written as though it did not, which is a documentation gap rather than a divergence and is deliberately NOT filed as a defect. TWO scenarios because one cannot carry the claim: `case-up-skip-non-blocking-commands-wait-for-default` runs under the default, where "reads the field" and "hardcodes the default" predict the SAME file, and `case-up-skip-non-blocking-commands-wait-for-on-create` moves `waitFor` one phase earlier so the two readings disagree. The second is the discriminator; the first alone would have been an assertion that could not fail for the reason it was written. This is also the configuration in which a later "fix" aligning the behaviour to deacon's own help text would be a silent regression — the assertions are what would catch it. |

The PARSE half has been covered since batch 1 by `case-readconfig-upstream-initialize-command-object`, whose own notes say the quiet part out loud: "the operation is a configuration read, so nothing is executed on either side and the case measures the parse alone." MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: the reference logs `Running 'touch-test1' from initializeCommand...` and `'touch-test2'` interleaved and leaves both host markers; deacon, given `--trust-workspace`, exits 0 and leaves both. Asserted by `case-up-initialize-command-object-executes`, which is `spec-expectation` RATHER THAN `live-differential` for a reason that is itself the point: deacon gates every host-side hook behind the workspace-trust check, a deacon extension the reference has no equivalent for, and measured on this fixture WITHOUT the flag deacon exits 1 with `WorkspaceUntrusted` while the reference exits 0 and runs the hooks. The runner sends ONE argv to both sides by construction, so no invocation both trusts deacon and stays legal for the reference. This is the first case in the suite that executes a host hook at all, so it pins the TRUSTED path — the one a developer actually takes — end to end, and being oracle-free it runs on `parity_docker`, which gates pull requests and the release. |
| Bringing a STOPPED container back up re-runs `postStartCommand` and `postAttachCommand` but NOT `postCreateCommand` — the create phases stay completed across a restart. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | The single most-repeated claim in the reference's `cli.exec.base.ts` — upstream asserts it five times over different configuration shapes — and until [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) landed the stop/start primitive, NOTHING in this suite asserted it in any form. It is the property that makes the create-phase marker mechanism worth having: an implementation that re-ran `postCreate` on every restart would re-install, re-seed and re-migrate on every machine reboot, and would still exit 0 every time. It could not be expressed before because the operation vocabulary had no way to stop a container without removing it, and `down` is not a substitute — it removes, so the next `up` recreates and `postCreate` re-runs by definition, which is the negation of the claim. `Operation::stopContainerBefore` is the primitive, scoped to the side's own workspace label so the two sides of a differential cannot stop each other's containers; stopping nothing is a fail-loud `ObservationFault` rather than a no-op, because an operation that silently ran against a fresh container would measure a first create while reporting as a restart. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on both shapes: both CLIs exit 0 from both operations, both REATTACH to the same container id, and both leave `onCreate\nupdateContent\npostCreate\npostStart\npostAttach\npostStart\npostAttach\n` — seven lines, the three create-side phases exactly once and the two start-side phases twice. TWO scenarios because the two paths reach the restart by different mechanisms: `case-up-restart-timing-single-container` through `docker start` on a container, `case-up-restart-timing-compose` through `docker compose up` against an existing project — and compose is where deacon has historically diverged (#448, #265/#564, #572 all bit compose and not the single-container path), so a create/restart distinction that held for one and not the other would otherwise be invisible. The compose case is specifically upstream's *stop individual container* shape: Compose sidecars carry no `devcontainer.local_folder` label, so the primitive stops the dev service and leaves the project up. The whole-project variant asserts the same markers and is deliberately not landed — it would buy a container build per nightly and no new information. Both carry the exact content in ADDITION to the differential (FR-043): a comparison alone would stay green if BOTH CLIs started re-running `postCreate`, and this is a claim the spec supports independently, since a phase named for container CREATION cannot correctly run on a restart. Watched failing: removing `stopContainerBefore` from the single-container case turns it red at `chan-file-content.lifecycle-phases.txt`, so the primitive is known to be load-bearing rather than decorative. |

The defect was OUTPUT-ONLY — measured on the same container, `deacon exec` injected `TEST_RE=TEST_VALUE3` correctly — so what was wrong was the document a consumer reads, not the environment deacon builds. Fail-safe by construction: an unreadable container environment SKIPS the pass, because `resolve_variable` answers `Some("")` for a missing key once `container_env` is `Some`, and a caller cannot tell an empty string from a failed resolution. Measured after the fix against oracle 0.87.0 on the same fixture and machine: both blocks match key-for-key on `remoteEnv`, and the full result-document diff against the pre-fix run is exactly the two resolved values in each block and nothing else. Asserted by `case-up-upstream-include-configuration`, which carried the case for this claim from the day it was opened and now carries the two `TEST_RE` assertions upstream's own e2e test makes. `CONTAINER_PATH` (`${containerEnv:PATH}`) is resolved by the same pass and measured identical on both CLIs, but stays unasserted for the reason `LOCAL_PATH` does: its value is the base image's `PATH`, a statement about `debian:bookworm-slim` rather than about deacon. | A backslash in a Compose `containerEnv` value reaches the container as a backslash. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | CLOSED by [#609](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/609). Was an open nonconformance, MEASURED byte for byte at oracle 0.87.0: the configuration authors `value with \back slash`; the reference delivered exactly that and deacon delivered `value with ` + **0x08** + `ack slash` — the backslash and the `b` collapsed into a BACKSPACE. `escape_yaml_value` in `crates/core/src/compose.rs` emitted a DOUBLE-QUOTED YAML scalar down both of its branches and escaped on only one; a value with no newline, colon, hash, quote or edge space took the other branch and its backslash was then read by the YAML parser as an escape. The fix deletes the predicate — every value is emitted double-quoted, therefore every value is escaped — which is a no-op for anything that needed no escaping and so narrows to exactly the mis-escaped values. Re-measured through `od -c` at the fix: both CLIs now deliver the identical bytes. The same defect at its worst was a lone TRAILING backslash, which escaped the closing quote and produced an override Compose could not parse at all; the reverted-fix perturbation reproduces the 0x08 on demand. COMPOSE-ONLY, and measured rather than assumed — the identical value on the identical key arrives intact on the image path (row above). One CLI, one input, two answers depending on which path built the container — so the flattening reads as an artifact of its Compose YAML serialization (the same code neighborhood as the backslash defect deacon fixed in #609, one row above) rather than as a designed behavior. Matching it would mean deliberately corrupting user data: a PEM key, a multi-line banner, any authored multi-line value. **Recorded as a documented choice and NOT as deacon-follows-the-spec**, deliberately. `devcontainerjson-reference.md:15` defines `containerEnv` as "a set of name-value pairs that sets or overrides environment variables" and says nothing about the bytes of a value; on a spec-silent output surface the reference is normally the authority and the difference would be deacon's. This row is the exception the maintainer ruled, which is exactly what `docs/PARITY.md` reserves a deliberate spec-silent divergence for — so it is written down as a choice made against the reference's authority, not dressed up as spec compliance. **The scenario is a `live-differential` with ONE tolerance, and the shape is the point.** `case-up-upstream-containerenv-compose-newline` runs `up` on `fx-upstream-containerenv-dollar-escape` and compares `chan-injected-process` across the two CLIs, tolerating exactly `chan-injected-process.env.Test_Message` (`bhv-compose-containerenv-newline-fidelity`). A spec-expectation would have pinned deacon's value and said nothing about the reference, so the day upstream stops flattening, nothing would notice and this row would quietly become fiction; a tolerance instead re-measures the divergence every nightly run and FAILS AS STALE the moment it stops reproducing, which is how the record retires itself. The case still carries an assertion — evaluated against deacon's side alone — because a differential cannot fail when both sides are equally wrong, and deacon's half here is a positive claim. **The channel was chosen by measurement, not by preference.** On this fixture the two CLIs' `.Config` process contexts are identical but for that one name: same `user`, `cwd`, `tty`, `PATH`, and the same nine other `containerEnv` values, so `chan-injected-process` costs exactly one tolerance. `chan-container-state` would have cost eight — the keep-alive `cmd`/`entrypoint` split, deacon's five identity labels, the Compose project name and its `config_files`/`config-hash` pair — for a case whose entire subject is one string, which is why batch 7 declined to write it at all and left the key unasserted instead. |

Three independent discriminators, all carried by one exit code: the root config's marker `/postCreateCommand.txt` must be ABSENT, the subfolder's `/subfolderConfigPostCreateCommand.txt` PRESENT, and `SUBFOLDER_CONFIG_IMAGE_ENV=true` — set by the subfolder's Dockerfile and only it — present in the environment. The absence check is what makes it more than a smoke test: an implementation that honoured `--config` for the build and fell back to discovery for the lifecycle would leave BOTH markers and pass an assertion naming only the subfolder one. `exec` carries the same `--config`, which is load-bearing — the two documents produce different container identities.
