| `up` against a `--workspace-folder` that does not exist exits 1, says which path it could not resolve, and still prints an `{"outcome": "error"}` result document. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The first `up` case in the suite that is HERMETIC — no `resourceGroup`, so it runs on every profile including `dev-fast` and on all three operating systems, joining the two `exec` negatives in gating every pull request. The lane is a claim, not a convenience: workspace resolution happens before any daemon contact, verified by re-running with `docker` off `PATH` and `DOCKER_HOST` at a nonexistent socket and getting identical output. It pins DEACON's answer rather than comparing it, exactly as its twin `case-exec-missing-workspace-rejected` does — MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0, both CLIs exit 1 and both print an `outcome: error` document, but deacon names the directory it could not resolve (`Failed to resolve workspace path …`) and the reference names a file it never looked for (`Dev container config (…) not found.`), because it never checks the directory. deacon's is the more accurate of the two, so the wording is not a divergence to fix and a differential here would need a tolerance to say only that the two disagree about phrasing. `case-up-upstream-missing-workspace-rejected`, mined from `src/test/cli.up.test.ts` at v0.87.0 ('should fail with "not found" error when config is not found'), [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 7. |
| With no `--workspace-folder`, `--container-id` or `--id-label`, `up` resolves its configuration from the CURRENT DIRECTORY. | matches the reference | 2 scenarios | FIXED — was open nonconformance against [#610](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/610). MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 in a directory holding a devcontainer config, before the fix: the reference exited 0 with a `containerId`; deacon exited 1 with `Missing required argument: One of --container-id, --id-label or --workspace-folder is required.` and never looked at the cwd. It was an INTERNAL inconsistency as much as a parity one — deacon's `exec` already defaulted to the cwd (`case-exec-default-workspace-folder`, `case-exec-upstream-no-config-in-cwd-rejected`) and so did `build` (`case-build-upstream-cwd-default-workspace`); `up` and `read-configuration` were the two that demanded the flag; `read-configuration` was closed separately by [#615](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/615), which reuses the helper this fix introduced, so no subcommand demands it now. `up` now materializes the default ONCE at its entry, ahead of argument validation, rather than inside `load_config`: the workspace folder is read on five further paths — argument validation, container identity hashing, the workspace-trust gate (whose allowlist is keyed on the CANONICALIZED path), the `--mount-workspace-git-root` mount-source walk and compose project naming — and all of them read the argument rather than the loader's result, so materializing it up front is what makes a defaulted cwd indistinguishable from an explicit `--workspace-folder $(pwd)` on every one of them. `down` and `run-user-commands` were audited alongside and needed no change: both already defaulted to the cwd. Two scenarios: `case-up-upstream-cwd-default-workspace`, the `live-differential` that was red on purpose and turned green on the fix rather than on an assertion edit, and `case-up-upstream-cwd-default-workspace-success`, the `spec-expectation` sibling the red case called for, which carries the claim into `parity_docker` where it gates every pull request instead of only the nightly. Mined from `src/test/cli.up.test.ts` at v0.87.0 ('should create and start container using current directory config'), [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 7. |
| `up` with no `--workspace-folder`, no `--id-label` and NO devcontainer config in the current directory exits 1 for the RIGHT reason — a missing config, not a missing flag. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Upstream's NEGATIVE arm of the cwd default ('should fail gracefully when no config in current directory'), held back from [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 7 on purpose and landed with the fix for [#610](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/610). Before that fix both CLIs exited 1 here, so the assertion would have been green while deacon failed on a missing FLAG and the reference on a missing CONFIG — an assertion that cannot fail for the reason it was written, the trap `docs/PARITY.md` names. It is worth landing now precisely because the reason changed. DISTINCT from `bhv-up-missing-workspace-rejected`, which names a `--workspace-folder` that does not exist and fails during workspace RESOLUTION before any config is looked for; this one resolves the workspace fine and finds no document, the path a developer meets by `cd`-ing into the wrong directory. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 in an empty directory: both CLIs exit 1 and both print an `outcome: error` document naming the absolute path they looked for, but they word it differently — deacon `No devcontainer.json found in workspace` / `Configuration file not found: <abs>/.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`, the reference `Dev container config (<abs>/.devcontainer/devcontainer.json) not found.` So `case-up-upstream-no-config-in-cwd-rejected` pins DEACON's answer rather than comparing, on the precedent of `bhv-up-missing-workspace-rejected` and the `exec` negatives; the `outcome: error` half is compared in effect, being written against the shape both CLIs emit. The wording gap is `up`'s own and not shared with `exec`, which words it identically to the reference — aligning `up` would mean changing the `message` field of its `outcome: error` document, a user-facing contract with its own tests, and is a separate decision from #610. HERMETIC (no `resourceGroup`): config resolution fails before any daemon contact, verified with `docker` off `PATH` and `DOCKER_HOST` pointed at a nonexistent socket, so it is the second `up` case to gate every pull request and it runs on all three operating systems. |
| An OCI Feature reference carrying NO version (`ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/git`) INSTALLS at `up` time, defaulting to `latest`, and the container that results honours the same configuration's `remoteUser` and `remoteEnv`. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | THE ITEM BATCH 7 PROMISED — upstream's 'should execute successfully with valid config with a Feature', named and deliberately not taken then because the fixture could not be brought up. `case-readconfig-upstream-feature-no-version-tag` proves the untagged id RESOLVES; nothing had ever installed one, and resolution reads a manifest while installation pulls a layer and runs `install.sh` inside a build. The assertion is falsifiable rather than a smoke test: an untagged reference that does not default to `latest` is a 404 against a tag that does not exist, so the Feature layer never builds and `up` exits 1. The `exec` line carries three claims — `id -un` is `node`, `TEST_REMOTE_ENV` arrives, and `command -v git` resolves to the Feature's build-from-source `/usr/local/bin/git` rather than the distribution's `/usr/bin/git`. FIXTURE ADAPTED, recorded in its `UPSTREAM.md`: batch 1 had repinned the base to `debian:bookworm-slim`, where the `node` user upstream's `remoteUser` names does not exist, so `up` failed for a reason unrelated to the claim; the base is now `mcr.microsoft.com/devcontainers/typescript-node:1-20-bookworm`, already named by the fixture tree and the same image/user pairing upstream itself relies on. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: both CLIs exit 0, report `remoteUser: node`, print the same three lines, and write the AUTHORED untagged id into `devcontainer.metadata` (`{"id":"ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/git"}`, no `:latest` appended) — agreeing with [#490](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/490)'s ruling that the declared reference is what gets reported. `case-up-upstream-feature-no-version-tag-installs`, mined from `src/test/cli.up.test.ts` at v0.87.0, [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 8. |
| A `mounts` entry in the object form requesting an ANONYMOUS volume — `type: volume` with a `target` and no `source` — creates the volume and mounts it. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | FIXED — was open nonconformance against [#617](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/617). The spec defers this property to Docker verbatim — 'Each value is a string that accepts the same values as the Docker CLI `--mount` flag' (`parity/spec/113500f4/devcontainerjson-reference.md:27`) — and omitting `source` on `type=volume` is precisely how that flag asks for an anonymous volume. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on `fx-upstream-mount-object-anonymous-volume` before the fix: the reference exited 0 and `docker inspect` showed a second mount, `Type: volume`, `Destination: /home/test_devcontainer_config`, `Name` a fresh 64-hex anonymous id; deacon exited 1 before creating anything — `Invalid mount in config: type=volume,target=/home/test_devcontainer_config: … volume mount requires a source`. `Mount::validate` in `crates/core/src/mount.rs` required a `source` for `MountType::Volume` as well as for `MountType::Bind`; a bind genuinely needs one, a volume does not. The fix splits the two arms rather than loosening the check: `bind` still fails fast without a source, an EMPTY `source=` stays an error on both types (a typo, not a request), and the short `source:target` form gains an explicit rejection for a leading colon so it cannot back into the anonymous shape by accident. Re-measured at the fix on the same fixture: deacon exits 0 and `docker inspect` returns the reference's shape, `Name` a fresh 64-hex id. It was an INTERNAL inconsistency as much as a parity one — the CLI's own `--mount type=volume,target=…` demanded a source through a SECOND validator (`NormalizedMount::parse` in `commands/up/args.rs`), and the compose path could not have rendered the mount even once it parsed: an empty source produced the malformed short form `- :/target` and a nameless top-level `volumes:` key, so it now emits the compose LONG form, which is the only spelling of an anonymous volume compose accepts. All three ingresses — object-form config, string-form config, `--mount` — and both runtime paths were fixed together. ANOTHER fixture vendored in batch 1 from an execution test (`src/test/imageMetadata.test.ts`, 'docker volume should not be named undefined if the src argument is omitted in mount command') and covered only by a `read-configuration` case, which is green because both sides ECHO the object-form mount identically. `case-up-upstream-mount-object-anonymous-volume` observes through `/proc/mounts` inside the container rather than `chan-container-state`, because the volume's name is a fresh random id on each side by construction and a whole-channel comparison would diverge every run while saying nothing about behaviour — and a real filesystem mounted at the target is a stronger claim than upstream's 'not named undefined' anyway. `live-differential`, so it was red in the nightly lane and turned green on the fix rather than on an assertion edit. Mined from the reference's e2e suite at v0.87.0 ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 8). |
| `updateRemoteUserUID` does NOT remap `remoteUser` onto a UID another user already owns: when the host UID is taken inside the image, the remote user keeps its own UID and stays the identity the configuration named. When only the GID is taken, the UID moves and the remote user keeps its OLD GID. | matches the reference | 1 scenario, 1 Docker-gated test, 9 hermetic tests | Was an open nonconformance ([#618](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/618)), closed 2026-08-22. The reference's own `scripts/updateUID.Dockerfile` has carried both guards since before the pin, and deacon had neither: `elif [ "$OLD_UID" != "$NEW_UID" -a -n "$EXISTING_USER" ]; then echo "User with UID exists ($EXISTING_USER=$NEW_UID)."` refuses the remap OUTRIGHT when another `/etc/passwd` entry already holds the target uid, and inside the branch that does remap, `if [ "$OLD_GID" != "$NEW_GID" -a -n "$EXISTING_GROUP" ]; then NEW_GID="$OLD_GID"` keeps the old gid when another `/etc/group` entry holds the target one. `crates/core/src/user_mapping.rs` remapped whenever the uid or gid differed, with no check that either target was free. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 before the fix, host uid:gid 1000:1000. Reference `/etc/passwd`: `foo:x:1000:1000` and `bar:x:1001:1001`; `id -un` prints `bar` and the three lifecycle markers are owned by `bar`. deacon: `foo:x:1000:1000` and **`bar:x:1000:1000`** — two entries sharing uid 1000 — so every name lookup for 1000 returned `foo`. `id -un` printed `foo`, the markers were owned by `foo`, and even an explicit `deacon exec --user bar` landed on `foo`, while deacon's own `up` result document still reported `"remoteUser": "bar"`. NOT compose-specific: reproduced on the single-container path with the same two-user image. THE FIX IS THE REFERENCE'S DECISION TABLE, LIFTED WHOLE. `decide_uid_remap` is a pure function over (current uid/gid, target uid/gid, who owns the target uid, who owns the target gid) returning `NoChange` / `SkippedUidTaken` / `Remap`, branch-for-branch with the shell above; the container half is two `UserMapper` lookups (`find_user_by_uid`, `find_group_by_gid`) that read `/etc/passwd` and `/etc/group` directly with `awk`, because those two files are what the remap rewrites and so the only place a collision can be created or observed — `getent` would consult NSS sources the remap never touches. Ordering carries the guard's subtlety for free: neither lookup can name the remote user itself in the branch that consults it, because that branch only runs when the corresponding id differs. ONE CONSEQUENCE WAS FOLLOWED THROUGH RATHER THAN LEFT: deacon chowns the workspace to the resolved user, which the reference does not do at all (its script chowns `$HOME`, and only inside the remapping branch). With the remap refused, the remote user keeps an image-assigned uid unrelated to the host's, so chowning the BIND MOUNT to it would take the developer's own workspace away from them — the same failure mode the neighbouring root-uid skip already exists to prevent. The adjustment is skipped on a refused remap, and the Docker-gated test asserts the host workspace's owner after `up` to pin it. THE FIXTURE HAD TO BE HARDENED BEFORE THE CASE MEANT ANYTHING, and that is the durable lesson here. `case-up-upstream-remote-user-uid-collision` landed red-on-purpose in [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 8 on the vendored `fx-upstream-devport-userenvprobe`, whose upstream Dockerfile writes `adduser foo` then `adduser bar` and takes whatever uids the base hands out — 1000 and 1001. That made the collision a fact about the HOST rather than about deacon: it fired in a uid-1000 dev container and was a silent no-op on a GitHub-hosted runner, whose uid is 1001 and therefore equals `bar`'s own. Measured on PR [#623](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/pull/623)'s CI run, the case AGREED on every channel while the defect was unfixed — a red-on-purpose case that was green on the substrate that gates nothing and red only where a human happened to look. The fixture now pins `foo` to 1000, adds `foo2` at 1001 and moves `bar` to 1002, so the target uid is occupied on both plausible substrates; the case's passwd assertion moved 1001 → 1002 with it and asserts the same thing about the same user. The Docker-gated test goes further and takes the host out of the claim entirely: it reads `id -u` at run time and creates the decoy at exactly that uid, so the collision is constructed rather than hoped for. WATCHED TO FAIL against the pinned oracle 0.87.0, on the hardened fixture, at host uid 1000. The neutralization has to be a BINARY difference, so two `deacon` builds were run over the same workspace and compared with `devcontainer` 0.87.0's own answer to the case's `op-exec`. Reference: `bar` / `1`. deacon with `decide_uid_remap` collapsed to an unconditional remap: `foo` / `0` — the case's `chan-stdout` assertion, diverging in both fields. deacon with the guard: `bar` / `1`, byte-identical to the reference, with `id bar` reporting `uid=1002(bar) gid=1002(bar)` and the lifecycle markers owned by `bar`. With the guard in, the live `--profile parity` run reports this case `overall: agree` on all three of its channels, and the only diverging case left in the `docker-shared` group is #617's `case-up-upstream-mount-object-anonymous-volume` — a different, still-open defect. The GID half has no parity case — constructing one needs a base image where the host's gid is taken and its uid is not, which no vendored fixture provides — so it is pinned by unit tests over `decide_uid_remap` instead, including the corner where the gid fallback eats the only change there was to make and the remap becomes a no-op. |
| Local Features declared as `./name` from a config at `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` are INSTALLED into the image with their authored option values, and the programs they write are on the remote user's `PATH`. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The execution half of a fixture parsed since batch 1. `case-readconfig-upstream-local-feature-inside-devcontainer` proves the two directories RESOLVE; installation is different code — it copies the directory into the build context, runs `install.sh` with the options in the environment, and layers the result. The two commands are the proof AND the options: `color` prints `my favorite color is gold` only if `localFeatureB`'s `favorite` arrived (its default is `red`), and `hello` prints `Hello there, vscode!!!!` only if BOTH of `localFeatureA`'s options arrived (defaults `hey` and `?????`), so a Feature installed with defaults passes neither. `vscode` is a third claim riding along — `hello` runs `whoami` at exec time, so the name is the effective remote user of the process the caller gets. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: both CLIs exit 0 from both operations and print byte-identical output, ANSI colour codes included. `case-up-upstream-local-feature-inside-devcontainer-installs`, mined from `src/test/container-features/e2e.test.ts` at v0.87.0 ('should exec the color command' / 'should exec the helloworld command'), [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 8. |
| Local Features declared as `./.devcontainer/name` from a config at the WORKSPACE ROOT resolve against that config's own directory and install the same way. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The other half of the pair, and the half that can only pass for the right reason: an installer anchored at `<workspace>/.devcontainer` looks for `.devcontainer/.devcontainer/localFeatureA` and fails, while one anchored at the workspace root fails the sibling case instead. `case-readconfig-upstream-local-feature-config-at-workspace-root` pins the pairing at READ time; this pins it at INSTALL time, where the directory is actually copied into a build context. Same two commands and same three claims as the sibling — both Features installed, both authored option values delivered, and `whoami` inside `hello` reporting the effective remote user. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: both CLIs exit 0 and print byte-identical output. `case-up-upstream-local-feature-root-config-installs`, mined from `src/test/container-features/e2e.test.ts` at v0.87.0, [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 8. |
| `up --dotfiles-repository <r>` with no `--dotfiles-install-command` runs the repository's install script, from a clone at `~/dotfiles`. | matches the reference | 1 scenario + 3 hermetic tests | Fixes [#647](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/647), the first of six differences measured on one seam. deacon cloned the repository and ran NOTHING — not for some repositories, for all of them — and the reason is worth writing down because it is invisible by inspection: the install-script detection was its own `docker exec` whose `stdout` was read back, and a `silent: false` exec never captures stdout at all. That branch of `Docker::exec` forwards the child's stdout to deacon's own stderr and returns an `ExecResult` whose `stdout` is empty (`crates/core/src/docker.rs:1974-2008`), so `!result.stdout.trim().is_empty()` was false on every input and the code took the not-found arm. One measured run shows the whole mechanism: the string `install.sh` — the detector's own answer, forwarded — appears on deacon's stderr immediately above the log line `No install script found in dotfiles repository`. The probe worked; the return trip lost it. **The fix is a shape change, not a patch.** The phase now builds ONE shell script and runs it (`build_dotfiles_script`), which is what the reference does (`src/spec-common/dotfiles.ts`) and what makes the class of bug unreachable: a shell that decides for itself cannot lose the answer on the way back. The same rewrite carries the other four mechanical differences — the default target path (deacon cloned to `~/.dotfiles`; the reference's default, and deacon's own `--help`, say `~/dotfiles`), the eight-name search list in the reference's order where deacon looked at two, the shallow `--depth 1` clone, and the linking fallback and install-command resolution in the two rows below. **Measured at oracle 0.87.0** on a Debian devcontainer image with a bind-mounted local bare repository, so no arm of the measurement needed network. Backed by `case-up-dotfiles-default-install-script-runs` (spec-expectation, so it runs on the PR- and release-gating `parity_docker` lane); its assertion is the install script's own `pwd`, which pins the script running AND the target path in one file. Watched to fail: against the pre-fix binary the file is simply absent, while `up` still exits 0 — which is why the exit code cannot be the observation here. |
| A dotfiles repository with NO install script has its dotfiles symlinked into the home directory. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#647](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/647). This is the plain shape of most dotfiles repositories, not an edge case: no installer, just files that belong in `$HOME`. The reference lists the clone's dotfiles, drops `.`, `..` and `.git`, and `ln -sf`s the rest into `~`; deacon cloned and stopped, leaving the repository on disk with nothing using it. Backed by `case-up-dotfiles-no-install-script-links-into-home`, which reads the link back through `postStartCommand` — a phase that runs after dotfiles — and writes `NONE` when there is no link, so the pre-fix behavior fails on CONTENT rather than on an absent file and cannot pass by accident. One deliberate difference from the reference travels with the fix: its exclusion pattern `/(.|..|.git)$` leaves its dots unescaped, so it also drops any two-character entry such as `.z`; deacon escapes them. The divergence is unobservable for any realistic dotfile name, and importing a latent bug to match it is the worse trade. |
| `--dotfiles-install-command` names a FILE, resolved as `./<cmd>` then `<cmd>`, made executable if it is not. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#647](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/647). deacon spliced the flag's value into `sh -c "cd <target> && <cmd>"`, which makes it a PATH lookup rather than a path. Both of the arms the reference's own e2e suite asserts therefore failed under deacon with `sh: 1: <cmd>: not found`, exit 127: a bare filename (`install.sh`) and a script the repository ships non-executable. The reference tests `-f "./$cmd"`, then `-f "$cmd"`, `chmod +x`es a non-executable hit, execs it, and exits 126 when neither exists — measured, including that `--dotfiles-install-command 'echo hi'` prints `Could not locate 'echo hi'...` rather than running `echo`. That last point is a narrowing of what deacon used to accept, and it is the reference's contract: the value is a file name, never a shell fragment. Backed by `case-up-dotfiles-install-command-resolved-as-a-path`, whose fixture commits the script at mode 644 on purpose — a fix that resolved the path but skipped the `chmod` would still fail it with exit 126, so the case discriminates the two halves rather than only the first. |
| A dotfiles target directory that already exists is left alone, not removed and re-cloned. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#649](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/649), which is a data-loss bug rather than a parity nicety. deacon's dotfiles phase opened with `test -d <target>` and, on a hit, `rm -rf <target>` — a recursive removal of a path the user supplies through `--dotfiles-target-path`. A second `up` therefore deleted the dotfiles directory in the container's home and cloned it again; measured, an edit made inside the clone between two `up`s survives under the reference and is gone under pre-fix deacon. The reference's guard for the same condition is `[ -e ${targetPath} ] || git clone …`, and that is now deacon's. Backed by `case-up-dotfiles-existing-target-is-not-re-cloned`, which ships the pre-existing directory IN THE FIXTURE and runs ONE `up`: a two-`up` shape would have had to observe the removal through some file written between the runs, and every candidate for writing it is itself subject to the separately characterized difference that deacon re-runs a completed hook where the reference's container-side marker skips it (measured here on `postStart`). **Not adopted:** the reference's container-side `.dotfilesMarker`. deacon has its own host-side dotfiles marker and the right fix was to consult it, which is [#652](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/652) and the row below — this row's first draft filed the residual re-run under `wvr-ruc-completed-hooks-not-rerun` and that was wrong: for dotfiles specifically deacon disagreed with its own 008 SC-002, not only with the reference. The two guards are independent and both belong: this one keeps a `--dotfiles-target-path` that already exists from being deleted even on a FIRST `up`, where no marker exists to consult. |
| `--dotfiles-repository owner/repo` is expanded to `https://github.com/owner/repo.git`; a URL or a path is left alone. | matches the reference | 1 hermetic test | Fixes [#647](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/647). deacon handed the shorthand to `git clone` verbatim and got `fatal: repository 'codspace/test-dotfiles' does not exist` — while deacon's own `--help` had advertised the shorthand since before anything implemented it. The reference's rule is one line: a value containing `:` is already a remote, one matching `/^\.{0,2}\//` is a path, and everything else is `owner/repo`. **No scenario, and the reason is deliberate.** Demonstrating the expansion end to end means actually cloning from GitHub, which would make a Docker-lane case depend on an external repository staying alive; the four dotfiles scenarios are hermetic by construction, building their source repository inside the container. The expansion is pinned by `normalize_dotfiles_repository_expands_only_the_shorthand`, and the end-to-end behavior was measured once by hand at oracle 0.87.0 against `codspace/test-dotfiles` — the reference's own e2e fixture — where both CLIs now clone, install and leave `/tmp/.dotfilesMarker`. |
| A dotfiles failure — an unreachable repository, or an install script that exits non-zero — does not fail `up`. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#648](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/648), the seventh difference measured on the dotfiles seam and the only one that is a CONTRACT rather than a mechanism. deacon returned `Err` from the dotfiles phase and it propagated to the top of `up`: exit 1, `"outcome": "error"`, and **no `containerId` in the result** — even though the container was created, the image built and every lifecycle hook ran, so the caller lost the handle it needed to `exec` into or `down` the container that exists. A transient network failure while cloning a personal dotfiles repository left an orphan the user had no way to name. The reference wraps its whole dotfiles invocation in a `try`/`catch` that emits a `failed` progress event and does not re-throw (`src/spec-common/dotfiles.ts`); measured on `--dotfiles-repository /nope-not-a-repo`, both CLIs run the same `git clone`, both see the same exit 128, and the reference reports success. The contract is defensible on its own terms — dotfiles are a personal convenience layered on a container that is already correctly provisioned. **It only appears to cut against deacon's fail-fast principle.** The reference is not silent about the failure and neither is deacon: the git or script output is on stderr, a `warn!` names the exit code, the phase is recorded as failed, and — the part that matters for a later run — no completion marker is written, so the next `up` retries rather than assuming the dotfiles are installed. Spec-silent surface (`grep -rn -i dotfiles parity/spec/113500f4/` → nothing), so the reference is the authority. Backed by `case-up-dotfiles-failure-does-not-fail-up`, which asserts three channels rather than two on purpose: exit code and `outcome` alone would also be satisfied by a deacon that never attempted the clone or attempted it silently, which is exactly the failure mode a "do not fail on dotfiles errors" change invites, so `chan-stderr` pins that the attempt happened and that its diagnosis reached the user. |
| A resume `up` does not re-install dotfiles that already completed. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | Fixes [#652](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/652), found while fixing the rest of this seam and the only one of the eight where deacon disagreed with its OWN specification as well as with the reference. On a second `up` against the container the first one created, deacon skipped `onCreate`, `updateContent` and `postCreate` on their completion markers and then installed the dotfiles again. 008 SC-002 says otherwise in as many words — *"on resume after a completed initial `up`, 100% of runs skip onCreate, updateContent, postCreate, and dotfiles"* — two comments in the code repeat it, and `core::lifecycle::should_skip_phase` already implemented it, since `Dotfiles` is not a runtime hook and a prior `Executed` marker returns `Some("prior completion marker")`. **The decision was never asked for**: `commands/up/lifecycle.rs` ran `should_skip_phase` over an array of five phases that did not include `Dotfiles`, and built the dotfiles configuration from the CLI flags alone, so deacon wrote a `dotfiles.json` marker after every run and read it as a gate nowhere. A second, older witness to the same gap: `deacon_core::dotfiles::should_skip_dotfiles_phase` takes `has_prior_marker`, returns exactly that reason, and has no callers outside its own module — the behavior was written twice and wired zero times. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on a fixture whose hooks and install script each append a line to the bind-mounted workspace: after two `up`s the reference counts onCreate 1 / postCreate 1 / dotfiles 1 / postStart 1 and deacon counted 1 / 1 / 2 / 2. The `postStart` difference is deliberate and untouched — deacon runs runtime hooks whenever the container is brought up, where the reference's container-side marker suppresses them — which is why the case counts dotfiles installs rather than hooks. The fix keeps dotfiles on deacon's existing marker semantics rather than adopting the reference's container-side marker, so it inherits the trade its siblings already make: host-side markers survive `down` and are cleared by `--remove-existing-container` (#117), measured on a third `up`. Backed by `case-up-dotfiles-resume-does-not-reinstall`, whose observation is a LINE COUNT — both `up`s exit 0 either way and the clone is present either way, so only the number of install runs distinguishes the two behaviors. |
| `--secrets-file` values and the configuration's `remoteEnv` are both visible to the dotfiles install command. | matches the reference | 1 scenario | The last unmined arm of the reference's own `src/test/dotfiles.test.ts`, and the only one of the eight that found NO defect — deacon already did this. It is recorded rather than dropped because the two sides arrive at it by different routes and nothing pinned deacon's: the reference builds an `${allEnv}` prefix by hand and prepends it to both the `git clone` and the install command (`src/spec-common/dotfiles.ts`), while deacon passes the same values as `-e` flags on the `docker exec` that runs the phase. A change to how that phase is exec'd — exactly what [#647](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/647) rewrote — could drop the env without disturbing anything the other dotfiles scenarios observe. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: the file is byte-identical between the two CLIs. Backed by `case-up-dotfiles-secrets-reach-install-script`, whose install script writes both values on ONE line so a failure names which source was lost rather than only that something was. |
| A mount string is CSV, so a comma in a path is quoted — on the way out, and on the way in when an author writes Docker's own quoted form. | matches the reference | 1 scenario + 3 hermetic tests + 1 Docker-gated test | Fixes [#663](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/663), found mining `src/test/workspaceConfiguration.test.ts` ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 14). deacon quoted nowhere and its parser did not accept quotes, and both halves were reachable from an ordinary workspace. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0: on a workspace at `…/com,ma` the reference reports `type=bind,"source=…/com,ma","target=/workspaces/com,ma"` and `up` exits 0 with the mount landed; deacon reported the unquoted string and `up` exited 1 at `docker create` with `invalid field 'ma' must be a key=value pair` — it could not start a devcontainer in such a workspace at all. On an authored `mounts` entry in the quoted form the reference mounted it and deacon exited 1 with `bind mount requires a source`. The reference quotes the whole `key=value`, and only when the value contains a comma (`spec-node/utils.ts:412-417`); matching that exactly is what keeps every comma-free mount string byte-identical, which `a_comma_free_workspace_path_is_not_quoted` pins from the other side. Docker parses the argument with Go's `encoding/csv`, so the parser accepts `""` as a literal quote too. `case-up-mount-comma-quoted` carries the authored half on the gating Docker lane and reads the directory INSIDE the container, since a mount dropped on the way out would still report success; the workspace-path half is not expressible as a case — the workspace directory's name is the harness's to choose, not the case's — so it is `integration_up_worktree_mounts::up_starts_in_a_workspace_whose_path_contains_a_comma`. |
| `--mount-git-worktree-common-dir` mounts a relative-paths worktree's common `.git` directory, relocating the workspace mount to the ancestor they share so the worktree's `gitdir:` still resolves inside the container. | matches the reference | 3 hermetic tests + 2 Docker-gated tests | Closes [#664](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/664), a flag deacon did not have — eight of the fifteen `it`s in `src/test/workspaceConfiguration.test.ts` exercise it ([#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 14). Without it a devcontainer opened on a git worktree has a `.git` file pointing at a common dir that is not mounted, so every git command inside fails. Declared on `up`, `read-configuration` and `exec` — the three subcommands carrying its gate partner `--mount-workspace-git-root`; it is a no-op on `exec` for the same reason that one is. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 on four layouts, all now byte-identical: a sibling worktree (`gitdir: ../mainrepo/…`) keeps `/workspaces/feature` and mounts the common dir at `/workspaces/mainrepo/.git`; the same with the workspace in a subfolder keeps `/workspaces/feature/packages/app`; a worktree two levels down (`gitdir: ../../repos/main/…`) is mounted at `/workspaces/worktrees/feature`, NOT `/workspaces/<basename>` — the relocation is the whole mechanism, since only that arrangement lets the relative gitdir resolve; and an ABSOLUTE `gitdir` produces no additional mount and no relocation. Without the flag all four already agreed with the reference, so this is purely the missing opt-in. Not expressible as a parity case: a fixture cannot carry a `.git` file (git will not track a path with a `.git` component) and no operation can create one before the workspace mount is decided. The tests build the layout by hand rather than with `git worktree add --relative-paths`, which needs git 2.48 — deacon never shells out to git here, so nothing is lost. `up_leaves_a_worktree_alone_without_the_flag` is the control arm: relocating unconditionally would otherwise pass. |
| A symlinked `--workspace-folder` is reported, mounted and IDENTIFIED under the path the user named. | matches the reference | 2 hermetic tests + 2 Docker-gated tests | RULED and FIXED — [#665](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/665), closed by [#480](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/480) batch 15 with the maintainer ruling *follow the spec*, taking the issue's option (2): identity follows the report. deacon canonicalized the workspace folder, silently renaming a workspace reached through a symlink to its real path; the reference deliberately does not — `findGitRootFolder` runs `git rev-parse --show-cdup` rather than `--show-toplevel`, commented *"Preserves symlinked paths"* (`spec-common/git.ts:24`). MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 with `…/ws/link` → `…/ws/real`, EVERY workspace-derived value differed: `${localWorkspaceFolder}`, `${localWorkspaceFolderBasename}`, `${containerWorkspaceFolder}`, `workspace.workspaceFolder`, the mount's source and target, and `configFilePath`. The spec is not silent — `${localWorkspaceFolder}` is *"Path of the local folder that was opened"* (`devcontainerjson-reference.md:157`) — which is why this was a correction rather than a preference. The fix is one shared helper, `deacon_core::workspace::absolutize`: absolute and lexically normalized (`.`/`..` collapsed, matching the reference's `path.resolve`), never symlink-resolved. It replaces `canonicalize` at every site that REPORTS, MOUNTS or HASHES the workspace, including `ContainerIdentity::hash_workspace_path` and the `devcontainer.local_folder` label — so `up`/`exec`/`down` still agree on one spelling, and two spellings are two containers, which is what the reference does and what its own `exec` was measured to do. **`trust.rs::canonicalize_workspace` deliberately still resolves symlinks and must not be unified with it**: trust is a security boundary and has to key on the directory whose contents will actually run, so a repointed link cannot carry its trust across. Not expressible as a parity case — the harness's `copy_tree` cannot stage a symlinked directory into an isolated workspace — so the identity half is `integration_up_symlinked_workspace`, whose second test is the control arm that keeps re-canonicalizing from passing. |
| `${devcontainerId}` is the SPEC's own computation — a SHA-256 over the sorted id-labels as compact JSON, rendered base-32 and left-padded to 52 digits — over the reference's id-label SET: the caller's `--id-label` pairs when given, else `{devcontainer.local_folder, devcontainer.config_file}`. | matches the reference | 1 scenario, 5 hermetic tests | Fixes [#670](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/670) and [#672](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/672). **ADJUDICATED 2026-08-24: adopt the spec's computation, breaking change accepted** — the project is pre-1.0 and the maintainer ruled to align. The ruling was needed because the spec PERMITS deacon's old answer: it requires only that the id be unique on the host, stable across rebuilds and alphanumeric, and offers the computation as *"a way to do this"* implementations *"may choose to follow"* (`devcontainer-id-variable.md:19-23`). What settles it is what the spec says the computation is FOR — *"to ensure implementations get to the same result"* (`:47`) — and what the variable NAMES: the spec's own example is a `docker-in-docker` volume, `dind-var-lib-docker-${devcontainerId}`, so two CLIs that disagree give one workspace two volumes and silently split its state. The cost is real and is the breaking half: every existing `${devcontainerId}`-named volume is orphaned on the next `up`, with the data still on the host but no longer mounted. **The issue as filed understated the defect, and the second half is the sharper one.** deacon had TWO unrelated implementations of this one token (#672): `compute_dev_container_id` over the id-labels, and a `DefaultHasher` of the workspace-path STRING truncated to 12 hex characters, seeded by every `SubstitutionContext`. Because the config loader resolves the token at load time, the second is what users actually saw — in `containerEnv`, `remoteEnv`, `mounts`, `name` and every lifecycle hook — while the first reached only mounts declared by a FEATURE, which is the spec's own example use. Measured at the parent commit: `--id-label foo=bar` and no flag produced the IDENTICAL id (`01cf320a8068`), which is impossible for a function that hashes the labels, and is how the load-time implementation was identified as the one that wins. `DefaultHasher` also carries no cross-release stability guarantee from Rust, so the user-visible id was not guaranteed stable across a rebuild of DEACON — the one thing the spec REQUIRES of it. **One id, computed once, from the id-labels** — the reference's own structure, where `findContainerAndIdLabels` runs before `beforeContainerSubstitute` (`spec-node/configContainer.ts:57-58`). The labels now travel WITH the config load (`ConfigLoadArgs::id_labels`), because that is the pass that resolves the token; threading them there is what lets `--id-label` reach it at all, and all seven load sites name their answer explicitly rather than inheriting one. `id_hash_labels` was narrowed to the reference's pair: `devcontainer.source`, `workspaceHash`, `configHash` and `devcontainer.name` are deacon-only bookkeeping that no reference-created container carries, so keeping them would have produced a THIRD value agreeing with neither CLI even with the right algorithm — and `configHash` moves whenever the configuration is edited, which is precisely the churn the stability MUST forbids. MEASURED at oracle 0.87.0 end to end, on both label shapes and both surfaces: with no flag both CLIs write `157p2d57rcuei8mjomedb8jm89v2nemrk78ve69rrdvu4g892ouf` into `containerEnv` AND into a `${devcontainerId}`-named volume; with `--id-label foo=bar` both write `0uhonu0v70vmigpqqrkg1kqr7ohoam9veqjrfaqt8darhei1toib`. Two unit vectors pin the algorithm against the REFERENCE's output rather than against deacon's own, which `--id-label` makes possible by removing every host path from the input. **The scenario is observed through a FILE, and that is forced rather than stylistic**: the normalizer's `devcontainer_id_token` rewrites any id-shaped run to `<ID>` inside `configuration`/`mergedConfiguration`, so no assertion on the result document can pin this variable's VALUE — the trap `case-set-up-substitution-scope` recorded when the id was still 12 hex characters. A `postCreateCommand` writing into a bind-mounted file puts it on a channel that compares raw bytes. Watched to fail on BOTH arms independently: with the algorithm reverted, and with `--id-label` no longer reaching the load pass, `parity_docker` reports exactly one diverging case each time — `case-up-devcontainer-id-spec-value` on `chan-file-content` — out of the whole lane. **Two coverage gaps are deliberate and both are worth reading, because each is a harness limit rather than a choice.** The DEFAULT label set cannot be a differential: its input contains the workspace path, which the runner makes per-side, so the two sides compute genuinely different ids and no token can bridge a digest OF the thing the token would rewrite — that half is carried by unit tests, including one that edits the configuration and requires the id not to move. And there is no differential twin at all: a successful Docker `up` must leave a container discoverable by `devcontainer.local_folder=<workspace>` (`require_observed_container`, the guard that stops a case observing nothing from passing vacuously), and `--id-label` REPLACES that label — so the flag that makes the expectation machine-independent also makes the container undiscoverable. A twin was written, run, and removed after failing on exactly that guard. The cost is that the pinned digest has no automatic upstream-drift detector; it is re-measured by hand at each oracle bump, and #670 records the derivation in full. One normalizer change travelled with it: the id token rule now matches 52 base-32 digits instead of 12 hex, and its collapsing risk went DOWN rather than up — the rule no longer hides a DIFFERENCE at all, since the two CLIs now agree byte for byte, and what it still buys is assertability for cases that cannot hardcode a machine-derived id. |
| A disallowed-Features entry matches by PREFIX terminated at a Feature-id separator — `/`, `:` or `@` — so `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/node` covers `…/node:1`, `…/node/js` and `…/node@abc`, and covers neither `…/nodejs` nor `…/node.js`. | deacon extension | 1 scenario + 2 hermetic tests | Fixes one arm of [#675](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/675). deacon matched by exact string, and since a configuration almost always names a version, the entry an operator would actually write blocked nothing at all — a security-shaped knob that failed OPEN while reading as if it were on. Measured: with `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/node` on the list, deacon proceeded to build `…/node:1`. The rule and its seven vectors are the reference's own `findDisallowedFeatureEntry` (`src/spec-node/disallowedFeatures.ts`, pinned by `src/test/disallowedFeatures.test.ts`); an empty entry is dropped rather than treated as a prefix of everything, because deacon's list can acquire one from a stray comma and blocking every Feature is never what that meant. The scenario became writable when the harness gained an `env` channel on `Operation`: before that, a knob with no backing flag had no ingress from a case at all. Each of these scenarios was verified to DEPEND on that channel by disabling `substitute_env` and confirming the case diverges — which is how the `build` one was caught asserting `outcome: error` alone and passing for the wrong reason, since `build` fails in a hermetic lane anyway. |
| The disallowed-Features gate sees the Features a run would INSTALL — the configuration's set unioned with `--additional-features`, minus an overlay `--ignore-additional-features` drops. | deacon extension | 1 scenario + 2 hermetic tests | Fixes one arm of [#675](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/675). The gate read the configuration alone, so moving a Feature to the command line walked straight past it — measured on both sides: the reference refused an `--additional-features` overlay naming a disallowed Feature before touching the daemon, deacon built it. deacon gates the MERGED set rather than the reference's raw union (`{…config.features, …additionalFeatures}`), which is the same set on every input the reference can express and additionally takes a dropped overlay back out of scope — deacon has `--ignore-additional-features` and the reference does not. Still upstream of every registry, daemon and host-hook operation, which is where the reference refuses from. The scenario became writable when the harness gained an `env` channel on `Operation`: before that, a knob with no backing flag had no ingress from a case at all. Each of these scenarios was verified to DEPEND on that channel by disabling `substitute_env` and confirming the case diverges — which is how the `build` one was caught asserting `outcome: error` alone and passing for the wrong reason, since `build` fails in a hermetic lane anyway. |
| `up`'s error JSON names a policy-blocked Feature in `disallowedFeatureId`, alongside the prose. | deacon extension | 3 scenarios + 1 hermetic test | Fixes one arm of [#675](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/675). `UpError` modelled the field from the start and no production path ever filled it; the builder that sets it carried `#[allow(dead_code)]` and a TODO naming a task that had shipped. The prose was the only machine-readable answer, so a caller had to scrape it. deacon now raises a dedicated `ConfigError::DisallowedFeature` carrying both the blocked id and the ENTRY that matched — the two are usually different, since an entry matches by prefix — and `up` reports the id structurally while the entry stays in the sentence. `build` does not emit the field, which is also what the reference does (measured at oracle 0.87.0: `disallowedFeatureId` on `up`, absent on `build`). |
| `--control-manifest` (`DEACON_CONTROL_MANIFEST`) points `up` and `build` at a control manifest in the reference CLI's format — a URL or a local file — whose `disallowedFeatures` entries refuse a run by the same prefix rule as the environment list, reporting the matched entry and its `documentationURL` (as `learnMoreUrl`). | deacon extension | 1 scenario + 6 hermetic tests | Implements the maintainer's ruling on [#676](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/676). The reference hard-codes `https://containers.dev/static/devcontainer-control-manifest.json` and consults it on every `up`/`build` (`configContainer.ts:64`, `devContainersSpecCLI.ts:680`); deacon implements the MECHANISM and no default, so it fetches and reads nothing unless an operator names a source. Two reasons, both traced rather than assumed: the capability was proposed as [devcontainers/spec#226](https://github.com/devcontainers/spec/issues/226) in April 2023 and CLOSED BY ITS OWN AUTHOR in June 2023 ("Out of scope for the spec for now") before shipping in the CLI anyway — so the standing rule that the reference is the authority on a spec-silent surface does not apply, the spec having explicitly declined rather than stayed silent; and the list itself is a single mutable `latest` tag on `ghcr.io/devcontainers/control-manifest`, `oras pull`ed by a daily cron into the containers.dev site build, with no public source, no pull request, no review and no retained history (`_data/` holds two unrelated YAMLs, `static/` holds a `.keep`). All six entries live on it today are test fixtures and two of their documentation URLs 404. Verified live at oracle 0.87.0: deacon pointed at that URL and the reference on the same workspace produce identical `outcome`, `disallowedFeatureId` and `learnMoreUrl`, both exit 1 — so opting in reproduces the reference exactly. The prefix rule is shared with the environment list via `control_manifest::feature_id_covered_by` so the two can never drift. |
| A control-manifest source that cannot be fetched, read or parsed FAILS the run; deacon never falls back to an empty manifest. A stale cached copy is preferred to failing, with a warning; a URL response is cached for 5 minutes. | deacon follows the spec, the CLI deviates | 1 scenario + 6 hermetic tests | The reference's `updateControlManifest` returns `emptyControlManifest` when a fetch fails and it has no cached copy, which silently disables the very gate the operator asked for — a network blip turns the policy off and nothing says so. Since deacon's gate exists ONLY because someone named a source, silently not having it is the one outcome nobody wants, and constitution principle IV (no silent fallbacks — fail fast) says so independently. deacon therefore refuses with a diagnostic naming the source, and uses a stale cached copy in preference to failing so a transient outage degrades rather than blocks. The manifest is also loaded even when the configuration declares NO Features, so a typo'd path is reported at once instead of lying dormant until someone adds one. Entry-level leniency is unchanged from the reference — a malformed ENTRY is dropped and the rest survive (`sanitizeControlManifest`), because this is somebody else's document and one future entry shape must not disable the entries deacon understands; that is deliberately not the strictness deacon applies to a user's own `devcontainer.json`. |
| Feature advisories from a control manifest warn — never block — when a Feature's RESOLVED version falls in the half-open range `[introducedInVersion, fixedInVersion)`, using the reference's own component-wise version arithmetic. | deacon extension | no scenario — the operation vocabulary cannot carry a per-case manifest source; 4 hermetic unit tests + a live measurement | Second half of the ruling on [#676](https://github.com/get2knowio/deacon/issues/676). Emitted between Feature resolution and the image build, which is where the reference emits it (`logFeatureAdvisories` inside `generateFeaturesConfig`) and the last moment a warning can still precede an install. Driven off the LOCKFILE, because that is what carries the resolved version — an authored `git:1.3.2` is what the user wrote but `node:1` is equally legal and resolves to something like `1.6.3`, and an advisory range is written against real versions. Matching the authored id instead would parse `1` as `[1]`, fall outside `[1.0.7, 1.1.10)` and silently miss every advisory while looking like it worked. The arithmetic is the reference's `parseVersion`/`isEarlierVersion` (`spec-common/commonUtils.ts`) and is deliberately NOT semver: it takes the leading dotted-integer run and ignores the rest, accepts any number of components and has no prerelease ordering, so `1.2` and `1.2.0` are the same version — reusing `deacon_core::semver_utils::parse_version` would quietly change which versions an advisory covers. An unparseable version on either side excludes the advisory rather than including it: a warning nobody can act on is worse than silence. The lookup is best-effort and never fails a build, because the refusal surface already loaded the same source at the command layer and failed the run there if it could not. Measured on `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/git:1.3.2` against a synthetic `[1.3.0, 1.4.0)` advisory: the banner is emitted and `build` still exits 0; a `[2.0.0, 3.0.0)` advisory and an absent manifest both stay silent. |
